No project picked: the project floors are whole and can be clicked to pick one. A project picked: its floor whole with a soft amber glow, everything else at 7% and not clickable; the project list stays beside it on a wide screen to move between floors, and on a phone it slides away and **All floors** brings it back. (4) Motion: from the map, the map scales toward the clicked tower with an ease-in (slow, then fast) and the building scales up and settles with an ease-out; picking a project slides, zooms and fades in one 0.75 s ease-in-out; leaving, the map eases back from close up. Reduced motion has none of it. (5) An agent's card opens where the project list is on a wide screen, so it never covers the floor. (6) People are drawn larger than the furniture's true scale so they can be recognised and tapped.

172. **A floor seats eight and belongs to one project; scroll and the arrow keys go from floor to floor (2026-11-10).** The owner, looking at a floor of Research: scroll or Up/Down should move between floors so every floor with workers can be checked; a floor holds at most 8 workers, filled by headcount; a floor is exactly one project's, and a project whose floor is full continues on a new floor of the same project, labelled 1a, then 1b, 1c; a new project always gets a new floor, even when an existing floor has free seats; and outside the building the choices are projects, not floors, with a pick going to the project's top floor. Decided: (1) `deptProjects` splits each project's people (manager first) into floors of 8, lowest first; floors are numbered up the tower from 2 with no gaps, so a later project sits above all of an earlier project's floors. (2) A project with one floor is named by its title; with more, each floor is `<title> · 1a`, `· 1b`… (the owner's `project-<name>-1a`, written the way the HUD writes names). (3) The room is the same eight desks on every screen (4 by 2 wide, 2 by 4 on a phone), replacing the 12-desk room of decision 171. (4) The address may name the floor (`#/city/visit/<dept>/<project>/<a|b|…>`); without it, the top floor. Going from floor to floor replaces the address, so Back still leaves the floors in one step. (5) Up/Down, the wheel over the building (one floor per gesture, at most one every 0.45 s) and two ▲ ▼ buttons in the header (for touch) move to the next floor that has people on it, across projects; with no floor in focus, Up starts at the lowest and Down at the highest. The arrow keys are left alone while typing or while an agent's card is open.

173. **The tab says VAIC and its icon is only the owner's eye (2026-11-10).** After decision 169 the owner's Safari tab still showed the old amber tile icon and "AI City": Safari keeps a site's icon in its own cache by address, and the page title was still "AI City". The tab icon now lives at new addresses (`/vaic-icon.svg`, the same theme-following eye; the old `/favicon.svg` is deleted), with two 64 px PNG copies of the eye, dark ink for a light browser and near-white for a dark one (`/vaic-icon-dark.png`, `/vaic-icon-light.png`, `media="(prefers-color-scheme: …)"`), for browsers that skip SVG icons. All are transparent, with no background. The page title is "VAIC". The sign-in card and other in-app text still say "AI City" where it names the place; only the tab changes here.

174. **The City no longer blinks black while zooming (2026-11-10).** The owner saw the map flash black every time they zoomed in or out in Safari. The canvas held one pixel per art pixel at every zoom, so zoomed out (down to 0.35×) it became several times the screen, about 5,000 px wide on a wide display; Safari refuses to draw a canvas that large and shows it black, and the canvas was resized on every frame of the zoom. Now, from one device pixel per art pixel up, nothing changes (art pixels, scaled up crisp by CSS); further out, the canvas is the screen's own size in device pixels and the drawing is scaled down into it (`ctx.setTransform`, smoothed). The resize happens only in `draw()`, right before the frame is painted, never in a wheel or pinch handler, and the canvas's own background is the map's ground colour rather than the page's dark. A Playwright check holds the canvas to the screen's pixels when far out and samples it while zooming for unpainted pixels. The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it.

175. **A connected tool hangs its pixel sign by the doors of the departments that use it (2026-11-10).** The owner: when a connector is added, the departments that will use it show it on the front of their buildings with the tool's logo, made into pixel art in the City's style; a tool every team uses (Jira) goes on every building, YouTube only on Media (maybe Research and Marketing); a hardcoded prompt built into the system decides, on every successful connection. Built so: when a connector turns `ready` (an MCP server, a package server or a web API, on its first connection and on every refresh), the connectors Lambda runs the hardcoded prompt `agents/utility/connector-departments.md` as one gated, costed utility turn on Claude Haiku 5.5 with the tool's name, catalog description and tools and the standing departments with their missions; the answer (`every_department`, `departments`) is kept only where it names real departments and stored on the connector record. The same step makes the sign once and caches it on the record (`sign`): the service's icon (`/apple-touch-icon.png`, then `/favicon.ico`, on the brand's root domain first, from the connector's link, a web API's base URL and the catalog's sign-up page) is fetched as untrusted bytes (public HTTPS only with the connector link checks, at most three redirects each checked again, 200 KB, raster formats only and never SVG, 512 px at most), its plain background dropped, cropped to the mark, scaled to 12×12 and every pixel snapped to the City's own palette; no icon means no `sign`, and the City letters the plaque with the tool's initial instead. A failure in either part is logged and never stops the connection. The record's departments and sign ride on `connector.updated` and on the `/city` snapshot (`connectors`, ready ones only), so the web HUD and the iPhone City get them the same way. In the City each tower carries a row of 14-pixel tenant plaques at door height, left and right of its doors on the lobby wall (the lobby glass is walled over where they hang): tools with a logo first, then by name; a narrow tower takes two, a wide one six, and when there are more the last place says "+N". A refresh keeps the sign up; a removed tool takes it down everywhere. Infra: the connectors Lambda gets `bedrock:InvokeModel` on the utility profile only, the utility turn's own ledger keys (`CONFIG`, `SPEND#*`, `TURN#*`, `IDEMP#*`, `AGENT#utility`) and a read of the ledger's `gsi1` for the department list. Not done: mail and calendar accounts (they are VAIC's and the managers', not a department's tool) get no sign; the catalog's own logo files are not used, the favicon is (it is drawn for 16 pixels, the closest to a 12-pixel sign).

176. **Traffic lights, roundabouts and highway pace in the City (2026-11-10).** The owner asked for traffic lights that every cab except their own car obeys, about 5 seconds of red, almost fully realistic, at some streets only; a couple of roundabouts, not too many; and faster driving outside the town, like a highway. The cruising cabs were placed by the clock alone (`trafficAt`), which cannot queue or wait, so they are now a small simulation stepped by the scene's clock (`createTraffic`, `stepTraffic`, `trafficCars` in `web/src/city/city-state.js`, steps of at most 50 ms, a long pause cut to one second). Decided: (1) Cars run on the streets, the avenue and now also the road west of HQ (it runs the whole map); the short side streets between towers stay empty. A junction is any crossing of a street with one of those roads; a T where the phone's avenue starts is not one. (2) The junctions nearest HQ get lights (about half of the rest after roundabouts), the outermost two (three from seven junctions up, one when there are only two or three) are roundabouts, and the others are plain crossings where the street gives way to the avenue. (3) A light runs green 3.6 s, amber 0.9 s, then 0.25 s red both ways before the crossing road goes green, so red lasts exactly 5 s and both ways are never green or amber together (`signalAt`, each light offset by its id). (4) Every cab follows the one in front with a braking curve (never closer than 6 px), stops at the stop line on red, and on amber stops when it can do so gently and drives on when it is too close; it never enters a junction while a crossing car is in it or while the road beyond is jammed. The owner's sports car (the rare cab) runs the lights, but never into a car in the junction. (5) At a give-way crossing the street cab slows to a crawl and goes when no avenue cab is within about two seconds. (6) A roundabout is a ring of road round a planted island, anticlockwise (traffic keeps right); a cab gives way to cars on the ring, at most three on it, goes straight on half the time and otherwise turns left or right (never back), so cabs now change roads there. (7) In town cabs drive at the old pace (34 px/s, each cab ±12 %); out in the countryside at 96 px/s, easing off before the town's edge. (8) Brake lights show while a cab slows or waits. (9) Reduced motion: the traffic and the lights stand still, as the cabs did before. Commuters' own cabs (an agent riding to work) still follow the commute's timing and do not stop at lights; that would delay commutes and is left for the owner to ask for. Tests: vitest `traffic.test.ts` (the cycle and five seconds of red, a cab waiting at the line through red and leaving on green, amber, the rare car running a red, five minutes of traffic on four maps with no overlaps, no crossing traffic in a junction together, no gridlock, town and highway pace) and Playwright `traffic.spec.ts` in the page (lights never green both ways, a cab standing at a red line, a cab on a roundabout, the two paces). The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it.

177. **Commuters' cabs keep to the traffic rules too (2026-11-10).** Decision 176 left the cabs that carry an agent to or from work outside the traffic; the owner: "Yes, all cabs stop". Now only the owner's car is exempt. Decided: (1) A commute's cab legs drive the route's centre line at cab pace (56 px/s, `RIDE_PX_S`, a little quicker than the cruising cabs' 34 so a ride still feels purposeful), pulling away and braking smoothly; the walks at either end keep the old brisk pace (about 2.5 s). (2) Each frame the traffic simulation says how far the cab may go (`rideStep`, `rideHold`): it stops at the stop line on red, on amber when it can stop gently, at a give-way line while avenue traffic is near (on the street), and at a junction while a crossing car is in it; the owner's car runs the lights but not into anyone. (3) A ride through a roundabout goes round the ring anticlockwise instead of over the island (`roundaboutDetours` turns the straight run or turn at its centre into an arc), giving way to cars on the ring and keeping behind whoever is ahead on it. (4) The simulation sees each riding cab as a guest: cruising cabs do not enter a junction it is crossing, wait for it at a give-way line, and give way to it on a roundabout. (5) So a commute takes longer: typically 10 to 30 s instead of 2.5 s, depending on the distance and the lights; nothing else waits on it. Tests: vitest `commute-traffic.test.ts` (the ring detour's shape and direction, a riding cab stopping at a red line and leaving on green, the owner's car going through, every tower's commute from two houses finishing in the traffic and never entering a junction on red or across a crossing cab) and Playwright `traffic.spec.ts` (a hired worker's cab is one of the traffic's guests and never faster than cab pace); the commute and tower tests wait up to a minute for a ride. The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it.

178. **Work is handed off, never waited on: no more jobs open with nobody working (2026-11-10).** The owner asked VAIC twice to make an app and saw nothing happen: no letters from the Orchestrator to the managers, no hiring, no workers walking in, no lit windows, while the City counted two jobs running and no agents working. The dispatcher Lambda (SQS → AgentCore) handed Workers off as async jobs but called the Orchestrator and Department Managers synchronously and waited for their whole turn. Those planning turns on Fable 5.2 may take minutes (the turn limit is 15), while the dispatcher may run only 120 s. When a wait was cut off, SQS made the message, and every other message in its batch of up to five, invisible again for the queue's visibility timeout of 16 minutes; and within a batch the messages ran one after another, so each task also waited for the others' planning turns. Now every role except VAIC is invoked as an AgentCore async job (the runtime accepts at once, reports HealthyBusy while the turn runs and runs it in the background, exactly as workers already did), and a batch's messages start side by side. VAIC stays synchronous because its reply streams to the HUD from the call and is quick. Leases, the crash sweep and the worker slots are unchanged; a turn that dies is still sent again by the sweep. The letters, hiring, walks to work and lit windows are all drawn from the events these turns write, so they appear once the turns run. Not measured on prod: there are no AWS credentials on the owner's Mac for VAIC, and the HUD needs the owner's sign-in.

179. **Settings → Connectors: a catalog card never spills, and a card with a form open takes the whole row (2026-11-10).** The owner's screenshot (desktop Safari, three cards a row): on Jira & Confluence, "Add its key" showed the key form inside the card, and the form's box, Key field and Save button ran past the card's right edge and were clipped, while the card grew so tall that Stripe and Slack beside it showed large empty gaps; "Create a Stripe account" also wrapped awkwardly beside Connect. "Fix this bug in the UI and make it completely robust." Causes: the connect card inside a catalog card sized its single grid column to its content (a text input's default width in Safari is wider than a narrow card), nothing in the card was allowed to shrink below its content, a long error or link had no place to break, and the screen's reading measure for list items (`.screen li`) capped any wider card. Decided: (1) Every catalog card is a grid child that never grows past its column (`min-width: 0`) and breaks long words anywhere; its connect card has one `minmax(0, 1fr)` column, is at most the card's width, and its fields, selects and buttons are the card's width (buttons wrap their label instead of overflowing). This applies to the connect cards under VAIC's chat too. (2) A card with a sign-in or key form open (`needs_login`, `login_expired`, `needs_key`) takes the whole row (`hub-card-form`), with the form at most 34 rem wide, rather than opening in a sheet: the form stays where the owner tapped and needs no extra close step; the grid fills densely, so the next cards move up into the row above instead of leaving a hole, and no neighbour is stretched. Once the key is saved or the sign-in done, the card goes back to one column. (3) The card's foot wraps: "Create a … account" sits beside Connect when it fits on one line and above it otherwise; a long status (an error, shown in full by the owner's rule) wraps inside the card. Tests: Playwright `connector-cards.spec.ts` (desktop, at 1440, 1280, 1100, 1000, 900 and 768 px: the Key field and Save button inside the Jira card, every element of every catalog card inside its card, no element and no page scrolling sideways, the form card as wide as the grid; saving a key turns the card back into one column; the access-token switch fits) and `phone.spec.ts` (the same checks at 375 and 412 px). Not tested: Safari or WebKit (only Chromium runs here), so the exact Safari overflow was not reproduced, only the same layout rules checked.

180. **Half as many cabs (2026-11-10).** The owner, to VAIC: "can you cut the amount of taxis in half". The cruising cabs are now one per 44 tiles of road each way (`CAB_GAP_PX`, was 22), so every lane has about half as many (at least one each). Nothing else changes: the lights, roundabouts, give-way, highway pace and the commuters' own cabs (one per commute) work as in decisions 176 and 177. The owner's car stays at one arrival in a thousand (`RARE_CAB_ODDS`), so with half the arrivals it now turns up about half as often; the odds were not changed because the owner asked only for fewer taxis. Tests: vitest checks the spacing and that a town has 40 to 60 % of the cabs it had; every traffic and commute test passes with the thinner traffic. The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it.

181. **A model AWS refuses falls back to Opus 5.5 instead of stopping the City (2026-11-10).** Why nobody went to work since 9 November: on that evening the Orchestrator, the Department Managers, the Chief of Staff and the executives moved to Claude Fable 5.2 (commit e3b01e3), but the account's AWS Marketplace subscription for that model was never completed. A new Anthropic model is subscribed on its first call by a principal allowed `aws-marketplace:Subscribe`, and the agents' roles rightly are not. Every Fable 5.2 call was refused ("Model access is denied … required AWS Marketplace actions", CloudTrail AccessDenied on the `aic-orchestrator` application inference profile), and Claude Code reported it inside the runtime only as "AWS authentication failed", so each Orchestrator turn ended at its first step: no plan, no letters, no hiring, no lit windows. Found from the runtime's CloudWatch log (`turn.failed`) and CloudTrail, read from CloudShell in the owner's console session. Two fixes: the owner turns Fable 5.2 on once in the Bedrock console (playground), which completes the subscription for the account; and the turn gate now treats "model access is denied", AWS Marketplace and Claude Code's "AWS authentication failed" as an unavailable model, so the turn runs once on the role's fallback (Opus 5.5) and the fallback is marked in the ledger, instead of dying. The agents' roles still get no Marketplace permission.

182. **Back to Fable 5.1 (2026-11-10).** The owner tried to turn Fable 5.2 on in the Bedrock playground and was refused too ("not authorized to perform the required AWS Marketplace actions"): their console role, or the organisation's policy for this Basefarm lab account, does not allow Marketplace subscriptions, so the account cannot use Fable 5.2 (decision 181). The owner chose: "well lets just use fable 5.1 then". Fable 5.1 is already subscribed (it ran every Fable role until decision 154). Every role, department and default that was Fable 5.2 is Fable 5.1 again: the Orchestrator, every Department Manager, the Chief of Staff, the four executives, and the Media, Research and Legal workers (`config/models.json`, the 5.1 prices restored), and the HUD, the iPhone app and the docs name it. Ledger records saved with `fable-5-2` resolve to `fable-5-1` (`LEGACY_MODELS`, reversed). The application inference profiles copy the Fable 5.1 global profile again on deploy. Decision 181's fallback stays: a model AWS refuses falls back to Opus 5.5 for the turn.

183. **Stop drops VAIC's answer, like ChatGPT and Claude (2026-11-10).** The owner: "can you add a cancel button when a user wants to cancel a messge they have sent in the chat and just make you drop it just like chatgpt has it or like you have it here in claude". Decided: (1) While VAIC is working on the owner's latest message (its turn has begun and no finished reply follows), the composer's Send button is a Stop button with a filled square, in the web HUD's Chat screen and the iPhone app; Esc does the same (on the iPhone with a hardware keyboard), unless a menu or dialog is open, which Esc closes first. Typing and Enter still work while VAIC answers, as before. (2) Stop acts at once on the device: VAIC's half-written answer to that message is removed, the owner's message stays with "Stopped." and **Edit and send again** (the message leaves the chat and its words go back in the box), the turn ends, VAIC's speech stops, and anything still arriving for that message is dropped. To tell a late piece of a stopped answer from a reply to the next message, every `vaic.reply` now carries `message_id` (the owner's `owner-<client id>`), as `vaic.files` already did. (3) The HUD calls `POST /messages/{client_id}/stop`, which writes `STOP#owner-<client id>` in the ledger (kept a day). The API Lambda and VAIC's runtime are separate processes, and an AgentCore invocation cannot be interrupted from outside, so VAIC's runtime polls that flag (one DynamoDB read every 0.4 s, only while a VAIC turn runs; `runtime/stop.py`) and checks it before the turn starts. When set, the turn is cancelled: the ConverseStream reader stops and closes the stream, no later text or final reply is sent, no further tool runs, the turn's cost is not recorded (the AWS bill still has it), and the work item is finished so the crash sweep never sends it again. A message stopped before VAIC picked it up never runs. (4) A tool call already under way when Stop lands is shielded and finishes whole rather than being cut in half; VAIC then says `vaic.stopped` (to every open HUD, straight and through the bus) with the tool calls it had started, which the chat shows plainly ("VAIC had already done: Passing this to my team"). Stop never undoes work: a job already handed to the Orchestrator keeps going, and the note says to stop it in Jobs (`POST /tasks/{id}/cancel`); that is shown only when VAIC had handed work on. (5) A stopped exchange stays in VAIC's conversation history for context, as ChatGPT and Claude keep a stopped turn (the owner, to VAIC: "but still save them as chat history for context in future prompts"): the owner's message, then VAIC's line beginning "(stopped by the owner" ("before VAIC answered" when nothing had streamed; the tools already called, if any) and "Not an open request: do not carry it on unless the owner asks again.)", followed by whatever part of the answer had already streamed. The marker comes first so the 4,000-character cap on a history line can only cut the partial answer. A message stopped before VAIC picked it up is kept the same way. "Edit and send again" removes the line from the screen only; the history keeps it. (6) The voice turn of the Nova Sonic path (off by default) is not wired to Stop; spoken messages on the standard voice path are ordinary chat messages and stop the same way. Project chats in the Apps studio have their own composer and are not changed. Tests: pytest (stopping mid-stream drops the rest and keeps the stopped exchange in the history, the next turn's prompt reads it as stopped, a reply after Stop is never sent, a message stopped before pickup never runs, tools already called are named and remembered, a shielded tool finishes, the route), vitest (the store and outbox), Playwright desktop and phone (the button, Esc, Edit, late pieces ignored, accessibility at 412 px), XCTest (the iPhone store and route). Not tested: against real Bedrock and AgentCore, or on a real iPhone.

184. **Activity folds away (2026-11-10).** The owner asked to hide the City's Activity box when they don't want to see it. Its title is now a button with a ▾/▸ arrow (`aria-expanded`): folded, only the title bar stays, the log's live announcements stop, and on a phone it no longer stretches to the box beside it. The choice is remembered on this device (`localStorage` key `vaic.activity`; a private window just forgets it). Nothing else in the City moves.

185. **A website link becomes a brand and site profile, kept apart per brand (2026-11-10).** The owner: "add a feature by default that allows the user to add a link and then make the AI agent use whatever it find in the website even the html and css … so it can store it in the knowledge base for what brand color … as well as font style (everything with the design and content of the website …)", and then: index it into vectors as RAG and into the graph, and if there are several brands for different projects, keep them separate in both. Decided (`docs/KNOWLEDGE.md` §16): (1) It hooks into the existing link path (`fetch_link`), so it is on for every https web page, from Knowledge or VAIC's `add_to_knowledge`, with no new route, infra or IAM. (2) Colours, fonts, logo, radius, shadows, spacing and light or dark are extracted **deterministically** from the HTML and same-site CSS. Haiku 5.5 only names roles, tone and content from those facts, and a palette colour it names must be one the site's code uses, so a page cannot talk the profile into a colour. When the model fails the facts alone make the profile. (3) Extra fetches (stylesheets, logo, up to 3 pages) are https only and on the page's own site, through the existing guard on every hop. They have tighter caps (1.5 MB per stylesheet, 4 MB in all, 48 KB logo, 2 MB per page, 10 s each, 60 s in all), and Google Fonts are read from the link, not fetched. Scripts are never kept or run; JSON-LD is read as data. (4) **Multi-tenant per brand:** the tenant key is the site's registrable domain (`brand`). It is a metadata attribute on both vector documents (`page.md`, and `site-profile.md` with the profile and the main pages), a `brand` filter on `search_knowledge` (Bedrock filter plus a post-filter on the record), and part of every colour and font node's name in the graph, so two brands never share a node. The profile is ingested as a markdown document rather than a PDF: Bedrock chunks and embeds both the same way, and a PDF renderer would add a dependency for nothing. (5) Re-adding a link makes a new item that inherits the old scope; once it is ready the older copies are deleted, so a refresh never leaves the brand without a profile. (6) The logo is kept as a ≤ 48 KB `data:` image on the record and shown only in an `<img>`: the HUD's CSP stays as it is and opening Knowledge sends nothing to the site. (7) Agents are told in the Media, Marketing, Product, Application and Apps playbooks, `worker.md`, the `knowledge-first` skill and the tool description to search the brand profile with `brand` before making anything for a company and never to mix brands. Tests: pytest (sample company, store, paper and docs sites: colours, roles, fonts, Google Fonts, `@font-face`, `@import`, logo, light/dark, store prices, paper authors and abstract, docs sections; private, metadata, http and off-site fetches refused, including through redirects, and oversized ones skipped; script, comment and hidden-text injection never reaching the facts; model output trimmed to the site's own colours and known fields; the pipeline end to end with the vector `brand` metadata, graph nodes, refresh with the kept scope and the brand filter), vitest (profile view, fallback swatches, no markup, remote image or style from site data, the old copy dropped) and Playwright (paste, profile visible, axe clean, phone width). Not tested: real websites, the real Bedrock filter on `brand`, or Haiku's real replies.

186. **One eye on the sign-in card (2026-11-10).** The owner: "i dont want it to be two eyes just one". The sign-in card showed the animated VAIC-screen eye (`Eyes`) beside the logo, which already carries the owner's eye (decision 169), so two eyes stood side by side. The card now shows only the logo. The iPhone sign-in already showed just the logo. (185 is taken by the website-link capture, pushed by its own session.)

187. **Two tap sounds: a bloop when it works, a kalimba when it can't (2026-11-10).** The owner heard twenty generated tap sounds and chose: "use 11 for stuff that are not possible or just unavailable and errors and use 19 for all normal things that are supported and work properly". Sound 19, a round synth "bloop" (a triangle wave gliding 220 → 440 Hz over 120 ms), now plays for every tap that works; sound 11, a kalimba note (an FM thumb-piano tine at 784 Hz, modulator 3.5×, ringing 450 ms), plays for a tap on a control that is switched off (`:disabled`, `aria-disabled`, a disabled fieldset; on the iPhone a disabled UIControl or SwiftUI's `.disabled`, read from the accessibility traits), for an error toast, and for an error banner that appears within four seconds of the owner's own tap, so problems found in the background stay silent. One sound per tap, the kalimba never twice in a second. Both replace the glass "tink" (decision 148's switch in Settings still turns them off). The web makes them with Web Audio (`web/src/lib/tapFx.ts`), the iPhone plays the same tones as bundled system sounds (`ios/scripts/make_tap_sound.py` writes `tap.wav` and `refuse.wav`).

188. **The homes are three New York apartment buildings a block (2026-11-10).** The owner: "instead of houses lets have a apartment complex, very new york style buildings three that are in the a asymmetric placed position", with a screenshot of the block under HQ (two rows of four houses and a lawn). `layout()` now lays the homes out as blocks of three apartment buildings, each in a 10×11-tile box at the foot of its block. The box holds a tall slim red-brick building (3×9 tiles, 8 storeys, fire escape, water tower), a brownstone mid-rise in front (4×5, stoop and iron railings, fire escape, bulkheads and a chimney) and a wide, shorter buff-brick one at the back (5×4, an awning and a lit corner shop either side, a water tower). Each stands at its own depth, with a courtyard of trees, a bench and a flower box between them, and every other block is mirrored. No building stands below another's door, so every walk goes straight out to the street (or, on a phone, to the footpath under each row of blocks). A block holds 24 residents, eight by each building's door, two rows of four on the pavement. More residents add blocks (and streets) below, as the houses did. `homes.houses` is gone: `homes.apartments`, `homes.blocks`, `homes.paths` and `homes.parks`, with `homeOf(world, slot)`, take its place, and `layout(state, { homes })` takes the number of home slots (was `houses`, two slots each). Commutes from and to home go through the building's door (`commuteRoute(..., { leaving / entering: apartment })`), and the light that marked a house now lights one window of the worker's own building. The "+N MORE IN WORKFORCE" sign hangs over the last block's courtyard. Same buildings on the wide map, on the phone and in the iPhone app's City. Tests: `web/test/map.test.ts` (sizes, uneven placement, doors, mirroring, routes, room for every resident), `web/e2e/apartments.spec.ts` (three buildings, a hired worker leaves through its door).

189. **Skills → Yours | Discover: browse and add skills like connectors (2026-11-10).** The owner: "I also need to be able to browse for skills as well like the image i attached in the chat where its in claude. just like i can browse for MCP connectors", with Claude's Settings → Skills → Discover beside AI City's Skills page, which only had an upload form. The Skills page now has the Connectors pieces (decision 129): a Yours | Discover switch, a search box and + Add (Write a skill, Upload a skill). Discover shows a featured skill, department chips (the City's own department names), and a grid of cards (a tile, the name, what it does, where it comes from and who it is for, and Add, then Added). A card opens what the skill does and its SKILL.md. The catalog is bundled, not fetched: 29 new skills written for AI City in `agents/skill-catalog/<name>/SKILL.md`, with `catalog.json` for titles, departments and the featured one, across Finance, Product, Operations, Marketing, Sales, Research, Legal, Data & AI, Media (design), Application and IT Infrastructure (engineering) and Customer Support. Next to them are the City's 57 built-in department skills, labelled "Built into <department>": adding one gives it to every team. Adding one is `POST /skills/catalog/<name>`, which hands the folder's files to the same upload code (frontmatter reduction, path checks, roles, versioned S3, the `SKILLS` ledger row, `source: "catalog"`), so the policy and per-role access are exactly an upload's. Every role may use it unless `roles` says otherwise. `GET /skills/catalog?q=&department=` and `GET /skills/catalog/<name>` read the catalog. A skill already in Yours answers 409, and "catalog" became a reserved skill name. Added shows at once with an Undo toast; Yours marks catalog skills "From Discover" and removing one offers Undo, which adds it back with the same roles. The page opens on Discover while Yours is empty. VAIC adds one the same way through `city_api` ("add the finance skill": search, pick or offer the matches, `POST`; `agents/vaic.md`), on the owner's own message only. Not built: a remote catalog. `skills_catalog.entries()` is the one place a fetched list would join, with files fetched at install time; nothing else assumes the repo. Not built either: install counts like Claude's "2.1M installs". AI City has one owner, so a card says who the skill is for instead.

190. **The brand reaches Canva and Higgsfield by itself; a bare link in Chat is learnt (2026-11-10).** The owner, to VAIC: "I should be able to paste a website link in the chat of VAIC and make it store that in the RAG (vector database and graph database) and make it scrape the website's information such as html divs h1, h2 and css so they can be using those styles for videos or marketing images in canva, so if the user pasts a website with orange theme color, then the user will use that automatically to create canva or higgsfield videos with that theme". Decision 185 already stored a link's design and content in the vector store and the graph, per brand. Added (`docs/KNOWLEDGE.md` §16.1):

(1) **Chat:** a message that is only a link puts one line in VAIC's turn input: store it, then say what was learnt. `add_to_knowledge` waits up to 30 s for the profile (it is written in the pipeline's first steps, before indexing) and answers with the name, kind, colours, fonts and tone, marked as site data. A slower page is said plainly.

(2) **Structure:** the capture keeps the h1/h2/h3 outline in order, the main sections and hinted divs with their headings and opening text, calls to action and key copy. Hidden elements are skipped, so hidden prompt-injection text never becomes structure. The model writes tone and layout from it.

(3) **Automatic brand on creative calls:** in the connector call path itself (`runtime/gateway.py`, which every connector call by every role takes), not in prompts, so it doesn't depend on an agent remembering.
- Canva and Higgsfield tools get an optional city-only `vaic_brand`.
- The brand is picked from that argument, the turn's earlier choice, the one brand the task names, or the only one stored.
- Ambiguous: the call is not sent and the agent is told to ask once, then pass `vaic_brand`. The choice sticks for the turn, so the owner is asked once.
- It is added as a paragraph to the call's prompt-like text field: the theme colour in words and hex, the palette by role, fonts with the closest kind as a fallback, the logo, the tone.
- Text was chosen over service-specific fields because the two services' tool schemas differ and change, and every one of their generate tools takes a prompt. A call without such a field gets the brand in front of its reply.

Not done:
- The logo is passed as a link, not uploaded into Canva as an asset.
- Fonts are named, not installed.
- Other image or video connectors (Ideogram, Runway) are not included: the owner named Canva and Higgsfield, and adding one is a single entry in `CREATIVE`.

Tests: pytest `test_brand_context.py`:
- the structure in order and hidden text left out;
- colour words (an orange site gives orange) and font kinds;
- one brand applied, several asked once then sticking, the task naming one, `none`, no text field, validated values only;
- through `connector_tools`: Canva and Higgsfield branded, Notion untouched, `vaic_brand` never sent, an ambiguous call never reaching the service;
- brands loaded only for turns with a creative connector;
- a bare link detected and the VAIC prompt note added only then;
- `add_to_knowledge` answering with what was learnt while the pipeline runs, and a slow page said plainly;
- a stored link becoming the next Canva call's brand.

vitest and Playwright show the page structure and calls to action.

Also fixed: decision 185's e2e mock gave every https link the sample brand and renamed it, which broke the journeys Knowledge test; the mock now does it for the sample site only. Not tested: the real Canva and Higgsfield MCP servers (their tool schemas were assumed to take a prompt-like field), Haiku's real replies, a real website.

191. **A quarter of the original cabs, and the owner's car exactly one in 50 (2026-11-10).** The owner, to VAIC: "I want you to cut the mount of taxis in half and make sure the porsche comes 1 every 50 cars". Decided: (1) The cruising cabs are halved again on top of decision 180: the map gets exactly half of decision 180's total (`cabsPerLane`, `CAB_SHARE = 0.5`), still at least one per lane, the extra ones going to the lanes that had the most; so a four-tower town drives 12 cabs (it had 24, and 48 before decision 180). (2) The owner's car was a seeded one-in-a-thousand roll per arrival (`rareCab`), so its share was only ever about right. It is now counted: every car that enters the road takes the next number from the traffic's counter (`arrive`), a cruising cab each time it comes round past the border again and a commuter's cab when it is hailed, and every 50th number (from an offset fixed per map) is the owner's car. So exactly 1 in 50 cars that enter is the Porsche, a steady 2 % that never bunches; on the road it is about 2 % of the cars over time. `RARE_CAB_ODDS` is 50; `rareCab` and `seededRandom` are gone. (3) With the Porsche now 20 times as common, it runs a red only when the way is clear: no crossing car in the junction or within about two seconds of it (a cab before could only check the junction itself), so it still never drives into anyone. Lights, roundabouts, give-way, highway pace and commuters' cabs are otherwise unchanged. Tests: vitest `rare-cab.test.ts` (every 50th number exactly, and a three-hour simulated run of cruising and hailed cabs where the Porsche is 1 in 50 of the cars that entered, within one car, and about 2 % of the cars seen on the road) and `traffic.test.ts` (half of decision 180's count, at least one per lane, on five map shapes); every traffic and commute test passes. The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it.

192. **One owner message, one VAIC turn; VAIC remembers what it did; simple, informal answers (2026-11-10).** The owner, to the team, after VAIC deleted two repos as asked and then sent three more replies to the same message saying it had never deleted anything: "can you fix these type of memory issues … it deleted my repos as i wanted and then it tries to see if there are any repos but forgets that he already deleted the repos … and can you system prompt the chat to always answer me in a simple and structured manner like it is a natural and normal person using informal language". The same happened on "create Test Repo-2" ("Done", then twice "it already exists") and on the delete's yes/no question (asked three times).
    Root cause: VAIC is the one role the dispatcher Lambda still waits on (decision 178), and it called the runtime through the shared boto3 client, whose read timeout is 10 s and which retries up to five times (`shared/clients.py`). A turn that ran a tool took longer than 10 s (the delete took 12 s), so botocore sent the same invocation again, and again: every retry started a new VAIC turn for the same message in the runtime, which never checked whether that message already had one. The extra turns could not see the first one's delete either: VAIC's conversation kept only the owner's message and VAIC's final words, written at the end of a turn, so a turn running alongside it found no repos, no record of deleting them, and said "I never ran a delete; my earlier Done was wrong".
    Decided: (1) **One turn per message.** The runtime claims `vaic-turn:<message id>` (ledger `claim`, a day) before a VAIC turn; any further delivery of the same message (a retried call, SQS redelivering, the crash sweep, the HUD sending twice) returns at once with no turn and no reply. A turn that fails before calling any tool gives the claim back, so a throttle or model error is still retried; one that already acted never runs again. (2) **The dispatcher calls VAIC once.** VAIC's call has its own client: no retries, a 105-second read timeout (under the Lambda's 120 s); if it still times out, the turn is left running in the runtime (its reply streams to the HUD as usual) and the message is not sent again. Other roles are unchanged (asynchronous since decision 178). (3) **VAIC's action log.** Every tool call that can change something (all but the read-only ones: status, lists, searches, showing files, offering choices, using a skill) is written into VAIC's conversation the moment it finishes, as `[VAIC action 2026-11-10 19:40 UTC] run_command: gh repo delete ProjectVAIC/test-repo-1 --yes → ok: exit 0 …` (FAILED when it was denied, invalid, raised, or a command exited non-zero). The owner's message is now written when the turn starts, so the history reads message, actions, reply. VAIC's prompt reads the last 16 lines instead of 12 to make room. `agents/vaic.md` gains "Your own actions": the log is fact; if it shows the thing was done, say it's already done and when; a re-check that finds nothing is because VAIC did it; never deny, doubt or apologise for a logged action; say "Done" only after a tool came back successful. (4) **Style.** "How you speak" in `agents/vaic.md` now starts with the owner's rule: casual, friendly and informal like a normal person, short sentences and plain words, the answer first, short bullets or numbered steps only when there's more than one thing, each thing said once, no corporate filler. "Sir" stays; spoken replies stay one to three sentences of plain speech without markdown, and both voice prompts (`voice.md`, `voice_live.md`) say the same. (5) **The HUD** shows one answer per message id: once a message's final reply is in, anything else for that id is dropped.
    Tests: pytest `test_vaic_one_turn.py` (the same message delivered again, three at once, and twice through the queue gives one turn and one reply; the action is in the history mid-turn, after the owner's line; a later turn's prompt holds the earlier delete; read-only tools aren't logged; a turn that failed before acting runs again and one that acted doesn't; the direct engine reports each tool after it ran; the action line's format; VAIC's call on its own client, never retried, a timeout left running, other roles still failing on one; the prompt's style and action-log rules); vitest `stop.test.tsx` (a second turn's reply for an answered message never shows). The full backend suite, ruff, mypy, the HUD typecheck and the touched vitest files pass. Not tested: against live AgentCore and Bedrock (no AWS access from this session), whether Haiku now actually phrases things as asked (prompt only), the iPhone app's chat (it takes replies the same way and is not changed), and the gated turn's own throttle retry after a tool already ran (rare; it reruns the turn inside the same claim).

193. **A real favicon.ico, so Safari drops the old amber icon (2026-11-10).** The owner still saw the old amber skyline icon in Safari's tab after decision 173. The site served only the eye, but Safari also asks for `/favicon.ico` on its own, and that address returned the HUD's page (the single-page fallback), so Safari kept the icon it had saved before. `web/public/favicon.ico` now holds the owner's eye at 16, 32 and 48 px (PNG inside ICO): a light eye with a thin dark edge, so it reads on both a dark and a light tab bar, because an .ico cannot follow the browser's theme. It is linked first with `sizes="32x32"`; browsers that take SVG still pick the theme-following `vaic-icon.svg` after it. Safari may keep its saved icon until its website data for the site is cleared once.

194. **GitHub has one place: its card in Connectors (2026-11-10).** The owner asked why GitHub had its own Settings page when it is also a connector. It was two doors to one thing: Settings → GitHub saved the city's one token (`vaic/prod/github`, decision 110), and the GitHub connector already used that token, yet its card still showed a key or "own app" form of its own. Now the token lives on GitHub's row in Settings → Connectors (Connected view) and on its card in Browse: add, replace or remove it there. Saving it on the Browse card also connects GitHub's tools, which use the same token, so there is no second key or sign-in. The card has no separate Connect button. The Settings → GitHub page is gone from the menu; its old address (`#/settings/github`, still used in links from VAIC, Away and the iPhone app) opens Connectors with the GitHub row open. The token's API (`/github`, `/github/token`), its storage and who uses it are unchanged.

195. **The tab icon is only the eye's lines, never on a tile (2026-11-10).** After decision 193 Safari showed the eye on a white tile with its inside filled white: it picked the linked `favicon.ico`, which was a light eye with a dark outline. The owner: no background, nothing filled in except the eye's own lines; light on a dark browser, dark on a light one. That is exactly `vaic-icon.svg` (the traced lines, coloured by `prefers-color-scheme`), so `favicon.ico` is no longer linked and Safari takes the SVG (or the dark and light PNG copies of the same lines). `favicon.ico` stays at its address for browsers that ask for it by name, now only the lines in a mid grey that reads on both, with no outline or fill.

196. **The voice conversation hears the owner in Mac Safari (2026-11-10).** The owner, to VAIC: "I can't hear anything when I try to talk to Vice. In the chat". In Mac Safari, on the voice button: "Voice on · standard", then "You" and "Listening to you…", and it stayed there; nothing was sent, so VAIC never answered.
    Ruled out, live from the signed-in HUD in the browser pane: the recreated identity pool hands out speech credentials; Polly in eu-central-1 returns audio (generative, neural and standard engines); Transcribe streaming in eu-central-1, with and without the `vaic-prod-names` word list, turned a Polly-spoken "Hey Vice, check my mail, please" into exactly that text at 0.99 confidence. The OpenAI key being gone is not it either: without it the page uses Polly, as designed. In Chromium the real voice code (not the test fake) gets credentials, asks Polly once per sentence and plays each one.
    Root cause: the bar never changed to "You're talking", which it shows as soon as the microphone's level rises, so the page was reading silence while the owner spoke. The microphone was read through an AudioContext made inside the tap (`prime`, for Safari's audio unlock), before Safari granted the microphone; Safari can give a context made then nothing but silence from the microphone, and Transcribe, hearing silence, never returns a phrase. No error is raised, so nothing was said. The tap sounds of decision 187 use their own context and are not involved.
    Decided: (1) The microphone's context is made after the microphone is granted (Safari lets audio start while the microphone is on; other browsers still have the tap's activation); the context made in the tap is only the fallback if a new one stays paused. The reply's output context and the media element are still unlocked in the tap as before. (2) If the microphone hands over nothing but digital silence for 4 s, the audio graph is rebuilt once on a fresh context; if it is still silent, VAIC says so on screen ("The microphone is on, but no sound reaches VAIC from it", with where to check in macOS and Safari) and keeps listening, in case the room is just very quiet. (3) The loudness gate keeps a phrase that is clearly louder than the room (2.5 × its 20th percentile) without the absolute minimum of 0.01, which Safari's quiet voice-processed microphone could fall under. (4) Always listening is unchanged and now also tested while VAIC is thinking: the owner's words are heard and sent while VAIC works, and talking over VAIC while it speaks stops it at once (decision 160).
    Tests: Playwright `safari.spec.ts` in a new `safari` project (WebKit, Safari's engine) runs the real voice code with Safari's behaviour played back (a context made before the grant reads silence) and a mock Transcribe that, like the real one, answers only when it gets sound: the owner is heard and the message sent; and a microphone that is silent everywhere is said plainly with nothing sent. Both fail on the old code. vitest: the silence check, a quiet but clear phrase kept, and speech during thinking sent. The manual `ci.yml` installs WebKit too. Not tested: real Mac Safari with a real microphone and real Transcribe end to end (Safari cannot be driven from this session), and iPhone Safari.
197. **Top menu: a sliding glass highlight (2026-11-10).** The owner asked for the "City | Features" slide on the top menu, in a glassier style. One absolutely placed pill (`.navthumb`) inside `nav.tabs`; App.tsx measures the open tab (`offsetLeft/Top/Width/Height`) into CSS variables on every view change and on resize (ResizeObserver), so it works with any tab widths and pinned apps. The first placement jumps, later ones slide (`data-ready`). Glass: translucent white gradient over `--glass-3`, backdrop blur and saturation, a 1px lit top edge and a soft shadow; a stronger white in the light look. No SVG displacement: it only bends in Chromium and the bar under it is already a blur, so it would add cost without a visible difference in Safari.
198. **Files: photos load behind frosted glass (2026-11-10).** `FileThumb` in Files.tsx: for an image, a frost layer (backdrop blur, white gradient, lit edge, a sweeping light) over a soft colour wash, and the thumbnail fading in from blur(18px)/scale(1.06) on `onLoad`. During an upload from this browser, an object URL of the photo (kept in memory in lib/files.ts, revoked after the thumbnail shows) sits blurred under the frost. Reduced motion: no sweep, no fade. The "upsert" and "removed" reducer cases now keep `count` in step, so a new upload no longer reads "0 files".
199. **Connect cards survive a reload (2026-11-10).** The cards lived only in the in-memory store while the chat was in localStorage, so a reload or deploy dropped them. They are now saved under `vaic.cards` next to `vaic.chat` (no keys or passwords are ever in a card), cleared on sign-out; approval cards are not saved because they may already be decided. Restored sign-in cards are followed again on return, so a sign-in finished meanwhile turns them to connected.
200. **Tab icon: white lines only (2026-11-10).** Replaces decision 195's theme-following pair. Safari ignores `media` on icon links and drew the dark-lined PNG on a light tile in its dark tab bar. Now one SVG (`fill="#ffffff"`, no style or background) and one 64px PNG of the same white lines on transparency, at new names; favicon.ico rebuilt from that PNG; the apple-touch-icon (white eye on black, needed opaque by iOS) is unchanged. On a light browser the white lines are faint: the owner chose white because the browser is dark.
201. **No manager inside the tower (2026-11-10).** `deptProjects` (lib/visit.ts) no longer adds the department's manager to a project's people and leaves out any agent of kind `manager`, so floors seat workers only and a project with only its manager has one empty floor. The manager is still drawn and clickable at the tower's door on the City map (scene.js), unchanged.
202. **Agent card: one layer, fewer rows (2026-11-10).** `CityVisit` marks the project picker `behind` (hidden, inert) while `turned` is set, because the glass card and the picker share the top-right corner and the picker read through it. The agent card drops "Reports to", folds department and model into one line, drops the ticket status words before the goal, and shows three recent actions under "Just did" instead of six.
203. **Tab icon follows the browser, as an SVG only (2026-11-10).** The owner wants the hollow eye black on a light browser and white on a dark one. The SVG carries `path{fill:#000}` and a `prefers-color-scheme:dark` rule to `#fff`, which browsers apply inside SVG icons; it is the only icon link, so Safari has no PNG to tile. favicon.ico (white lines) stays for browsers that request it by name. Supersedes 200.
204. **Build steps, like Claude (2026-11-10).** The runtime already sent an `activity` event for every allowed tool call; it now carries `detail` from `step_detail` (runtime/engine.py): a plain line from the tool name and a few input fields (command, file path, query, URL), trimmed and with secret-shaped text (GitHub/OpenAI/Slack/AWS keys, bearer tokens, `password=`/`token=`-style pairs, URL credentials, 40+ character blobs) replaced by •••. Paths drop the `/tmp/work/<agent>/` prefix. Tool input is outside content, so only this short label leaves the runtime; nothing is stored server-side beyond what was already audited. On the web, `lib/buildSteps.ts` files each step under the project conversation found by walking the agent's job up to the ticket that carries `conversation` (at arrival, since finished tickets leave the live state), keeps 200 per project in localStorage (`vaic.buildsteps`, cleared on sign-out), and moves them when a new project is published as an app. `BuildLog` in Projects.tsx groups consecutive steps of one kind into `<details>` with a count, and shows a ring with three bouncing dots while any live ticket belongs to the project. VAIC's chat steps (`lib/steps.ts`) show such a sentence as-is instead of mapping a tool name.
205. **A worker's folder exists before its turn; a failed turn shows (2026-11-11).** `AgentHost` gave workers `cwd=/tmp/work/<agent>` since the Python port (Nov 6) but never created it; only `run_command` made the workspace root on first use. The Claude Agent SDK now refuses a missing working directory (`CLIConnectionError`), so every worker turn failed before its first step and the crash sweep re-sent it every five minutes. The host now `mkdir -p`s the folder before the engine runs. On any exception in a turn it also puts an `activity` event `failed` with "Its last try failed: <type>: <message>" (one line, secret-blanked, 400 characters), which the agent card shows in place of the generic words; the failure is still raised, logged as `turn.failed`, and retried by the sweep as before.
206. **VAIC's changes wait for the owner's Yes (2026-11-11).** The owner wants to change any setting, add skills and do anything clickable from the chat or by voice, with VAIC showing what will change and asking "Do you want to save these changes?" with Yes / No buttons first. Decision 143 had VAIC run every write at once. Now `city_api` PUT, POST and DELETE (and the appearance switch) only stage the call: one per conversation in the ledger (`VAIC_PENDING`), shown in the main chat as a card (title, one line per change with old → new, Yes / No; a skill's description with Add / Not now). `save_change` runs or drops it, and refuses in the turn that staged it, so the model can't answer for the owner: the yes has to come in the owner's next message, typed, tapped or spoken (a tap is sent as an ordinary message, so all three are one path). The owner may say something else instead; the change keeps waiting, and a newer change replaces it. `find_skill` searches the catalog Discover uses: an exact title or name wins, several matches become tappable choices, none is said. Settings are checked before the card appears with the same function the route uses (`caps.settings_patch`), so the card never shows a change the city would refuse; keys that are not settings (safety rules, IAM, the policy file, self-improvement) are refused, since the API has no such setting. Spend caps stay changeable here because they are the owner's own settings (decision 143) and now need the Yes; the self-improvement loop is untouched. The `confirm: true` flag for deletes is gone: the Yes card is the confirmation. The main chat now draws `vaic.question` (until now only project chats did); a new `vaic.change` event closes the card with Saved or Not saved. The iPhone app doesn't draw the card yet; the owner can answer there by typing or speaking.
207. **A new hire's taxi to work, called, waiting and highlighted (2026-11-11).** The owner: highlight a car carrying someone to work; show the taxi being called, driving to the worker's apartment building and stopping there until the worker gets in; keep it highlighted to the department's tower; when the worker gets out, no highlight, and the worker walks in. Decided, all in the City scene from the existing hire commute (no new backend events): (1) When a worker is hired, `taxiCall` (city-state.js) finds the kerb where the commute's first ridden leg starts and a start point up to `CALL_PX` (150 px) behind it on the same street, heading the way the ride sets off and kept on that street's own stretch. The called cab drives there with the traffic (`rideStep`, a guest to the cruising cabs, round any roundabout), slows and stops at the kerb; after 25 s stuck it is simply placed there. (2) The worker stays at home until the cab has pulled up, then walks out of the building's door to it; stepping in turns the waiting cab into the ride (same car, no fade-in, the Porsche count taken at the call). (3) From the call until the worker is dropped at the tower's door the cab glows: a soft breathing yellow light around it that grows as the map zooms out, a yellow light on the road under it, a two-pixel yellow outline, and the selected agent's yellow arrow above it (scaled up when zoomed out). The cab that drives off after the drop is ordinary again. (4) Each hire has its own taxi. Rides home and executives' rides are unchanged and not highlighted. Reduced motion keeps placement instant (no taxi, as before), and the glow does not breathe. Test hooks: `data-taxi-calls`, `data-glowing-cabs`, `p.call`, `p.toWork`. Tests: vitest `commute-traffic.test.ts` (the call's kerb and start for many homes and towers); Playwright `commute.spec.ts` (two hires: both called and glowing, the worker held at home until the cab arrives, the cab at the kerb, riding highlighted, both dropped inside, glow and calls back to 0; screenshots `city-taxi-called.png`, `city-taxi-to-work.png`). The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it.
208. **Chat: quiet while sending, pinned to new replies (2026-11-11).** An owner line starts `queued` until POST /messages answers; the chat showed the offline note and the outbox banner for that state, so every send flashed a warning. Now the note shows for `failed`, or `queued` while the live connection is not online, and the banner counts only `failed`. Scrolling: a `stick` flag (true unless the owner scrolled more than 120 px from the bottom) plus a MutationObserver and ResizeObserver on the chat keep it at the bottom while content grows; a new VAIC or owner line sets `stick` and scrolls down.
209. **Chat saves take the shape VAIC read; bad shapes are 400s (2026-11-11).** `executives.set_config` normalises its body with `_keyed`: the documented `{enabled: {coo: true}, reviews: {id: {enabled, every_days}}}` or the lists `GET /executives` returns (`executives: [{id, enabled}]`, `reviews: [{id, enabled, every_days}]`), and raises `Invalid` (400) for anything else. `vaic_call` maps `AttributeError`, `TypeError` and `KeyError` from a route to a 400 that names the method, path and error, so a mis-shaped body after the owner's Yes reads as fixable, not as "something went wrong on our side". `agents/vaic.md` gives the executives body and tells VAIC to restage a refused change with the fix and ask again.
210. **The chat box is one slim row on a phone (2026-11-11).** On a narrow phone the hint "Type your message…" wrapped to two lines and the auto-height measured it, so the empty box stood two lines tall. An empty box is now exactly one line, the hint is "Ask VAIC…" with `white-space: nowrap`, and the phone padding is a little tighter. The buttons stay 44px tap targets (the phone tests require it). Tested: Playwright at 375px (box ≤ 54px with and without text) and all web checks; not checked on the owner's iPhone.
211. **A reload never replays the commute; to-work cabs are 2.5x (2026-11-11).** The owner: reloading the City while people work restarted the taxi rides from their homes; it should just show the lit windows, and the taxi animation only play for a new hire while the City is open; a cab taking someone to work drives 2.5x the normal speed. Decided: `load()` (every snapshot: opening, reloading, reconnecting) calls `sync(true)`, so everyone is placed at once (workers inside, their windows lit); only live events walk, call a taxi and ride. `rideStep` takes the rider's `boost` (default 1) on top speed, the hold-limited speed and acceleration; the scene sets `TO_WORK_BOOST` (2.5) while `p.toWork`, so the cab still stops for lights and the car ahead. The called cab's drive to the pickup and rides home are unchanged. Tests: vitest (2.5x on an open road), e2e (a snapshot with a working worker: no ride, no taxi call; a live hire still rides), the traffic e2e cap is now 2.5x cab pace. Not checked on the live site.
212. **Every tower always has its manager at the door (2026-11-11).** The owner wants a Department Manager outside every department at all times, built-in or built on demand. In `web/src/city/city-state.js` `placement()` puts any manager who is not retired at his station, which the scene draws beside the tower's street door (decision 201 keeps him off the floors), so a dormant or idle manager no longer goes home. `staffed()` runs after every snapshot and event: each standing department without a live manager record gets a stand-in (`standin: true`, id `manager_id` or `mgr-<key>` like `manager_id()` in `backend/src/vaic/shared/ids.py`), dropped when a real manager for that department arrives or the department is demolished; `topBar` does not count stand-ins. The backend is unchanged: departments always carry a `manager_id`, but the manager record is still created with the department's first goal (`ensure_manager` in `backend/src/vaic/runtime/tools.py`, decision 119), so before that the City shows the stand-in. Tested: vitest (dormant manager at the door, stand-in for a department with no agent, live `department.created`, replacement and demolition) and Playwright `e2e/managers.spec.ts` (three towers and no manager agents → one manager beside each door; a live department adds one); not checked on the owner's iPhone.
213. **At home means indoors, and the neighbourhood never grows (2026-11-11).** The owner: residents with nothing to do stacked up on the pavement outside their apartment buildings; they should go in, light one window each, and once the windows are full the building should not grow but show the number at home like the towers. Decided: `homeSpot` is `inside: true`, so anyone at home (placed, walked or driven home, or a fresh hire before the taxi comes) is inside and drawn hidden; `setInside` counts them under `home:<apartment index>`. The scene lights one window per resident in a stable order per building (capped at its windows) and draws the count above each roof, like the towers' counters. The scene lays out one block of homes only (`homes` capped at `HOMES_PER_BLOCK`), and `homeOf` wraps later slots onto the same buildings. `RETIRED_SHOWN` goes from 24 to 1000, so everyone at home is counted and the "+N MORE IN WORKFORCE" sign no longer shows in practice. Leaving home still comes out of the door when the taxi has pulled up. Tests: e2e with 30 released workers (nobody outside, all counted, one block, screenshot `city-homes.png`), and all web checks. Not checked on the live site.
214. **Drag and drop onto the chat, 10 images at most (2026-11-11).** The owner: allow dragging and dropping up to 10 images. The chat panel (`.talk` in `VaicScreen.tsx`) takes file drops (only drags that carry files; a depth counter keeps the hint steady over child elements) and hands them to the same `onFiles` as the + menu, so they upload to the knowledge base and show as chips. `capImages` (`lib/knowledge.ts`) keeps every non-image file and the first images up to `MAX_CHAT_IMAGES` (10), counting images already attached; the rest are left out with a toast. The cap applies to the + menu too. Tests: vitest `chat-images.test.ts`, e2e (drop 12 images and a PDF: 11 chips, the note, 11 uploads). Not checked with a real drag in Safari.
215. **Executive changes from the chat are checked when staged; every refused save is logged (2026-11-11).** After decision 209 the owner again got "the save didn't go through" for the COO bottleneck review, daily. The logs showed no failed request in the API and nothing in VAIC's runtime: the API only logged crashes, and a 400 refusal (`problem()`) or a failed invoke left no trace, so the exact body could not be recovered. Decided: (1) `executives.check_config` validates and normalises a `PUT /executives` body without saving it; `set_config` uses it, and `city_api` runs it at staging, so a body the save would refuse is answered "Not staged, the city would refuse it: …" before the owner's Yes, and VAIC fixes it. (2) It accepts what VAIC tends to send: `every_days` as 1/7/14/30, a numeric string or daily/weekly/fortnightly/monthly; on/off/yes/no for switches; the GET's own fields (`title`, `last_run_at`) and ids that are not the four executives are ignored. (3) The staged card names the change ("COO bottleneck review: on, daily"). (4) Logging: the API logs `api.vaic_refused` for every 4xx/5xx it returns to VAIC, the runtime logs `vaic.save` for every save outcome, and a failed Lambda invoke becomes a logged 502 with the reason instead of a raw exception. Tests: `test_owner_api.py` (a wrong review id is not staged; words and GET fields save the COO review daily), `test_executives.py`. Not reproduced live: the original body is unknown.
216. **Managers wear suits and sunglasses (2026-11-11).** The owner: "make all managers have sunglasses and suits on". Every figure of kind `manager` is drawn by `paintFigure` in `web/src/city/scene.js`, so the door, the stand-ins (decision 212) and any walk share it: jacket and trousers `SUIT.jacket`, lapels either side, a white collar and a two-pixel tie in the department's colour (the old hat colour, so each manager still carries his department), and sunglasses across both eyes with a glint on each lens. The department-coloured hat is dropped so the manager's own hair shows; skin and hair come from `identity()` as before. Inside a tower (`web/src/lib/officeArt.ts`) the manager already wore a navy suit, shirt and tie; his trousers are now the suit's and, facing you, he wears sunglasses (from behind you see no face). Workers and everyone else are unchanged. Tests: vitest `web/test/manager-suits.test.ts` reads the painted pixels back (jacket, trousers, collar, tie, both lenses in three poses; no hat; a worker has none of it; the seated manager's sunglasses and shirt); a screenshot zoomed in on a manager by his door in light and dark looked right. The iPhone app's embedded City is rebuilt with it. Not checked on the owner's iPhone.
217. **Department knowledge bases, marketing first (2026-11-11).** The owner's brief: a foundational knowledge base per department in the stores the city already uses, marketing only for now, department-generic, defaults shipped with the deploy and then the owner's to change. Following the existing setup instead of the brief's defaults: one shared OpenSearch Serverless index with a mandatory department filter (an index or collection per department would add about $186/month each and the Bedrock knowledge base owns the index), Bedrock's own chunking with each entry pre-split into small parts (`<entry id>/<n>.md`, each starting with the title and heading path, under the default 300-token chunk) so ids are deterministic and every chunk carries its context, the existing knowledge table as the registry (gsi1 `ENTRY#<department>`), Neptune with separate `KnowledgeEntry`/taxonomy vertices so the owner items' graph is untouched, and the knowledge Lambda as the seeder (Neptune Analytics is reached through its IAM endpoint, so no VPC step). Isolation is enforced twice in `search.py`: a `notIn` Retrieve filter and a re-check of every record, so an index that cannot filter only shortens answers. The seeder's rules (create, follow untouched defaults, keep edits and tombstones, record `newer_default`, never touch the owner's entries, retire removed seeds unless edited, re-embed on a model change, repair lagging projections) run on every deploy in a `knowledge` job beside the HUD upload, which fails red without holding the HUD back. Content: 131 entries restructured from the research file only, every labelled claim kept word for word with its date and link, `[GAP]` passages moved to `knowledge/gaps/marketing.md`; confidence and volatility derived from the labels. Infra the deploy applies: six API Gateway routes, `bedrock:Retrieve` on the knowledge Lambda (read-only, for the retrieval evaluation), one Terraform output; nothing for S3 (versioning was already on), OpenSearch or Neptune. Tests: backend `test_knowledge_entries.py` (schema, every reconciliation rule, idempotency, isolation with and without a working filter, graph, API, evaluation), web vitest and a Playwright journey. Offline retrieval evaluation 22/22 in the top 5 (keywords only, questions written by the builder); the live hybrid hit rate is printed by the deploy. Not checked against the real index before merge.
218. **Department filters off the Retrieve call (2026-11-11).** The first deploy of decision 217 seeded and indexed all 131 marketing entries (131 ready), but the live retrieval evaluation found 0 of 22, with no hits at all. The only new thing in the Retrieve call was the department `notIn` filter (plus audience and entry filters) on metadata fields the index had never filtered on; the CloudWatch logs could not be read from the build session. Because isolation was already enforced on every record (`entry_visible`, tested with an index that ignores filters), the filter is now kept off Retrieve (`RETRIEVE_ENTRY_FILTERS = False`), Retrieve uses the scope filter that has worked since v2, and a caller from a department with a knowledge base fetches 30 hits so its entries survive the record check. If the index-side filter was also emptying other agents' searches, this restores them. The evaluation now records, for each miss, the item ids the index returned with the scope filter alone, so the next deploy log separates "the index has nothing" from "the filter drops it". Not verified before merge: that the live hit rate recovers.
219. **Diagnose an empty knowledge index (2026-11-11).** After decision 218 the live evaluation still found 0 of 22, and Retrieve with only the scope filter (in use since v2) returned no ids either, so the cause is not the department filter: either the index answers nothing, or its hits carry no `item_id`/`scope` metadata (which would also empty searches of the owner's own items and the guide). The build session has no AWS access, so `live_eval` now records, for the first miss, one Retrieve with no filter: hit count, metadata keys, item ids and source URIs. Read-only; nothing else changes.
220. **One department for apps; ask before an overlapping department (2026-11-11).** The owner: "Remove apps department and make sure to only have application department", and when a new project would get a new department whose work overlaps an existing one, VAIC and the Orchestrator ask first: the department they suggest, or the project in the best-fitting existing department; several departments may still work on different parts of the same project. How "Apps" existed: it was never a preset; the Orchestrator's prompt told it to create an Apps department for games and small tools for AI City, the App build workflow (`config/workflows.json`) named it, and the City and HUD defaulted an app's department to "Apps". Decided: (1) Application is the one department for building and publishing apps. Its preset gets `"replaces": ["Apps"]` and the aliases apps, app, app studio, games and game development; `presets.replaced_by` and `preset_named` make "Apps" mean Application everywhere, so `build_department`, `create_department` and `assign_to_department` never make an Apps department again. The App build workflow, `home_department` in `shared/apps.py` (a late submission from the old department), the City's cabinets (`scene.js`) and the HUD's `appsDepartment` all land on Application. The Apps worker and review rules moved from `agents/departments/apps.md` into `agents/departments/application.md`; the Orchestrator and VAIC prompts say so. (2) Prod data moves once, on deploy: the scheduler job `merge_departments` (invoked by `deploy.yml` after Terraform; a failed invoke only warns) runs `merge_replaced_departments`, which demolishes a standing Apps department through the normal demolition (workers home, open parts cancelled and handed back to the Orchestrator with "Hand that part to the Application department", manager retired, record kept as history with `merged_into`), re-homes its published apps (`APPS`) and drafts (`APPDRAFTS`) to Application, and records `MIGRATIONS / DEPT_MERGE#apps`. The City load calls it too: one read once done, and it relabels the files store (the scheduler has no files table) once. Nothing is deleted: apps stay published at their URLs, old tickets and hires keep "Apps" as history. (3) Overlap: `presets.overlapping(name, standing)` matches the new name's words against each standing department's name, mission, aliases and teams (a name that already is a department or an alias, like "Engineering", is that department, not an overlap). In `delegate_to_orchestrator`, when triage suggests such a new department, VAIC shows a `vaic.question` card ("New department: X", "Use Y", "Not now") and starts nothing until the owner picks; the pick (`department_choice`) is stored on the task and told to the Orchestrator. The Orchestrator's `create_department` enforces it in code: "use" refuses the new department; with no choice it asks the owner (an `ask_owner` question in Needs you with the two options, default the existing department; the Chief of Staff answers under Autopilot) and creates nothing until the answer, which it then records. A department the owner asks to build by name goes through `build_department` with no question. Tests: `backend/tests/test_one_app_department.py`; vitest `appsDepartment`; Playwright apps specs use the Application building, with an old "Apps" app shown there. Not verified before merge: the prod data the merge finds (the deploy log prints it).

221. **Plain Retrieve when a filtered one finds nothing (2026-11-11).** The diagnostic of decision 219 showed the live index answering a Retrieve with no filter and the default search type with exactly the expected marketing entries and all their metadata (`item_id`, `scope`, `department`, ...), while every filtered hybrid Retrieve, even the scope-only filter in use since v2, returned nothing. So agents' knowledge searches have probably found nothing since the filter or hybrid setting broke, not only for marketing. `_retrieve` now falls back, whenever a filtered call is empty, to the proven plain call (no filter, no `overrideSearchType`, 30 results); the record check (`_usable`: status, scope, market, brand, department, audience, entry filters) is unchanged and decides everything. Which of the two settings the index rejects (the filter or `HYBRID`) is not yet known; fixing it at the index would save the second call and restore keyword matching. Not verified before merge: the live hit rate, which the deploy prints.
222. **The owner's house-style document (2026-11-11).** The owner added `marketing-hormozi-playbook.md`: keep it unchanged at `knowledge/seed/marketing/`, seed it as a built-in marketing document titled "Marketing: House style (Hormozi playbook)", give it `house-style` where documents carry a confidence or type, have managers and workers apply it by default (compliance overrides; strategy, unit economics and measurement govern budget and judging; the brand's voice, never Hormozi's name, likeness or catchphrases), and keep its section cross-references working. Since entries carry front matter and the file must not change, whole documents are described in a sidecar `_documents.yaml`; `house-style` was added to the allowed types and confidences and a `house-style` domain to the taxonomy; `cross_refs` (only set on documents, so the 131 entries keep their hashes and are not re-indexed) map its section numbers to the current entry ids and are checked like links. Workers had no department prompt, so `agents/departments/<key>.workers.md` now reaches every worker of that department. Long documents return their matching passages. Tests pin the file's SHA-256, check the cross-references, the prompts and that an owner edit survives a redeploy; the offline evaluation (24 questions with two house-style ones) is 24/24. The deploy now prints the raw evaluation result, since the previous run showed only "skipped". Not verified before merge: live retrieval.
223. **BatchGetItem keys plain, not typed (2026-11-11).** With decision 221 the live index answered, and the deploy's evaluation then failed with "The provided key element does not match the schema" from BatchGetItem. `DynamoTable.batch_get` sent typed keys (`{"S": pk}`) through `table.meta.client`, the table resource's client, which converts plain values itself, so the keys went out as `{"M": {"S": {"S": ...}}}` (shown offline with boto3: the plain form is serialised correctly, the typed one is not). Its only caller is `KnowledgeStore.get_items`, used by every `search_knowledge`, so any search that found something failed; the earlier test faked a raw client and could not see it. Keys and answers are now plain; a new test builds a real boto3 table resource and checks the request body on the wire. The unused deserializer is removed. Not verified before merge: the live hit rate.
224. **Sales knowledge base (2026-11-11).** The owner's brief: the same built-in, editable knowledge as marketing for the sales department, from `sales-research.md` only (saved unchanged at `knowledge/source/sales-research.md`, SHA-256 pinned in a test). One document per numbered section (32) plus "Sales: Overview, labels and conclusion" (the title, introduction, reading guide and conclusion), each at `knowledge/seed/sales/<domain>/<slug>.md` with the section's text verbatim (labels, dates, links and the "checked on primary source" wording unchanged; `[GAP]` passages stay in place, section 32 is its own document), adding only front matter, a "When to use this" line and the source heading. Seeding, storage, replace, reset, delete, re-index and isolation reuse the marketing mechanism unchanged (the seeder, registry, projections, record check and HUD card are department-generic). The one code change, approved by the owner: `MAX_BODY_CHARS` 20,000 → 32,000, since sections 11, 15 and 24 are 32k, 25k and 23k characters (one document per section, as asked; long documents return their matching passages).
