Four tables are longer than one 300-token chunk (kept whole, each row repeats its link; Bedrock splits them). Types: sections 2 to 10 `house-style` (type and confidence); 27 to 31 domain `compliance` and type `policy`, the only sales documents of that type, so `entry_type: policy` retrieves exactly the compliance documents; section 11 confidence `contested`; the others by their dominant label, volatility from the `[FAST-MOVING]` count. Section numbers cited in a text are `cross_refs` to the documents that hold them ("sections 30 and 33" cites a section the source does not have). Prompts: `agents/departments/sales.md` and the new `sales.workers.md` carry the order compliance, evidence, house style (section 11), the brand's own voice with no Hormozi name, likeness, catchphrases or figures, and `needs_verification` and `[GAP]` handling. Point 5: the owner chose no new approval gates, disclosure check or send log, and asked to remove or relax the existing approval requirements; that is a separate change, not made here. Download of a document is not built (marketing has none either; the owner: skip). Tests: `backend/tests/test_sales_knowledge.py` (verbatim split, types, idempotent seeding, sales and marketing isolation both ways with and without index filters, replace leaves only the new parts and graph edges, prompts, retrieval); offline evaluation 22/23 in the top 5 (keywords only); the deploy prints the live hybrid rate. Not verified before merge: live retrieval.
225. **Application knowledge base (2026-11-11).** The owner's brief: the same built-in, editable knowledge as marketing and sales for the Application department, from `application-research.md` only (saved unchanged at `knowledge/source/application-research.md`, 27,172 words, last heading "Conclusion", SHA-256 pinned in a test). One document per numbered section (18) plus "Application: About this knowledge base" (the opening summary and "How to read and split this document"), each at `knowledge/seed/application/<domain>/<slug>.md` with the section's text verbatim (labels, dates, links and the "checked on primary source" wording unchanged; `[GAP]` passages stay in place), adding only front matter, a "When to use this" line and the source heading; the conclusion stays with section 18 (consolidated gaps), its own document. Seeding, storage, edit, reset, delete, re-index and isolation reuse the marketing mechanism unchanged; no code changed. Types: sections 11 to 13 (EU regulation, Sweden, customer trust) domain `compliance` and type `policy`, section 16 domain `guardrails` and type `checklist`, each the only application documents of their type so `entry_type` retrieves them directly; no `[HOUSE STYLE]` material exists in the source. Section numbers cited in a text are `cross_refs`. Prompts: `agents/departments/application.md` and the new `application.workers.md`: consult first; law and compliance first, independent evidence second, the rest after (the source states no precedence, so this is the owner's order); section 16's three lists, with `ask_owner` before every "needs human approval" action and a manager's approval never counting as a human one; `needs_verification`, stale `[FAST-MOVING]` and `[GAP]` handling; legal content as orientation; section 17 for handoffs. Point 7: none of the approval gates can be enforced for Application alone today, because `config/policy.json` rules match tool and input only, with no department; city-wide "ask" rules would also gate every other department and the owner's own work, against decision 100, so none were added and the owner was asked. Not built, reported to the owner: Download of a document's original, a versioned Replace that keeps the last ready version searchable while a new one indexes and after a failed index (its test is an expected failure), and the "shared" scope (plan in docs/KNOWLEDGE.md §17). Two shared tests widened: kept-whole tables up to 600 words (the guardrails' tables are 553 and 527) and the graph's departments. Tests: `backend/tests/test_application_knowledge.py`; offline evaluation 17/18 in the top 5 (keywords only); the deploy prints the live hybrid rate. Not verified before merge: live retrieval.
226. **IT infrastructure knowledge base (2026-11-11).** The owner's brief: the same built-in, editable knowledge as marketing and sales for the IT Infrastructure department, from `it-infrastructure-research.md` only (saved unchanged at `knowledge/source/it-infrastructure-research.md`, 27,346 words, last heading "Conclusion", SHA-256 pinned in a test). One document per numbered section (20) plus "IT infrastructure: About this knowledge base" (the title, opening summary and "How to read and split this document"); the Conclusion stays with section 20 ("Consolidated gaps and conclusion"). Each document is `knowledge/seed/infrastructure/<domain>/<slug>.md`, the section's text verbatim (labels, dates, links and "checked on primary source" wording unchanged), adding only front matter, a "When to use this" line and the source heading. The folder is `infrastructure`, not the brief's `it-infrastructure`: the seeder requires the department key, and renaming the department would be a code and data change for no gain; the HUD card shows the taxonomy label "IT infrastructure". Seeding, storage, replace, reset, delete, re-index, isolation and the HUD card reuse the marketing mechanism unchanged; no code change. Types: sections 12 and 13 domain `compliance`, 18 domain `guardrails`, all three type `policy` and the only infrastructure documents of that type; no `[HOUSE STYLE]` paragraphs exist, so nothing is `house-style`. Confidence by dominant label, volatility by the `[FAST-MOVING]` count; "Kubernetes, containers and serverless" is due for review on 2026-11-26 (the EKS 1.31 forced upgrade). Section numbers cited in a text are `cross_refs` (the Annex "sections 1 to 3" of Regulation 2024/2690 are not). Prompts: `agents/departments/infrastructure.md` and the new `infrastructure.workers.md`: consult the knowledge base first; law and compliance first, independent evidence second, anything else after (the owner's order; the source's opening section gives the label meanings and says agents must not gate a live decision on an unverified legal point, but states no precedence order itself); the section 18 summary (alone, with a named human's recorded approval, never); legal content as orientation for a human to confirm; `needs_verification` and `[GAP]` said, not guessed; section 19 for hand-offs. Approval controls (point 7): the policy gate already denies every agent AWS CLI writes, IAM, KMS, secrets, S3 deletes, Terraform/OpenTofu apply and destroy, CDK/SAM/Pulumi deploys and image pushes (decision 100's city-survival rules), which is stricter than approval; ask rules have no department scope, so spend thresholds, network and security changes through connectors, other clouds and `kubectl`, and destructive SQL stay prompt rules: the owner chose prompts only (2026-11-11), so no new gate rule was built. Download of a document is not built (marketing and sales have none). Not built (shared, every department; see decision 225): a versioned Replace that keeps the last ready version searchable while a new one indexes or after it fails, and Download; the failed-re-index test is `xfail(strict)` until it lands. Tests: `backend/tests/test_infrastructure_knowledge.py`; offline evaluation 19/19 in the top 5 (keywords only); the deploy prints the live hybrid rate. Not verified before merge: live retrieval.
227. **Data and AI knowledge base (2026-11-11).** The owner's brief: the same built-in, editable knowledge as marketing, sales and application for the Data and AI department, from `data-and-ai-research.md` only (saved unchanged at `knowledge/source/data-and-ai-research.md`, 30,357 words, last heading "Conclusion", SHA-256 pinned in a test). 22 documents: "Data and AI: About this knowledge base" (the title paragraph and the reading guide with the labels, qualifiers and caveats), one per numbered section 1 to 20, and section 21 "Data and AI: Consolidated gaps" as its own document with the conclusion kept with it, each at `knowledge/seed/data/<domain>/<slug>.md` with the text verbatim (labels, dates, links and the "checked on primary source" wording unchanged; `[GAP]` passages stay in place), adding only front matter, a "When to use this" line and the source heading. The folder is `data`, not `data-and-ai` as the brief named it: the seeder requires the folder to be the department key, and Data & AI's key in `config/departments.json` is `data`; the label and titles say "Data and AI". Seeding, storage, replace, reset, delete, re-index and isolation reuse the existing mechanism unchanged; no code change. Types: sections 6 (privacy), 16 (AI governance and law), 17 (responsible AI) and 19 (guardrails) are type `policy`, the only data documents of that type, so `entry_type: policy` retrieves exactly the legal, compliance and guardrail documents; section 19 also has its own domain `guardrails`. The source has no `[HOUSE STYLE]` material (it says so), so no document is `house-style`. Confidence from the dominant label, volatility from the `[FAST-MOVING]` count. Section 8 (Neptune and OpenSearch) is knowledge the agents consult for client and product work; it changes nothing in the city's own indexing. Prompts: `agents/departments/data.md` and the new `data.workers.md`: consult before planning, advising or acting; law and compliance first, independent evidence second, the rest after (the owner's order); section 19's three tables, with `ask_owner` before every "needs human approval" row (personal-data access or a new purpose, anonymity claims, training on personal data, model or prompt promotion, production merges, large backfills, deleting or exporting datasets, outbound sends, new tools or suppliers, access grants, LF-Tags, retention or retrieval access filters, AI feature launches, publishing numbers, committing spend) and a manager's review never replacing it; stale `[FAST-MOVING]`, `needs_verification` and `[GAP]` said plainly; legal content as orientation for a human, never final legal, tax or accounting advice; section 20 for handoffs. 235. **Knowledge versions: the old version stays active until the new one is indexed (2026-11-11).** The owner ("Yes, build it now") for the Application brief's points 3 and 9 and its test "a failed re-index leaves the old version active", for every department. Before, an edit, reset or re-index set the record to `processing` at once and search only returns `ready` records, so the entry vanished from agents while indexing and stayed gone after a failed index; parts had one fixed URI, so a re-index overwrote the old version in place. Now every new version is written under `<entry id>/v<version>/` with `version` in each part's metadata; `keep_active` snapshots the last ready version into the record's `active` before any new version (seeder create/update/re-embed/resync/revive/repair, the owner's edit, reset and re-index); `seeder.settle` (from `refresh_status` and the Knowledge list) switches only when every part is indexed, then upserts the graph (edges replaced) and deletes the previous version's parts; a refused ingest or an index failure deletes the new parts, marks the record `failed` and keeps `active`; a second edit before the first is indexed drops the unfinished version; delete removes every version. `search` reads `served(record)` (the `active` version while `processing` or `failed`) and keeps only hits of that version (parts written before this change carry no version and are accepted, so live entries need no re-index). The graph now follows the switch, not the write, so the deploy's wait (`seed_status`) is where the graph is updated. HUD: "Previous version in use" next to "Indexing" or "Not indexed". Tests: `backend/tests/test_knowledge_versions.py` (marketing, sales and application: old read until indexed, then only the new parts and edges; a failed index and a refused ingest leave the old version active and Re-index recovers; delete while indexing; a second edit), the replace tests of every department (sales, application, IT infrastructure, data and AI, finance, support, product, research, legal, media, operations) now settle before checking, their "failed re-index" tests are no longer expected failures, and one flaky audit-order assertion was made order-free within a millisecond. Not verified before merge: Bedrock returning the `version` metadata on live hits (when it does not, every hit is accepted, as before).
236. **Probe which Retrieve setting the live index rejects (2026-11-11).** Since decision 221 every filtered hybrid Retrieve comes back empty while the plain call answers. Suspected cause: Bedrock turns a string filter on OpenSearch into a term query on `<key>.keyword` (re:Post, "Field 'name.keyword' not found"), and Terraform maps `item_id`, `scope` and `kind` as plain `keyword` fields with no `.keyword` subfield, while the attributes Bedrock maps itself (`department`, ...) get `text` with one. To prove it before touching the index, `knowledge_eval` now first runs `retrieve_probe`: one marketing question through Retrieve plain, `HYBRID`, `SEMANTIC`, and with filters on `scope` (`in`, `equals`), `item_id`, `department` (`equals`, `in`) alone and with `HYBRID`/`SEMANTIC`, recording the hit count or the error of each; the deploy prints it as one line. Read-only; nothing else changes. Test: `test_retrieve_probe_reports_each_variant`.
237. **Agents use every connector and tool without approval gates (2026-11-11).** The owner: "I want agents to have unrestricted access to every connector and tool, with no approval gates. Remove or relax the existing approval requirements where possible." (Asked while the sales knowledge base was built, decision 224, and left out of it on purpose.) The builder listed every gate first, and the owner approved removing these: (1) each connector's `approval` level (`destructive` by default, `writes`, `none`; `ToolSpec.ask`, `extra_ask` in `policy.gate`, `connectors.approval_reason`, the field on new records and the Settings → Connectors control). An old record's value and an old client's `approval` on `PUT /connectors/<id>` are ignored, so existing connectors stop asking without a data change. (2) The Meta ads budget cap (`caps.ad_daily_budget_cents`, 5,000 cents, in `config/policy.json` and its check in `policy.decide`). (3) Mail for the Orchestrator and managers: every mail and calendar tool by default (`mail_level`), the owner can set a role to `none`; a `read` saved before counts as all. Mail sending and calendar actions were already allowed since decision 100; their tool descriptions and VAIC's prompt still said they waited and now say they don't. (4) Prompt rules that held work for the owner's yes: Sales outreach and proposals, Marketing deliverables, Operations automations and purchases, Legal contracts, Support replies and refunds, Engineering merges, Infrastructure applies to the owner's production, Data deletes, the "some connector calls wait" line for VAIC, the Orchestrator, managers and workers, and about 20 skills (outreach, email marketing, social publishing, invoicing and payments, refunds, Stripe going live, help center, Zapier, Meta and Google ads created paused, surveys, CRM deletes, press release, meeting actions, video, vendors, proposals). Care rules stay (opt-outs, GDPR, test mode first, never invent prices); an ad budget still comes from the owner's goal or brief, and is asked for as a question when missing. Kept on purpose: the deny rules of `config/policy.json` (the city's own AWS account, deploys, credential files, keychain, sandbox root; decision 100 and `CLAUDE.md`), which go only if the owner explicitly confirms that too; the add-connector card (where the owner signs in or gives the key); the app publish approval and app mail proposals (they hold apps, not agents); no mail for workers (decision 79, IAM); the owner's own staged settings changes; and the knowledge bases, unchanged, with their guardrail documents and the prompt lines that point to them. Some of those are legal duties (AI disclosure under the AI Act Article 50; the Swedish written confirmation for phone sales to consumers). The "ask" path in `policy.gate` and the approval plumbing stay for a rule the owner may add to the policy file later.
238. **Shared cross-department handoffs knowledge (2026-11-11).** The owner's brief: ship `cross-department-handoffs.md` (saved unchanged at `knowledge/source/cross-department-handoffs.md`, 15,708 words, last heading "Conclusion", SHA-256 pinned in a test) as built-in, editable knowledge that every department's agents can retrieve. 11 documents at `knowledge/seed/shared/<domain>/<slug>.md`: "Shared: About this knowledge base" (the title, opening summary and "How to read and split this document") and one per numbered section 1 to 10, the text verbatim (labels, dates, links, "Sourced", "Partly sourced", H markers unchanged), adding only front matter, a "When to use this" line and the source heading; the conclusion stays with section 10, "Shared: Consolidated gaps and conclusion". Types: 8 (guardrails) and 9 (Sweden and EU) `policy`; 7 (routing rule and message template) and 10 `checklist`; 1, 3, 6 `principle`; 2, 4, 5 `playbook`; 6 confidence `vendor`, the rest `inference` (the document is mostly `[INFERENCE]`). How the scope works: `shared` is a scope, not a department (`SHARED` in `entries.py`). The seeder needed no change (a folder must match its taxonomy's `department`, and `department: shared` does). `all_departments()` leaves `shared` out, so the index filter, when turned on, never filters it out of a department's search; `entry_visible` gains one condition, a `shared` record is visible to any caller that has a department (managers and workers of every department, including ones the owner builds on demand); VAIC, the Orchestrator and the executives still see no department knowledge, shared included; audience, domain, type and platform filters apply to shared documents as to any. A department caller now always fetches 30 hits (before: only departments with a seed folder), so an unseeded department still finds the shared documents. Graph neighbours of a shared hit come from the shared documents. Tests prove no department's own isolation loosened: every department pair stays mutually invisible, with and without index filters. HUD: the "Shared knowledge" card comes last under Built in, tags Built in and Shared, with the same Edit (Replace), Delete, Reset to default, View newer default and Re-index. Download is not built (no department has it; the owner said skip it for Sales). Replace uses the versioned Replace of decision 235 (the old version stays searchable until the new one is indexed; then only the new version's parts and graph edges remain). Tests: `backend/tests/test_shared_knowledge.py` (verbatim split, seeding twice changes nothing, replace leaves only the new parts and graph edges, edited and deleted documents survive a redeploy, every department retrieves shared and only its own, the eval). `_eval.yaml`: 16 handoff questions, offline 16/16 in the top 5, and 16/16 when ranked together with each seeded department's own documents. Section 8 approval controls: prompt rules until the owner picks city-wide policy rules, a department-scoped gate change or prompts only (asked). Department prompts are updated as each department's knowledge base lands. Not verified before merge: live retrieval.
239. **Shared handoff rules in every department's prompts, and the disagreements (2026-11-11).** Point 4 of the owner's cross-department brief (decision 238): every department's managers and workers use the shared document when they pass work to or request work from another department. Built as one shared text instead of twelve copies: `agents/shared/handoffs.manager.md` is appended to every manager playbook by `department_template`, `agents/shared/handoffs.worker.md` to every department's worker rules by `department_worker_rules` (`department_own_worker_rules` returns a department's own file alone), so all twelve departments, `default.md` and departments the owner builds on demand get it, and the sibling sessions' department files were not touched. Content: search the shared documents first; one accountable owner (1.3, 1.4), and a disputed or unowned item goes to the Orchestrator or the owner, not settled by the agent; the catalogue's named artefact, acceptance check, time target and the common statuses; a handoff filed to a manager is accepted (`ticket_note` "Accepted"), needs input (`ticket_note` "Input required: …") or is rejected (`ticket_fixed` "Rejected (<reason code>): …"), never assumed; one decider per decision; the routing rule (new work, priority changes, section 8 classes and anything ambiguous go manager-to-manager through `file_handoff`; workers talk across departments only inside an accepted task with its id, logged on the task); the twelve fields of section 7.2 in `file_handoff`'s `details`; section 8's class named in the handoff, holding nothing beyond the owner's rules (decision 237) and never a hold of the agent's own. No new tool or field: rejection and input-required ride on the existing ticket tools. Each department's own handoff section, playbook and worker rules are unchanged and come first; where they disagree with the shared documents, agents follow their own and say so in the handoff or report. The comparison of every department's handoff and guardrail documents and prompts with the shared sections 1 to 4, 7 and 8 found 92 disagreements (57 of them marked possible), listed in `docs/HANDOFF_DISAGREEMENTS.md` for the owner; none was resolved. The biggest: section 8's always-human list against the city's no-approval rules (decisions 100 and 237) and against each department's own guardrail tables (some stricter, some looser); Sales receiving leads already qualified and accepted without an explicit step; invoicing (Sales' Stripe invoice against Finance); metric definitions (Finance, Operations, Product against Data and AI); the approved-tool list (IT infrastructure, Media against Operations). Section 8 approval controls: after decision 237 the policy gate enforces only its deny rules (the city's own AWS, deploys and image pushes, IAM, keys and secrets, credential files, keychain, sandbox root); pay, sign, publish, production deploys of the owner's work and grant-access outside AWS are not gated; the owner's answer (2026-11-11): "For shared handoffs: the company-wide guardrails are advice only. Relax the gates, no enforced approvals." So no policy rule was added, and the prompts word section 8 as advice (decision 240). Tests: `test_every_department_s_managers_and_workers_carry_the_shared_handoff_rules` (every preset and an on-demand department, manager and worker; each department's own file stays first and unchanged). Not verified: how agents follow it in live runs.
240. **Knowledge guardrails are advice, and the owner's ad spend limit is the one gate left (2026-11-11).** The owner's scope after decision 237, relayed from the main session: remove every human-approval gate on agents in code and prompts; the approval lists in each department's guardrails section stay in the knowledge base as advice and block nothing (the company-wide shared guardrails too, which the shared handoffs session applies); remove "scrape only with the owner's yes" from Research; leave `config/policy.json` (decision 100) and the ceilings in `config/caps.json` as they are, listed for the owner to decide separately; keep knowledge scoping as it is; and build one real control. **Prompts:** each guardrail line in the department prompts (Application, Data and AI, Finance, IT infrastructure, Legal, Media, Operations, Product, Research, Customer support; manager and worker files) now says its approval list is advice: take extra care, do it when the goal needs it, log it, report it, nothing waits for the owner's yes. The "never" lists, stop-and-escalate triggers and legal duties stay (they are prohibitions, not approvals); "your approval as manager is not a human approval" and "silence is never approval" went with the approvals; the Media second approver became a second check by another worker. Marketing's and Media's money lines and the Meta and Google ads skills now point to the limit. The knowledge-base documents are unchanged. **The limit** (`backend/src/vaic/shared/ad_spend.py`): the owner sets, replaces, reads, removes and sees the history of one amount per campaign through VAIC's `ad_spend_limit` tool, only on the owner's own message, saved at once and said back (no Yes/No card: the owner's words are the instruction). It lives in the ledger at `CONFIG` / `ADLIMIT`, with one `CONFIG` / `ADLIMIT#<at>#<n>#<id>` row per change (value and date); workers can read `CONFIG` but never write it (IAM, `check-iam.mjs`). Until it is set there is no limit. `planned_spend` reads the planned total from any tool call that sets an ad budget: keys `daily_budget`, `lifetime_budget`, `total_budget`, `amount_micros`, `total_amount_micros` and their `_micros`/`_cents` variants, anywhere in the arguments, with the start and end dates; daily budget times days (rounded up), plus lifetime budgets; Meta's `daily_budget`/`lifetime_budget` are in minor units, `*_micros` in millionths; a daily budget with no end date has no total and counts as over any limit. It covers connector tools (`mcp__gateway__*`), any other MCP tool and shell calls (`Bash`, `exec`, VAIC's `run_command`) to the Meta, Google, TikTok and LinkedIn ads APIs, so any agent in any department is checked. Amounts are compared in the ad account's currency; the limit's currency is the owner's label. Enforcement is in code where spend is committed: `policy.gate` takes `spend_ask` from `ad_spend.spend_ask` (VAIC's direct path, the agents' SDK path and `gate_tools` all pass it); over the limit the call becomes an "ask" of kind `ad.spend`, a deny in the policy file still wins, and it applies to workers even with `policy_applies_to_workers` off. The approval (`kind: "ad.spend"`, action "Start this ad campaign? …" with the amount, platform and campaign) shows as a chat card (Start it / Don't start) and in Needs you; the Chief of Staff and Autopilot never decide it (`chief_of_staff._waiting`); the owner's typed yes or no goes through `ad_spend_limit` confirm/refuse with the approval's id, which calls the same `POST /approvals/<id>` the buttons do. The agent is told not to ask again and to make exactly the same call once the owner confirms; the yes is bound to that exact call (`action_hash`). Raising a running campaign's budget is the same check. Not covered: an ads API reached some other way than these tool names (a script file the shell runs, say), budgets under keys other than those above, and conversions between currencies. Kept for the owner to decide separately: the deny rules of `config/policy.json` (the city's AWS account, Terraform, deploys and image pushes, credential files, secrets) and the ceilings in `config/caps.json` (max_workers_per_goal 3, max_concurrent_workers_per_department 10, max_concurrent_workers_global 2450, max_live_workers_city 10, max_open_jobs 3, max_departments 6, worker_idle_release_minutes 15, max_new_sessions_per_second 20, turn_timeout_minutes 15, async_job_soft_limit_minutes 450, max_review_rounds 3; spend caps off).
241. **Any department hands work to any other, workers too, and the result comes straight back to whoever filed it (2026-11-11).** The owner: "IT infrastructure should be able to file tickets to marketing as well, and even customer support, and even product. And vice versa, and all different combinations. It should just be a matter of decision-making by the intelligence of each agent… resolve that issue and get back on the matter in the Jira ticket to the customer support worker who instructed this… or filed the ticket in Jira." Asked whether workers file directly or only managers: "Whichever that's most efficient. And maximizes performance." Choices: (1) **Workers file too.** `file_handoff` is in every manager's and worker's tools, with the bounds of decision 135 unchanged (a chain of at most 4 departments, never to its own department, no duplicate open title, at most 10 open per department pair), and no approval (decision 237). The filing agent's id is kept on the ticket (`filed_by`), for handoffs and for customer tickets opened with `open_ticket`. Workers filing directly saves the manager's turn and keeps the context with the agent that has it; the bounds stop runaway filing. When to file is the agent's own judgement; the shared handoffs knowledge (decisions 238, 239) is advice, and its "manager-to-manager" routing rule is now described as such in the prompts. (2) **The result goes back to the filer.** When the work is fixed, the desk's return job goes straight to the worker who filed it if it is still hired (not retired or released, and in the filing department): an assignment from its own manager (`desk_direct`, `support_ticket`), delivered on the assignment the worker filed from when it is still open (so it carries on and reports as usual), with no Orchestrator or manager turn in between. `ticket_replied` closes the ticket and that job; the worker's report closes it too if it forgot. Otherwise (a manager filed it, or the worker went home: workers are released when their assignment finishes or after 15 idle minutes) it goes to the filing department through the Orchestrator as before. Customer tickets work the same way: the reply job goes to the Customer Support worker who opened the ticket while it is still hired. In practice a worker that reported and was released gets nothing back and its manager does; one still on its assignment gets the result itself. (3) **Jira stays mirrored as it was** (`tracker_key`): the receiving team is told to update that same issue (create it only when it has no key yet), comment progress and the result there and leave it open; the filer closes it. (4) **No waiting for the beat.** `open_ticket`, `file_handoff` and `ticket_fixed` move the ticket at once (`support_desk.step_now`) instead of on the next 5-minute step; the step stays as the safety net (a finished job without `ticket_fixed`, retries, a business that was off). An atomic claim on the ticket's status (`UpdateOptions.expect`) means the step and the tool never both start a job; a job that cannot be sent puts the status back for the next step. Before, a Slack issue waited up to 5 minutes after intake, and up to 5 more after the fix, plus up to 5 more after a job ended without the tool; now intake → fix → reply runs back to back, bounded by the agents' own turns. Intake itself is unchanged: the Customer Support standing order still reads Slack every 15 minutes (its own setting, at least 5), so a new message waits up to 15 minutes before it is seen. Not changed: a finished team job that never called `ticket_fixed` still waits for the next step (hooking job completion was left out as larger). Tests: `backend/tests/test_handoff_routing.py` (an IT worker files to Marketing and gets the result on its own assignment, with the Jira issue updated by Marketing and closed by the worker; a released filer falls back to the manager; the bounds for workers and every pair; a customer ticket's reply goes to the Support worker who opened it). Not tested against Jira or Slack themselves.
242. **The scope filter on a field the index can filter (2026-11-11).** The probe of decision 236 (deploy run 43083804265, one marketing question) settled it: plain, `HYBRID` and `SEMANTIC` without a filter each returned 5 hits; `department` `equals`/`in`, alone and with `HYBRID`, returned 5; every filter on `scope` (`in`, `equals`, with any search type) and on `item_id` returned 0. Bedrock filters a string on OpenSearch with a term query on `<key>.keyword`; Terraform created the index with `item_id`, `scope` and `kind` as plain `keyword` fields, which have no such subfield, while the attributes Bedrock adds itself are mapped `text` with one. So the scope filter, part of every filtered call since v2, emptied every search; hybrid search was never the problem. A field's type cannot change in place and rebuilding the index means a new knowledge base and re-ingesting everything, so instead: every document's metadata also carries `search_scope` (same value as `scope`; owner items via `kb_metadata`, entries via `kb_entry_metadata`, the guide through `kb_metadata`), and the Retrieve filter is `notIn search_scope <hidden scopes>` (nothing when every scope is allowed). A document indexed before `search_scope` existed lacks it and passes (`must_not`), so no re-ingestion is needed and nothing is hidden during the change; its record still decides its scope, and every new or re-ingested document is filtered by the index. `RETRIEVE_ENTRY_FILTERS` is on again (the department, audience and entry filters use attributes Bedrock mapped), and `caller_filter` now keeps `shared` visible to department callers and hides it from callers without a department, as `entry_visible` does. The Terraform mapping now declares the three fields as `text` with a `keyword` subfield, for a future rebuild; `ignore_changes = [mappings]` keeps the live index untouched (no plan change). The plain-call fallback of decision 221 stays as a safety net (the owner chose to keep it after this deploy showed 0 fallbacks: "Keep it"); `PLAIN_FALLBACKS` counts its use and the deploy prints it per department next to the hit rate, and the probe now also tries the exact filters search sends. Tests: a fake index that, like the live one, matches nothing on `scope`/`item_id` and passes `notIn` on a missing attribute (old and new documents found, no fallback, on-request kept out), the counted fallback, the shared-entries filter, and the updated metadata and filter shapes. Not verified before merge: the live hit rate with no fallbacks, which the deploy prints.
243. **Operations: full access, no approvals (2026-11-11).** The owner: follow decision 237; remove every "ask the owner (`ask_owner`) before…" and approval-list instruction from the Operations prompts, including section 24's approval items, vendor contracts and spending limits; agents have full access and act without asking by default and hold back only when the owner says so in VAIC; keep the knowledge base content as reference material only. Decision 240 had already made the list advice, but the prompts still listed every approval item with "take extra care", told agents to escalate people matters to the owner, sent people matters to Legal "before a human decides", named "written approval limits" in the house style, and carried section 24's "never" list. `agents/departments/operations.md` and `operations.workers.md` now say instead: full access to every connector and tool, nothing waits for the owner's yes (messages, purchases, vendor and contractor contracts, spend, access, hiring, pay and other people steps), hold back only when the owner has told you to (in VAIC, the goal, the brief or a later message) or a call is refused, and "Operations: Guardrails for the department's AI agents" is reference material only, never a list of approvals or limits; collisions under section 3 are recorded and reported instead of escalated. The "never" list went too: most of its items are "a human decides" rules (hires, rejections, pay, warnings, dismissals), which the owner's instruction removes; Swedish and EU law still comes first through the section 3 precedence, which stays, as do the house style in the brand's own voice with no Hormozi name, likeness or figures, `needs_verification` and `[GAP]` handling, and legal content as orientation. The knowledge-base documents are unchanged. Not changed here: the shared handoffs text every department gets (asking the owner which department owns an item when the map is unclear: routing, not an approval), the ad spend limit the owner sets in VAIC (decision 240, a hold the owner says), and `config/policy.json`'s deny rules and `config/caps.json` (decision 240 left those for the owner). Test: `test_operations_knowledge.py` checks the new wording and that neither Operations file contains `ask_owner`, "ask the owner", an approval list, a written approval or spending limit, an escalation, or the "never" list.
244. **VAIC as a desktop app: Electron, loading the live HUD, released on GitHub (2026-11-11).** The owner wants VAIC on the Mac and the PC, shareable with friends, with sign-in still limited to the owner's email, password and MFA, and on the iPhone too. Electron over Tauri: Tauri would run the HUD in Safari's engine on the Mac, where voice already needed its own fixes (decision 196); Electron is Chromium on both systems, at the cost of a ~120 MB download. The app loads https://d2lcjbjaz1khxz.cloudfront.net instead of a bundled copy, so every deploy reaches it at once and Cognito (SRP sign-in, no callback), the API's allowed origin and the CSP need no change. The window is locked down in `desktop/src/policy.js` (no Node, sandbox, HUD origin only, other links to the system browser, connector sign-ins in their own window, microphone audio only for the HUD). Installers are published by `.github/workflows/desktop.yml` as GitHub Releases of this public repository (the link can be shared; the file holds no secret). Unsigned until the owner pays for an Apple Developer ID and a Windows certificate: the Mac build carries an ad-hoc signature (Open Anyway once per version, no self-update), Windows shows SmartScreen's Run anyway and updates itself. The iPhone gets Add to Home Screen through a web manifest now and the native app through TestFlight once the Apple Developer Program exists (`docs/IOS_TESTFLIGHT.md`). Pushes that touch only `desktop/` don't deploy prod.
245. **VAIC calls the owner's iPhone, all on AWS; the ad limit can be a daily ceiling (2026-11-11).** The owner asked whether Haiku VAIC could call and text them when the city needs their input and they're away from the laptop, then narrowed it: "call once, and then send message if I don't answer. But if I answer, don't send a message", e.g. when a campaign goes above a "300 kr or $30 a day" ceiling or has spent it and wants more because it performs; and "fully AWS native… all the bills be on AWS", maybe through WhatsApp. Research (session notes `vaic-phone.md`): Amazon Connect isn't in eu-north-1, needs a support case to call Sweden and a Swedish business registration for a Swedish number, and its Nova Sonic voice is Connect's own agent (no supported two-way audio to our code), so VAIC couldn't be the one on the call; AWS End User Messaging can text a Swedish number ($10 a month, 1–2 weeks' registration); WhatsApp calling through End User Messaging Social (September 2026) only relays Meta's call signalling (we would run our own WebRTC media server) and Meta requires a 2,000-a-day messaging tier for business-initiated calls; Twilio works but bills outside AWS. Chosen: the AI City app is the phone. A PushKit VoIP push through SNS (`APNS_VOIP` platform applications from the same Apple key) rings the app as a CallKit incoming call from "Vice"; answering opens the existing Nova Sonic relay with a `call` message, the relay hands Sonic a `[call]` line with the reason so Vice speaks first, and VAIC itself acts on the answer. Declined, unanswered after 30 s, or unreported after the notifier's 45 s → one APNs alert with Yes / No buttons (`VAIC_DECIDE`, Face ID) that answer the approval. Each move is a conditional write on `CALL#<id>`, so a call is messaged at most once. What rings: `approval.requested` of kind `ad.spend` (rule `vaic-prod-call-owner`, notifier timeout 90 s) and the owner's own "call me" (`call_owner` → `call.requested`); with Autopilot on nothing else waits for the owner, so nothing else calls. `call_owner: false` in Settings = the message only. The ad spend limit takes `per: day` (planned spend per day: daily budgets plus a lifetime budget spread over its days); a raise above the ceiling waits the same way. No phone number is stored anywhere. Not built: SMS or ordinary phone calls (no data connection = no call); the owner can add them later on AWS (End User Messaging, Amazon Connect). Needs from the owner: the paid Apple Developer Program key pasted in Settings → Notifications, `VAIC_APNS_ENABLED=true` and one deploy, and the new iPhone build. The voice prompt's tool name typo `ask_aic` is fixed to `ask_vaic`.
246. **Download of a knowledge document's original (2026-11-11).** Every department brief (Marketing through Media, and the shared handoffs) listed a Download action and every session skipped it; the owner: "add it". Built: `GET /knowledge/<id>/download` (the owner's items and `kn-builtin-guide`) and `GET /knowledge/entries/<id>/download` (department and shared entries), owner-only like every route, each returning `{url, file_name, expires_in: 300}`: a presigned S3 GET with `Content-Disposition: attachment`. Items: the object under `raw_key`, byte for byte (an upload not yet arrived is 404; a link is 400, it has no file; pasted text is named after its title). Entries: the version agents read (`served`), so while an edit or a reset is still indexing the previous version downloads; while that is the shipped default the seed file exactly as shipped (front matter included; a whole document byte for byte), otherwise the same seed-file form made from the record. Entry text lives in the registry, not as one S3 object, so the file is written to `downloads/entries/<id>/<sha256>.md` on each request (a lifecycle rule expires `downloads/` after a day) and that object is presigned. Five minutes: long enough to start a download, too short to pass the link around. The api role gets `s3:GetObject` on `raw/*` and the guide's PDF and `s3:PutObject`/`s3:GetObject` on `downloads/*` (applied by the deploy). HUD: Download on each item card with a file and in each entry's actions; the iPhone app has no Knowledge screen, so nothing there. Tests: `backend/tests/test_knowledge_download.py` (owner only; the seed bytes for an entry, a whole document and a shared document; an edited entry, and the seed again after a reset is indexed; a deleted entry, a deleted upload and an unfinished upload are 404; a link is 400; the guide's PDF; the five-minute attachment presign) on the in-memory fake bucket (moto is not a dependency), and `web/e2e/knowledge-download.spec.ts`. Not tested before merge: a real presigned download from the live bucket.
247. **"New version" notices: a Reload banner in the HUD, a release check in the Mac app (2026-11-11).** The owner asked how the desktop app stays up to date and approved both suggestions ("Yes, add both"). The HUD (every copy: browser, iPhone home screen, the desktop app, which loads the live site) compares the build id it loaded with `/version.json` and offers one Reload button; it never reloads on its own, so typing or talking is never cut off. The id is a hash of the built `index.html` rather than the git sha, so deploys that leave the HUD unchanged (backend, infra) show no notice. `version.json` is excluded from the immutable `s3 sync`, uploaded with `no-cache` after `index.html` and invalidated; CloudFront's CachingOptimized policy (min TTL 1 s) honours that, so no Terraform change. The Mac app is ad-hoc signed, so `electron-updater` cannot install on macOS: it checks the GitHub releases list (newest non-draft, non-prerelease `desktop-vX.Y.Z`, not `releases/latest`, which could one day be another kind of release) on start and every 24 hours and shows a native dialog with Download, opening the `.dmg` for `process.arch`; only `https://github.com` links are opened. A dialog rather than a system notification, because notifications from an unsigned app may be silently suppressed. Windows keeps `electron-updater`. The app version still comes from the workflow run number (`1.0.<run>`), so this change's own release carries the check; 1.0.6 and older need one manual download.
248. **Every tower keeps its lot for good (2026-11-12).** The owner: "if I add a new building, no other building that existed before should change its location… it's only gonna expand… if I delete a building, then that building will just be deleted. Nothing else will change. No road, nothing." Their screenshot showed why it didn't hold: `layout()` placed towers from the department count (`cols = ceil(n / 3)`), so a thirteenth department (and its demolition, kept only until the next snapshot) reflowed every tower, street and HQ. Built: the ledger numbers lots (`LOT#<slot>` items, `holder` = department key, `size` = the name the lot is sized by, "" = sized by its number; `slot` on the department record). The first time (the first snapshot after the deploy, or the first build), the standing departments get lots 0, 1, 2… oldest first, the order the City already drew them, each lot sized by its tower, so today's twelve towers keep their exact positions (checked against that day's code). After that a new department takes the first free lot (a demolished department's, or an empty one in the last row), else lot N opens a new row at the edge; a rebuilt one goes back to its old lot if it is still free. Demolishing frees the lot (the record keeps its `slot`); Start over removes the lots with everything else. The snapshot sends `lots` and each department's `slot`; `department.created` carries `slot` and `lot`. The City: four lots to a row (two on a phone), lot `s` at row `s / 4`, column `s % 4`; a lot is always the size of its size name, whoever stands on it, so a row's height never changes; how far west the town reaches is fixed by the first two rows (a later row's west lots are narrowed to fit), so HQ never moves; on a phone the homes moved from below the towers to right under HQ (once), so new rows only add at the bottom; trees are drawn on a fixed grid of spots rather than scattered over the whole map, so growing the map moves no tree. From a server without lots the City numbers them itself the same way and keeps the numbers across snapshots in that session. Rejected: keeping lots only in the browser (another device or a reload would number them differently) and a fixed number of columns chosen from today's count stored in Settings (the grid of four already matches today's twelve). The camera still re-fits the whole map when the map grows by a row. iPhone City bundle rebuilt. Tests: `web/test/stable-lots.test.ts`, `web/e2e/stable-lots.spec.ts`, `backend/tests/test_city_lots.py`.
249. **The City loads without waiting: the fixed city at once, blurred placeholders, then focus (2026-11-12).** The owner: while the city loads, show "the headquarters and the apartments being fixed and almost hard coded", the other buildings as blurry placeholders, or something more creative. Since decision 248 every tower's lot is fixed, so the City can draw the whole town before `/city` answers. Built: after each snapshot the scene remembers where the towers stand (`localStorage` `vaic.city.lots`: each standing department's name, lot and state, and the lots' sizes; no missions, people or work). Opening the City before the HUD has its snapshot calls `preview()`: HQ, the roads, the trees and the block of homes are drawn at once (they never move), and every remembered tower stands on its lot as a "ghost": its own sprite drawn small and scaled back up with smoothing (a blur that is the same in every browser and in the iPhone's WKWebView, unlike `ctx.filter`), washed grey-blue like a building in mist, breathing slowly; the HUD says "Loading the city…". When the snapshot lands, each placeholder comes into focus where it stands (a 0.7 s cross-fade, 45 ms apart in lot order) instead of rising out of the ground; a tower that was not remembered rises as before; one demolished since simply isn't there. Nothing remembered (a first visit, a new device, private browsing): the fixed city and empty lots without a FREE LOT sign, then the towers rise. Reduced motion: placeholders don't breathe and the towers swap in. The iPhone app's City calls `preview()` too, until the app passes the snapshot. Tests: `web/test/city-loading.test.ts`, `web/e2e/city-loading.spec.ts`.
250. **One square City on every screen, growing a ring at a time (2026-11-12).** The owner, with a phone screenshot of the tall two-column phone layout: "It's all just vertical. I need to align them the same way it is in the desktop… so that it can be more cohesive when I switch", and growth "like in math when you have a square where you add on the sides… 3 by 3, and then 4 by 4, and then 5 by 5", removal moving nothing but the removed building. This replaces decision 248's rows of four and the separate phone arrangement. Built: the City is a grid of blocks 12 × 16 tiles with a 3-tile road on all four sides of every block; block (c, r) has its corner at (15c, 19r) tiles, so HQ is block (0, 0) and blocks west and north of it have negative coordinates (the map now has `left` and `top`; baking, the camera, the traffic and the taxis read them). HQ stands in its block, the one block of homes in block (0, 1), and lot n is always block `blockOf(n)`: the 3 × 3 square's blocks first (nearest HQ first, clockwise from north), then each new ring's. The square has the smallest side whose n² − 2 blocks hold every numbered lot, starting at 3 × 3 (7 lots); each step adds a column and a row on two sides, east and south for an even side, west and north for an odd one, so HQ stays within half a block of the middle and nothing already built moves. A tower stands at the street edge of its block, centred, at its lot's fixed size. Every avenue runs right through the map, so every crossing is a junction (lights nearest HQ, a few roundabouts). The phone draws exactly the same map (tested: identical coordinates) and starts zoomed to about three blocks across, centred on HQ; Fit shows the whole square. The ledger shows n² − 2 lots for its first-free choice (`lots_shown`). One-time move for the owner: the twelve standing towers keep their lot numbers and sizes but move from rows of four into the 4 × 4 square (two free lots), and the phone loses its tall layout; keeping the old rows was impossible, because HQ was in the top row of a 5 × 3 block, not the middle of a square. Tests: `web/test/stable-lots.test.ts` (rings 3 → 8 never move a tower, road, home or HQ; no overlaps; phone = desktop; remove-then-add reuses the lot), `web/test/map.test.ts`, `backend/tests/test_city_lots.py`, `web/e2e/stable-lots.spec.ts`.
251. **A building's panel, redesigned; the rare cars rolled, with a Lamborghini (2026-11-12).** The owner, with a screenshot of the HQ panel: "It's too like compressed. Work in progress, parentheses, zero parentheses, and then bullet point, none right now, and then the button of zoom in… re-architect it, redesign it so it's more cohesive… a better UX and a better UI… still keep it glassy because I want to see through it". And: "just like you did with a Porsche, can you add a Lamborghini driving by as well? … Every 50 cars, randomly, like let's do like a rand 50 kind of algorithm", with four photos of a black Aventador SVJ-style car. Built: `DeptPanel` is a column: a body that scrolls (the lede, a facts box, Work in progress with a count badge, each piece a glass card with a status chip and who has it, or an "All quiet" empty state, the Apps building's list, Demolish) above a footer row with Go inside (primary) and Zoom in that is always in reach and never overlaps. Same glass as the City's other boxes (parchment tint over the town, blur and saturate, a 1px lit edge); the panel became a `div role="dialog"` (axe flagged `aside` with that role). Cars: `arrive` no longer counts every 50th (decision 191); each car that enters rolls once, `rareCar(u)` mapping one draw to "porsche" (u < 1/50), "lambo" (1/50 ≤ u < 2/50) or a cab, so each rare car is independently 1 in 50 and never both. The roll has its own seedable stream (`sim.rareRand`, from `createTraffic`'s new `rareSeed`; random per page load when none is given), so tests force either car and the traffic itself is unchanged. The Lamborghini-style car is an original pixel drawing (no logos, badges, names or plates; the photos were not copied into the repo) and, like the Porsche, runs red lights when the way is clear, never into anyone. Test hooks: `data-rare-cabs` = "all" (half each), "porsche" or "lambo"; `data-lambo-shown`; `root._carSprite` for the close-up. Tests: `web/test/rare-cab.test.ts`, `web/e2e/building-panel.spec.ts`, `web/e2e/departments.spec.ts`.
252. **New York blocks, a column or row at a time; roundabouts by place, more and bigger (2026-11-12).** The owner refined decision 250: not strictly n × n but "New York style… 3 by 4 plus 1, and then 4 by 4 plus 3… a little bit more random, but still going towards the square or rectangular form"; deleting a building must not "redesign the whole thing… adding more roundabouts and stuff"; but more roundabouts as the city grows, "maybe twice the size, just to see that the cars are going in a circle". Built: the blocks grow one column or one row at a time, in turn east, south, west and north (`gridBounds(step)`: 3 × 3, 4 × 3, 4 × 4, 5 × 4, 5 × 5, 6 × 5…), so the town is always a square or one block off one, with HQ in the middle or half a block from it; a new column or row fills nearest HQ first, so a growing town shows a partly built edge ("4 by 4 plus 3"). Lot n is still always the same block, and the ledger's `lots_shown` follows the same steps (7, 10, 14, 18, 23, 28…). Crossings: each crossing's kind now follows from its place on the grid, never from how many there are (decision 173's "lights nearest HQ, two or three roundabouts" recomputed the whole town whenever it grew): lights at the six crossings around HQ and the homes (avenues west and east of HQ's column, streets north of HQ, between HQ and the homes, and south of the homes), a roundabout where the avenue index and the street index are both 1 more than a multiple of 3 (four at the outer corners of the first 3 × 3 blocks, then more as the town spreads, never two side by side), give-way elsewhere. A crossing's id is its grid position, so its light keeps its phase as the town grows. Adding or demolishing a tower changes no road or crossing. `RING_R` doubled from 20 to 40 px and the drawn roundabout from 30 to 52 px; the traffic simulation and the commuters' detours use RING_R, so cars circle the bigger ring. With six lights on the busiest crossings, the rare cars that run a red (decision 251) could meet a cab that had just got green; a car or a commuter's cab with a green now also waits for any cab across that has committed to the crossing and is not out of it (`committedAcross`). Not fixed here: `commute-traffic.test.ts`'s "never in a junction with crossing cabs" still fails now and then (about 1 run in 10 on main before this change too), when a rare car running a red meets a commuter's cab. Tests: `web/test/stable-lots.test.ts` (3 × 3 to 8 × 8 a step at a time, nothing moves, no overlaps), `web/test/traffic.test.ts` (six lights, roundabouts only ever added, a crossing never changes), `web/e2e/traffic.spec.ts`, `backend/tests/test_city_lots.py`.
253. **VAIC and the Orchestrator read every knowledge base; they search it only when it helps (2026-11-12).** The owner, after asking whether Vice could answer "how does Alex Hormozi recommend doing Meta ads": "make sure that both the orchestrator and the vice agent has access to all knowledge bases, no matter what. But it shouldn't go through the knowledge bases if it doesn't feel like it's necessary… what's the name of the biggest or largest ocean in the world? Why should it go through marketing strategies and sales strategies". Until now `entry_visible` hid every department entry, shared included, from callers without a department, which meant VAIC, the Orchestrator and the executives. Built: `EVERY_DEPARTMENT = {"vaic", "orchestrator"}` in `search.py`; for those roles `entry_visible` and `caller_filter` let through every department's entries, the shared documents and the owner's items (vector search, the owner-item graph expansion and the entry graph neighbours alike: neighbours now come from each hit's own department, the caller's own first, then shared), a role check on the record, not a prompt. `search_knowledge` takes `department` for those two roles only (one department or `shared`; Retrieve filter `equals department`; an unknown one is refused with the list); a manager's or worker's department still comes from who calls, so departments stay isolated from each other exactly as before, and the executives and the Chief of Staff are unchanged (no department knowledge). Every entry block says which knowledge base it is from (`from="Marketing knowledge base"`, from the seed taxonomy's label). The voice path needed nothing: Nova Sonic hands each request to a normal VAIC turn (`server.py` `_voice`), which has the same tool. Prompts (`agents/vaic.md`, `agents/orchestrator.md`): search only for the owner's own material or what a department knowledge base covers (strategies, playbooks, house style, policies, benchmarks, a department's domain); never for general knowledge, chit-chat, status from the ledger or what is in context; pass `department` when obvious (Meta ads → `marketing`); say where an answer came from; describing the Hormozi playbook to the owner is fine, imitating him or using his name with customers or the public never. Tests: `backend/tests/test_knowledge_every_department.py` (VAIC and the Orchestrator see every department and shared, with a filtering index and with one that ignores filters; the department filter; Marketing and Sales still can't see each other; graph neighbours; the tool; each department's evaluation questions over all knowledge bases at once ≥ 85% in the top 5 offline; the Hormozi Meta ads question ranks the Marketing playbook first; the prompts), `backend/tests/test_shared_knowledge.py` (updated). Not tested live: the deployed index's answer to the spoken question.
254. **City ⇄ Features: the City stays alive, the pages crossfade (2026-11-12).** The owner, about the City | Features switch: going to Features should be "more of a transition into the dark theme or whatever theme there is", and coming back "it shouldn't make the city buildings like pop up again… it shouldn't be having a animation every time I do that", in either theme. Cause: `App` rendered either `CityView` or `FeaturesScreen`, so every switch unmounted the City and mounted a new scene, whose first snapshot played the towers' entrance (decision 134) and the placeholders' focus (decision 249) again, with the camera reset. Built: `CityPages` (in `Features.tsx`) keeps `CityView` mounted under the City tab once it has been shown; with Features open it gets `away`, fades out (`.city.is-away`, opacity over 260 ms, ease) and is then `hidden`, which pauses the scene's frame loop (it already stops when the root has no layout); back on the City it shows at once and fades in, and `resume()` carries on without re-fitting a camera the owner moved. Features fades in over the fading City and, on the way back, lingers 260 ms fading out under the City's fade-in, so both pages crossfade over the page's own sky in either theme, with no flash; a direct link to Features doesn't fade. Reduced motion: no transition, an instant swap. While hidden the scene's ResizeObserver ignores the zero size (on a phone it would have taken the hidden City for landscape and rebuilt the town). Coming back to the City from another screen does build a new scene, but the entrance now plays once per page load (`entrances` in `scene.js`), so the towers stand there too. Both pages' City | Features pills follow the address, so the City's (kept alive) slides with the other one. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt (it shares `scene.js`; it has no Features page). Tests: `web/e2e/city-swap.spec.ts` (City → Features → City on desktop and phone: the same element, no second entrance, no animation, the same camera; the crossfade's transition and animation; an instant swap under reduced motion; back from Files with no entrance), `web/e2e/features.spec.ts` (axe after the crossfade). Not tested: the iPhone app on a device.
255. **City is the first tab in the top bar again (2026-11-12).** The owner: "in the top bar where we have chat, work, projects, files, knowledge, skills, cost, settings, I want to add city back in the beginning. So that I can click city and get navigated to the city." This reverses 2026-11-10's "the logo is the way to the City, no City tab". The menu renders `city` first, then the other views in their order; the tab links to `#/city` and is the open one (with the sliding glass highlight) on the City and on its Features pages, since both are the `city` view; the logo still links to the City. Tests: `web/e2e/journeys.spec.ts` (the highlight slides to City from the logo and from the tab), `web/e2e/features.spec.ts` (City first and open on the City and Features), `web/e2e/phone.spec.ts` (first tab, highlight lined up after the bar has scrolled).
256. **Images in the chat are drawn as pictures, like Claude Code (2026-11-12).** The owner, after dropping a photo of a car into the chat and seeing only a chip with its name ("A9A8BF3C-3DE2-49B0-B7CD-69F… Reading the image"): "make it just like it is in Claude Code, where I can see the image but not the text about the image." Built: in the box an image attachment is a 72 px (64 on a phone) rounded thumbnail made from the local file the moment it is dropped (`keepLocal` in `web/src/lib/chatImages.ts`, an object URL, via a new `onFile` hook on `addFiles`), the upload a thin ring over a dimmed picture, a failure a red outline and "!", the × shown on hover, focus or always on touch screens; the filename is only the image's alt text and the status only a screen-reader line. Non-image files keep a compact chip with an icon and a shortened name (`shortName`). The sent owner line now stores `attachments` (`{id, title, image}`, in the chat's localStorage) so its bubble draws the thumbnails above the words and other files as chips, and `bubbleText` hides the "Attached (now in my knowledge base): …" line, which is still sent so VAIC knows to search (decision 214); with no typed words the bubble shows only the pictures. A thumbnail opens a lightbox (`ChatAttachments.tsx`: a modal dialog in a portal, Esc / × / a click outside closes it, focus returns to the thumbnail). After a reload, or on another tab, a thumbnail's picture comes from `GET /knowledge/{id}/download` (the presigned S3 GET of decision 246; an <img> ignores its attachment disposition), cached per item until a minute before it expires; a deleted item shows a plain glass tile. "Edit and send again" on a stopped message puts its attachments back in the box. Bug fixed on the way: the pasted-key guard read the whole body including the attachment line, so a photo with a UUID-like name (as iPhone and macOS name them) made the message look like a key and it was silently not sent; now only the owner's words are checked (`submit` and `sendMessage`). No backend change. Tests: `web/test/chat-images.test.ts` (image detection, the stored attachments, the split, the bubble text, short names, local then signed addresses and their refresh), `web/e2e/chat-images.spec.ts` (drop 2 images and a PDF: 2 thumbnails from local files with no filename text and 1 chip; send: 2 thumbnails in the bubble, no attachment line; reload: both from signed addresses; the lightbox by click and keyboard, Esc and ×; removal from the box; a phone width, light theme). Not tested: a real S3 presigned image in the deployed app.
257. **VAIC writes a skill in full before it asks to add it, and the owner reads it beside the chat (2026-11-12).** The owner, after VAIC staged "Add a code-animation skill for Media", took Yes and was told the skill didn't save because its files hadn't been written: "if it recognizes that I want a skill, then it should write the skill and then … ask me if I'd like to add this skill. And it should write the skill in Markdown and just like in ChatGPT or in Claude, you can see the skill to the right … and maybe have another solution for … an iPhone." A new owner tool `draft_skill` (name, description, instructions, roles?, files?) builds SKILL.md and stages `POST /skills` with the files; staging runs the upload's own `prepare` and role check plus a 48,000-byte chat limit, and stages the checked body, so Yes saves exactly the previewed SKILL.md and can't fail for missing or invalid files (`city_api` `POST /skills` takes the same check). An existing name stages "Update the skill" with Save / Not now. `vaic.question` gains `skill` {name, description, markdown, files, replacing}. The HUD: at 900 px and wider the chat narrows to the left and the SKILL.md opens on the right, rendered with the chat's own Markdown renderer (no raw HTML), with a Markdown tab, Add / Not now and ×; a newer draft replaces it; saving says "Added. It's on the Skills page" and closes it. Narrower, the card shows an excerpt and View skill opens a full-screen sheet (portalled to the page, solid sky background) with Add / Not now at the bottom. A fixed split (chat | 46 %), not a drag handle: the smallest thing that reads well at the owner's 1000 px window. `agents/vaic.md` says to write first and never ask whether to write; spoken, VAIC says the skill is on the screen. Tests: `backend/tests/test_owner_api.py` (draft staged with its files and preview; invalid name, empty or long description, reserved name, too large, bad path, hook file and bad role refused at staging; Yes saves exactly the preview; No discards; a re-draft replaces; an existing skill is updated with extra files; `city_api` `POST /skills` checked; owner only), `web/test/skill-draft.test.tsx`, `web/e2e/skill-draft.spec.ts` (side panel at 1000 px with axe, re-draft, Add, Skills page; × and View skill; phone-width sheet). Not covered: the native iPhone app, which shows no question cards (the owner answers in words there); a real model drafting a skill.
258. **VAIC talks like a 24-year-old who's always on Instagram Reels, and calls the owner "bro" (2026-11-12).** The owner: "would it be possible to make Vice sound more natural … more informal and more like a '24-year-old' a person who watches Instagram reels and speaks with the slang and all that stuff that normal '24-year-old' does? Like, 'Sup, bro,' instead of 'What's up, bro.'" This replaces the always-"Sir", Jarvis-like address of 2026-11-08 and the executive-assistant persona of the voice prompts. Built: `agents/shared/vaic_voice.md` ("## Your voice") holds the two settings in one place, the **Address** ("bro" in casual lines, a light "Sir" only where it fits, never in every sentence) and the **Slang level** (medium: everyday slang such as "sup", "lowkey", "no cap", "bet", "say less", "ngl", "fr", at most one or two per reply, never forced), with examples and the limits: slang goes around facts, never instead of them (numbers, dates, money, names, errors, what a change does, anything risky, legal or about spending stay plain and exact), yes/no questions stay unambiguous, the slang drops when the owner is stressed or something broke, no emoji, no meme spam, and only VAIC talks this way to the owner (emails, posts, customer messages and briefs keep a professional voice; the Orchestrator, managers, workers and executives are untouched). `vaic_voice()` (`backend/src/vaic/shared/utility.py`) appends it to VAIC's system prompt (`host.py`), the Nova Sonic voice prompt (`server.py`) and the GPT-Live prompt (`speech.py`). `agents/vaic.md`, `voice.md` and `voice_live.md` point at it instead of pinning "Sir", and their examples are casual. Fixed texts: the staged-change card asks "Save it?" (`SAVE_QUESTION`), workflows ask "You mean the … workflow?", "Create it?" / "Build it?", "Want me to walk you through it step by step, or just do it for you?", the ad objective question and the HUD's connected line no longer say "Sir". Tests: `backend/tests/test_vaic_voice.py` (both settings and the slang list present, facts-plain and no-emoji rules, no prompt pins "Sir" or Jarvis, team prompts have no "bro", both voice prompts carry the section and still forbid Markdown and emoji, the short questions, and a scripted VAIC turn whose system prompt holds the section and whose casual reply reaches the chat as written), plus the updated wording in `test_workflows.py`, `test_owner_api.py`, `test_runtime.py`, `test_vaic_one_turn.py`, `test_projects.py`, `test_voice_live.py`, `web/test/connect-live.test.tsx`, `web/e2e/connect.spec.ts`. Same day, the owner: "this is not just the voice. This should be any message that is being sent by any response from Vice." Typed chat replies were already covered (the section is in VAIC's own system prompt, the same for the main chat and every project chat); the fixed lines that reach the owner as Vice were made casual too: the Chief of Staff's daily digest, the executives' scheduled reviews (also now ending the last reason with a full stop), the job-finished pushes and the missed-call push. Screen texts that are not Vice talking (API errors, settings labels, the away summary panel) keep their plain wording. Tests: `test_vaic_voice.py` (the digest's wording; no "Sir" in those modules), `test_executives.py`, `test_push.py`, `test_calls.py`. Not tested: how a real Haiku 5.5 or Nova Sonic actually sounds with it (no model calls in tests); the iPhone app's demo text is unchanged.
259. **An organic town in a forest: seeded lots, roads where the town is, scattered roundabouts, trees instead of FREE LOTs (2026-11-12).** The owner, seeing the 4 × 4 square from far away: "make it more like a randomly placed city with the roundabouts as well randomly placed… not in a square… random as in it should still be compact, but randomly placed in a circle almost"; "I don't need to have the free lot visual. You can just have the trees in the way, and if there's a new building, then it will cut down the trees… if the building is being destroyed… planting new trees, and they will grow by time." Built: lot n is still always the same block (decision 250's grid), but the blocks are ranked once by their distance from HQ in blocks (measured from between HQ and the homes) plus a seeded jitter of up to 1.4 blocks (`CITY_SEED`, which must never change), so the town fills outward compact, roughly round and ragged. The seed and the ranking are code, the lot numbers are the server's, so every device draws the same town, and reloads and growth move nothing. The town is the blocks ever numbered: an empty lot is forest, never a FREE LOT sign. Roads only where the town is: a street runs along the built blocks on either side of it and on to the avenue past HQ (so every door reaches it), an avenue along the built blocks beside it; the avenue past HQ and the street between HQ and the homes run on through the countryside. Every road carries cabs; a road ending at a T fades its cabs out 72 px short of the crossing (`T_END_PX`). Crossings: lights at the six around HQ and the homes, as before; roundabouts at seeded random crossings (about one in three is a candidate, and a candidate is a roundabout unless a neighbour, diagonals included, has a lower number), so they are scattered, never next to each other, and the town gets more as it grows. A crossing's kind depends only on where it is, so a demolition changes no road or roundabout, and a new road adds crossings without changing any. A new tower's building site shows stumps and a log (trees cut down). A demolished tower's lot gets saplings that grow to full trees over `REGROW_MS` (20 minutes): the ledger's lot keeps `freed_at`, the snapshot sends it, and the City re-bakes the ground every 15 s while anything is growing; reduced motion shows the trees grown at once. The camera no longer re-fits when the map changes from a live event (only on a load, a resize or Fit), so a growing town does not jump on screen; on a phone, Fit now shows the whole town. The ledger's first-free choice is the lowest free numbered lot (the nearest HQ), else the next new one. One-time move: the owner's twelve towers take the new blocks of their lot numbers. Tests: `web/test/stable-lots.test.ts` (compact within a radius growing with the square root of the count, never a rectangle, the jitter visible; 80 towers one at a time move nothing and keep every roundabout; demolish and rebuild), `web/test/traffic.test.ts`, `web/test/map.test.ts`, `web/test/departments.test.ts`, `backend/tests/test_city_lots.py` (`freed_at`), `web/e2e/stable-lots.spec.ts`.
260. **The town feels like a city; touch navigation that works with fingers (2026-11-12).** Two of the owner's requests, both in the City scene. (1) Finishing the sentence decision 259 was cut off at: the organic town "should still feel like a city", so dense and urban with connected streets and a clear centre, not towers scattered in a forest. Every built block is now paved city ground from road to road (four greys of pavement, no trees on it), and the seeded jitter is down from 1.4 to 0.9 blocks, so the centre is solid and only the edge is ragged; the forest is only outside the town and on demolished lots (decision 259's saplings). The lot order changed with the jitter, a one-time move before the owner saw decision 259's layout settle. (2) "I can't really zoom in and zoom out with two fingers… I want to… do whatever navigation I can with just my fingers on the city." Before, a pinch zoomed around a midpoint that it re-anchored every move (so moving both fingers did not pan), lifting one finger left the map frozen until both lifted, there was no glide and no double tap. Now the canvas handles pointers itself: two fingers keep the map point that was between them between them at the zoom their spread gives (pinch and pan in one), lifting one finger carries on as a drag (never a tap), one finger drags with the map gliding on after a flick (velocity from the last 100 ms, slowing with a 320 ms time constant; none with reduced motion), a tap picks as a click does, a double tap with a finger (within 320 ms and 30 px) zooms in one step there, and a double click on a tower still flies to it. A finger's pointerdown is default-prevented, Safari's gesture events are cancelled over the City, the canvas has `touch-action: none` and no text selection or callout, and the City has `overscroll-behavior: none`, so the page never scrolls, rubber-bands or zooms instead; the glass panels and buttons on top are untouched. The wheel, a trackpad's two-finger scroll and its pinch (ctrl + wheel) zoom around the pointer as before. The camera also no longer re-fits on live changes (decision 259). Checked: `web/e2e/city-touch.spec.ts` (pinch keeps the midpoint and doubles the zoom, two fingers pan, one finger carries on, a flick glides, a double tap zooms 1.5× there, the page does not scroll, the glass + button takes a tap) and, by hand, real two-finger pinches in and out and a flick in iOS Safari on the iPhone 17 simulator, on the iPhone City bundle's page with twelve departments (zoom 0.56 → 2.01 → 0.35, page never scrolled). Not checked on a physical iPhone or a MacBook trackpad.
261. **The town grows as an oval (2026-11-12).** The owner, still seeing a square (their screenshot was an older build, with FREE LOT signs): "the city is still in a square manner, so I thought you were going to make it like a circle, but still maybe oval, and making it randomized, so… it kind of expands in a same manner as a regular city would, not in a square manner or rectangular manner." With decision 259's ranking by block distance (a block is 15 × 19 tiles), twelve towers came out as a cross of straight rows. Now the blocks are ranked by their distance in tiles from the middle of an oval whose east–west axis is `OVAL` = 1.4 times its north–south one (measured from between HQ and the homes), plus a seeded jitter of up to 0.45 of a block (down from 0.9), so the outline follows the oval and only its edge is ragged. Tried and compared as text maps before choosing: aspect 1.9–2.2 gave a two-row strip at twelve towers, 1.2 a near circle, 1.55–1.7 long straight rows; 1.4 gives rows of 1, 5, 4 and 2 towers at twelve (compact, a little wider than tall, no square) and a clear oval about 1.3 to 1.5 times as wide as it is tall from thirty on. Built blocks stay paved, the forest stays outside, roads stay only where the town is (decision 260). Fit now shows the whole town on a phone: the lowest zoom is the smaller of 0.35 and the zoom that fits the town, and Fit no longer forces 0.35. One more one-time move of the owner's towers (lot numbers and sizes kept); after it the stability rules of decision 248 hold again. Tests: `web/test/stable-lots.test.ts` now also checks that from thirty lots the town is 1.15 to 1.9 times as wide as it is tall on the map and that every corner of its bounding box is forest. Screenshots at Fit on desktop and phone (twelve and forty towers) were checked by eye. An honest limit: with twelve blocks on a grid the oval is only suggested; it reads as one from about thirty towers.
262. **Roads meet cleanly; a few footbridges (2026-11-12).** The owner circled three places (by the top-right tower, at HQ's lower-left street corner, at the right avenue) where "a road that's going across… a sidewalk" looked like a tunnel, asked for "a bridge type of design", "don't create too many bridges", "do something else with those", and not to break the traffic lights or the physics. Cause: `street()` drew its north and south sidewalk strips the whole length of the street, straight across every avenue that crossed or ended at it, so each avenue appeared to dive under the pavement. Fix: a street's sidewalk now opens over the avenue's carriageway wherever the avenue joins it from that side (`joins`: north, south or both), so a T or a crossing meets cleanly, with the zebra crossing beside it as before; this removes every such spot, marked or not. Footbridges: `footbridges(world)` puts a stone footbridge halfway along a block side, only on roads inside the town and only where the town's seed says so (about one block side in eleven), so it is never at a junction (always over 80 px from one), a bridge once there stays, and two show at today's twelve towers (sixteen at eighty). It is drawn after the cabs (deck with railings, posts, steps and its shadow on the road), so the cabs pass under it; it is not in the traffic simulation at all, so lights, roundabouts, giving way, taxis and commuters behave exactly as before. Tests: `web/test/traffic.test.ts` (bridges far from every junction, inside the town, kept as the town grows, at most a third as many as junctions; over two minutes of traffic every bridge has cabs passing under it, almost never stopped there, and every light shows green, amber and red), `web/e2e/traffic.spec.ts` (the page has bridges, none near a junction, and six lights). Close-up and whole-town screenshots checked by eye.
263. **A faster deploy: checks, images and plan side by side (2026-11-12).** The owner asked to make the deploy faster ("doing some jobs parallel if it's possible … we can't deploy the backend before the IT infrastructure"). Measured on run 43219107620 (~20 min): the Python tests took 4.5 min of the backend job, plan waited for every test, and `terraform apply` took 11 min. Apply was slow for two reasons: the AWS provider uploads a local Lambda zip under a global lock, so the ~16 functions sharing the one bundle updated one at a time (~12 s each, ~3.5 min), and the agent image was built under QEMU inside apply.
