Now: pytest runs on every core (pytest-xdist, `-n auto`; the suite was already parallel-safe, 1,699 tests in ~1 min); a quick `build` job makes the Lambda package and both image tags; `image` builds each ARM64 image natively on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` and pushes it to ECR only when its tag is new (with a Buildx layer cache); `plan` runs right after `build`, beside web, backend and image; `apply` needs all five, so nothing in AWS changes unless every check passed, and still applies the saved plan exactly. The Lambda bundle goes to a versioned S3 bucket (`vaic-prod-lambda-code-<account>`, S3-managed encryption: the code is this public repository) and every function points at that object version, so they update in parallel. Terraform still runs `scripts/push-image.sh` before the runtimes, finds the image and moves on; only when an image is missing (a first deploy, before the ECR repositories exist) does apply set up QEMU and build it there. Terraform providers are cached between runs. The first parallel run caught one timing bug, in the MCP host (`services/mcp-host/server.py`): after a server answered and exited, the host could hand the retry to the still-exiting process; a process whose output has closed now counts as stopped. It also showed the runtimes waiting ~2 minutes behind three CloudFront distributions that changed on every deploy: they asked for TLSv1.2_2021, which CloudFront ignores with its default certificate (it keeps TLSv1), so Terraform saw a difference each time. They now state TLSv1, what is really in place; nothing changes for viewers. The order the owner asked for holds: infrastructure, then the runtimes, then the Lambdas that call them, then the HUD and the knowledge seed.

264. **The owner's car sees a commuter's cab before running a red (2026-11-12).** `web/test/commute-traffic.test.ts` ("a commuter's cab … never in a junction with crossing cabs") failed about 5 runs in 25 from decision 262 on and once blocked a deploy (run 43230276376). Not a random town or a test too strict: the town and traffic are seeded, only the rare-car roll is not (`createTraffic`'s `rareSeed`, decision 251), and every failure was the owner's Porsche or Lamborghini running a red at a light while the commuter's cab drove through on green. The rare car's check (`approaching` or `inBox` across) counted only the cruising cabs ahead of the box; a commuter's cab past its stop line but not yet in the box was invisible, so the rare car committed, and the commuter's cab, past its line, no longer stopped. Now `guestComing` (`web/src/city/city-state.js`) treats a commuter's cab on the crossing road within `JUNCTION_HALF + 100` px of the centre (enough for a boosted cab that can no longer stop) as coming, both for a cruising rare car and for a rare commuter's ride (another commuter's cab, not itself). Tested: the failing rare seeds (2, 3, 6, 22, 23, 25, 34 of 1–60) all pass; the test 50 runs in a row with the random roll; a new test (a rare car one pixel from committing, red for it, a commuter's cab 40 px short of the centre across) fails on the old code and passes now; `npm test` all green; the iPhone City bundle rebuilt. Not checked by eye in the browser.

265. **VAIC asks before it uses a connector in the owner's chat, and browses live with AgentCore Browser (2026-11-12).** The owner, with two Claude desktop screenshots: the "Claude wants to use Transcribe Audio or Video from Descript ▾" card ("can we implement this to Vice? … all connectors"), with "if I am explicit when I say don't use any connectors… then he should just start doing it without any questions asked"; and Claude's "Used the browser (18 actions) 1m 21s" with three thumbnails and a large view of the page being browsed. **The card** (`runtime/connector_permission.py`): on a VAIC turn whose input is the owner's own message, every connector tool is wrapped; a call to one the owner hasn't always-allowed is kept in the ledger (`VAIC_CONN_ASK`, one per conversation, a newer one replaces it) and shown as a `vaic.question` with `permission` (tool title from the MCP `title` or its name, the service, the inputs, the description, the parameter names, the schema, and `choices`: the other connected services whose tool has the same first word or mostly the same words). The answer is the owner's next message, tapped, typed or spoken, like decision 206's Yes / No, through `connector_permission` (allow_once, always, decline, and the service picked in the list): allowed, the kept call passes the policy gate and the ad spend limit under its own name and runs, and VAIC gets its result in the same step; always adds the tool to the connector's `always_allow` (editable with `PUT /connectors/<id>`, shown in Settings → Connectors as "Vice uses without asking" with × to take one back); another service in the list is allowed for that turn and VAIC calls it with that tool's inputs; decline settles the card and leaves that service out for 30 minutes, and VAIC does the job another way. It refuses in the turn that asked, so the model can't answer for the owner. **"No connectors"**: an owner message saying not to use connectors ("without any connectors", "don't use connectors") or a given one ("don't use Descript") takes those tools out of VAIC's turns in that conversation for 30 minutes (`VAIC_NO_CONN`); "use connectors again" or naming the service without a no puts them back. Since the tools are gone, VAIC can't ask; its prompt says to work another way, mostly the browser. **Kept as decided before**: decision 237 holds for agents (the Orchestrator, managers and workers never ask) and for VAIC's turns that aren't the owner's message (reports, results); this card is for VAIC's own calls in the owner's live chat. Defaults are the owner's: ask per connector tool, Always allow remembered. **The browser** (`runtime/browser.py`): VAIC had none (the workers' `mcp__agentcore__browser` name and IAM existed, but nothing served it). Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Browser is offered in eu-north-1 (AWS's region list for AgentCore; the workers' IAM already named `aws.browser.v1` there) and costs about $0.0895 per vCPU-hour and $0.00945 per GB-hour of active use, idle free (secondary sources, to check against the bill): a minute or two of browsing is well under a cent. `browse` starts one session per VAIC turn (stopped when the turn ends, 15-minute timeout), connects to its automation stream with a SigV4-signed WebSocket and drives the page over the Chrome DevTools Protocol with the `websockets` library already in the image (no Playwright or Chromium in our container). Steps, up to 10 per call and 60 per turn: open, click, click_text, type, key, scroll, back, wait, read, find, transcript (YouTube's own transcript panel, every line with its time), look and frames (screenshots at chosen seconds of the page's video, for a Twitch VOD or a video without a transcript); look and frames hand the screenshots to Haiku as images (`ToolReply`, sent as Converse image blocks). On a Google consent page it picks Reject all. It never signs in: typing into a password or one-time-code field is refused, and only public http(s) addresses open (no private, link-local or internal hosts, no user name or password in the link); page text reaches VAIC as outside content. It passes the policy gate as `mcp__city__browse`. After each step a JPEG goes to the files bucket under `browser/<conversation>/<message>/` (a lifecycle rule deletes it after a day; VAIC's role may write only there, `check-iam.mjs`) and a `vaic.browser` event carries the step's words, the address, a presigned link (one hour) and where the pointer clicked. The chat draws it under the owner's message: "Using the browser (N actions) 20s" with a large live view (address, current step, the pointer, a button to open it larger) while it works, then "Used the browser (N actions) 1m 21s ›" with the last three thumbnails and, opened, every step with its screenshot; any screenshot opens a larger viewer with ← →. The last eight runs are kept in the browser's storage; an expired link says so. IAM: VAIC's role gets Start/Stop/Get browser session and ConnectBrowserAutomationStream on `aws.browser.v1`, and Put/Get on `browser/*` of the files bucket. Not done: the iPhone app doesn't draw `vaic.question` cards in the main chat, so on the phone the owner sees VAIC's question and answers in words or by voice, which takes the same path; the browser isn't tested against the live AgentCore Browser or YouTube from here (the first real run is the check), and YouTube may change its transcript panel.

266. **Skills and knowledge are for the departments the owner picks, with Vice's recommendation picked first (2026-11-12).** The owner: when Vice writes a skill and asks to add it, "also ask me to which departments … and have the recommended departments", since "a Python skill — why should that be applied to finance or marketing?"; the same for documents attached in the chat and added as knowledge, answered by chips, "all", "all except two of them", or by voice, in plain words for a non-technical owner. Built: (1) `backend/src/vaic/shared/dept_pick.py`: the choices (the twelve pre-configured departments, then the owner's own), `parse` (the owner's words → keys, in code, so the selection never depends on the model: all / all except / just / also / not / none, longest names first, two-letter names such as IT only in capitals so "add it for finance" is Finance), `recommend` (a fallback from the words, never more than 4) and `check`. (2) Skills: a row's `departments` (empty = every department) is enforced in `runtime/skills.py` `for_agent` when a manager's or worker's turn loads its skills; VAIC and the Orchestrator keep all. `POST /skills`, `PUT /skills/<name>` and `POST /skills/catalog/<name>` take `departments` ("all" or names). (3) Knowledge: an owner's item may carry `departments`; `entry_visible` and the always-on block keep it to those departments (VAIC and the Orchestrator see everything, decision 253; the built-in department knowledge bases are unchanged); `PUT /knowledge/<id> {departments}`; no re-index (the record check decides; the item's index metadata has no department, so the Retrieve filter never hides it). (4) VAIC: `draft_skill` and `find_skill` take `departments: [{name, why}]`; a new `add_as_knowledge` stages a knowledge card for an attached document; `choose_departments` changes the pick from words; `save_change` reads the departments from the owner's own message (`TurnCtx.owner_text`) and refuses an empty pick. `vaic.question` gains `departments` {choices, recommended, selected} and `knowledge` {id, title, status}; a new `vaic.departments` event moves the chips. `GET /departments/choices` lists the chips for the pages. (5) HUD: `DeptPicker` chips (recommended marked, with why; All departments, Clear; "Pick at least one department") on the chat card; on the skill side panel a one-line "For … · Change" so the skill keeps the room; Add sends "Add for Data & AI and Finance" or "Add for all departments"; a knowledge card says "Adding it as knowledge for …, ready in a minute or two", then "Ready. … can use it now." on `knowledge.updated`. The Knowledge and Skills pages show "For …" with Change. Why the pick travels as words: the tap and the voice answer take one path, and the tap can't drift from what the city reads. Not built (suggested in `docs/KNOWLEDGE.md` §18): attachments are still readable by every department until the owner picks. Tests: `backend/tests/test_dept_pick.py` (51: the words, the recommendation, scoping a skill and a document for manager, worker, executive, VAIC and the Orchestrator, the always block, the API, the cards end to end with a tap, words and "all"), `web/test/dept-pick.test.ts`, `web/e2e/dept-picker.spec.ts` (the panel and card pre-picked, axe, All / Clear, a spoken pick, Add's words, a phone knowledge card through Ready, the Knowledge and Skills pages); screenshots looked at in light and dark, phone and desktop. Not covered: the iPhone app (no cards; words work the same), a real model choosing departments.

267. **Cabs come from the map's edge; every road ends properly; roundabouts only on four-way crossings (2026-11-12).** The owner, after adding and demolishing two test departments ("Tttt", "Oooo"), circled a roundabout between Customer Support, Application and Finance sitting in a "+"-shaped hole, road ends "blending into the forest", and paving past a road's end, and asked: cars "should spawn from the edges of the map… where the roads actually start… and never disappear inside the road or fade in or fade out". Causes: the paved blocks were drawn after the roundabouts, cutting the ring into a cross; a roundabout could sit on a T or a corner, its paint reaching into the forest; a street ending at an avenue drew its carriageway across the avenue's outer pavement; a street at the town's west or east edge ran on to the map's border through the forest (and lost that stretch once the town grew past it); and the cabs on a road ending at a T faded out 72 px short of it (decision 259) and re-appeared at its other end. Now: only the street between HQ and the homes and the avenue past HQ run off the map; every other road ends at a crossing (`arms` on each junction), with the avenue's pavement drawn across the street's end. Stop lines, give-way teeth and lights are only drawn on arms that exist. At a T where the street runs on, the avenue that ends there gives way. A roundabout needs four arms (a seeded T gives way until the town grows its fourth road, then becomes the roundabout for good; roads only grow). Blocks are paved before the roads, so the ring rounds their corners, and trees keep off it. The traffic (`createTraffic`) starts every cab beyond the edge, queued on the four lanes that come in from it, and the scene runs it for `WARM_MS` (90 s) first so the town opens busy. A cab at the end of its road turns onto the other road there, once there is room, giving way or on its green. Elsewhere it turns off with odds `TURN_ODDS` (0.3), so roads that start at a T get cabs; a left turn waits in the junction for a gap and goes on the red. A cab that drives off past the edge comes back in later on an entry road. The corner is eased over 350 ms in the drawing (`turn`). Cabs fade only on the stretch at the map's faded border. When the roads change, `carryTraffic` keeps every cab where it is on the lane through the same spot instead of starting the traffic afresh. Checked: a three-minute run at 4, 12 and 30 towers in which every cab's first and last sight and every faded frame is out in the border and none moves more than 12 px a step; every lane ends at the edge or a crossing; the gridlock runs (no cab still for 45 s, no crossing cabs in one junction); the carry-over keeps positions; Playwright screenshots of 12 towers after building and demolishing two, zoomed out and close up at a roundabout, a T and a corner. The first push failed its deploy on `commute-traffic.test.ts` (2 seeds in 60): a cab rounded a corner into a junction just as a commuter's cab drove into it. A turning cab now also waits while a commuter's cab is moving in or near the junction (one waiting at its own line doesn't count, or the two would wait for each other); all 300 seeds tried pass. `traffic.spec.ts` now waits for the first roundabout, since a town without its towers yet has no four-way crossing. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

268. **Download a department's whole knowledge as one zip (2026-11-12).** The owner, with a screenshot of the Knowledge page: "Why is there no download button on application knowledge, data and AI knowledge, finance knowledge, etc.? Only for AI City guide. I want there to be a download button under for all of them." Built: Download under the description of every department card under Built in, and of Shared, styled and placed like the AI City guide card's. `GET /knowledge/departments/<key>/download` (owner-only like every route; 404 for an unknown department) returns `{url, file_name, expires_in: 300, entries}`, a presigned GET of `<Department> knowledge.zip`: one `.md` per entry that is not deleted or retired, the same bytes as that entry's own Download (`entry_bytes`, the version agents read, so the owner's edits are in and deleted entries out), named by title (`Title (2).md` on a clash), plus a `README.md` listing file, title and entry id. The zip is written entry by entry into a spooled temporary file (memory to 16 MB, then `/tmp`) and uploaded from it with boto3's managed upload (`Blobs.put_file`, multipart when large) to `downloads/departments/<key>/<random>.zip` (the existing lifecycle rule expires it after a day). The api role gets `s3:AbortMultipartUpload` on `downloads/*` beside the existing put and get. Found on the way: decision 246 added no API Gateway routes, so `GET /knowledge/<id>/download` and `GET /knowledge/entries/<id>/download` (two path segments after the ones routed) were answered 404 by API Gateway before reaching the Lambda; the three download routes are now in the route list and `tests/infra.test.ts` checks them. Per-entry downloads are unchanged. Tests: `backend/tests/test_knowledge_download.py` (the zip holds exactly the active entries' seed bytes; an edited entry's new text and a deleted entry left out, matching the single download; Shared; owner only; unknown department 404; title clashes; the S3 upload from a file) and `web/e2e/knowledge-download.spec.ts` (every department card shows Download and saves `<Department> knowledge.zip`). Not tested before merge: a real zip from the live bucket.

269. **Centre lines that meet at every turn; footbridges drawn as bridges (2026-11-12).** The owner, after decision 267: "the lines don't connect, like the yellow lines in the middle of the streets", with some places "bugged out"; the main session saw the line stop short at every corner of the outer roads, odd stubs by the junctions, and the two footbridges reading as stripes standing in the road. Cause: each road drew its own dashes from its own start, straight across every junction it crossed (the street's line ran through crossings the avenue's line stopped at), and nothing joined two roads at a corner. Now `centreLines(world)` works the lines out once for the map: one run per stretch of road between junctions, ending at the junction's edge (a roundabout's ring); the road that runs on through a T keeps its line through it; and at a corner (two roads, one each way) a quarter circle of radius `BEND_R` (24 px, half a road) from the end of one road's line to the end of the other's. The scene paints whole dashes centred in each run, kept 12 px clear of a crossing's zebra and stop lines, starting right at a bend, and the bend as one curved stroke. Footbridges: a pale walkway between dark parapets with a lit top edge, pillars at the ends, a crisp shadow cast down-right across the carriageway, and a landing with steps on each pavement. Checked: `web/test/road-lines.test.ts` (4 to 60 towers: every corner's bend meets both lines, no line through a crossing but the through road of a T, every line end at a junction's edge, a bend or off the map); close-up screenshots of every junction type (corner, T with lights and giving way, crossroads, roundabout) and both footbridges in the 12-tower town. Also, the owner: "there are way too many cabs right now… I want 75% of what we have": the cruising cabs are now `CAB_KEEP` (0.75) of decision 191's count, rounded; commuters' rides, the called taxis and the rare cars' 1-in-50 odds are unchanged. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

270. **Knowledge: the owner picks the departments before a document is stored; the page filters by name, department and built in (2026-11-12).** (1) "when I tried to attach a new markdown … it didn't allow me to select a department … it said for all departments … I want to be selective sometimes … and if I don't specify anything, then just deploy for all." Before anything is stored, a dropped or chosen file (several share one choice), a folder, a pasted link or text shows "Adding …" with decision 266's chips, nothing ticked; the departments it seems to be for are marked Recommended from the file names and the start of small text files (`POST /departments/recommend`, decision 266's `recommend`) with "Use recommended"; none ticked says "No departments chosen: every department can use it" and adds for every department; ticking some limits it to exactly those. `POST /knowledge/uploads`, `/links` and `/text` take `departments` so the item is stored for them from the start, never visible to all for a moment. (2) "these filters … country or region, year, topic, this just bad … searching it by name … filter it by department as well. And also if it's built in. That's it." The three menus and the grouping switch are replaced by Search (word starts, any case; department cards match by name or entry titles), Department (what it can use, every-department documents and Shared included) and Built in (All, Built in, Mine), remembered per device; the place fields stay in the backend for agents and the place groups stay. Found on the way: `GET /departments/choices` (decision 266) was not routed by API Gateway either, so the chips never loaded and every document only said "For every department" with no Change, likely why the owner saw no choice; it is routed now and `test_every_route_the_api_answers_is_routed_by_api_gateway` checks every `ROUTES` entry against `infra/modules/api/main.tf`. Tests: `backend/tests/test_knowledge_add_departments.py` (Finance from the start; none, "all" and [] are every department; link and text; a bad value stores nothing; the recommendation), `web/e2e/knowledge-add-for.spec.ts` (drop → Recommended Finance unticked → Use recommended → `departments: ["finance"]` and "For Finance"; two files with none ticked → "all"; pasted text, Cancel, Legal; search by word start, department-card matches by entry, Department and Built in filters, remembered after a reload; phone width), and the knowledge specs updated for the extra step. Not tested before merge: the live pages after deploy.

271. **A New York close-up: towers rise over the street behind; every tower shows the tools it can use (2026-11-12).** The owner: "can you make sure to not have like fully 2D city? … one building before the street, and then the street is behind it. And then a building behind that street. So that the first building is covering up … some part of the street … so that there is no just a gray background … I want it to be like New York City", and "each building that has an MCP [should] showcase that logo in the same theme and style … if we have Jira for all departments, Jira's logo should be on the building. Very small, but subtle and still visible." Depth: `towerSize` now gives a lot 10 or 11 tiles wide (was 8 to 11) and 18 or 19 deep (was 11 to 15), so it runs over the 3-tile street behind its 16-tile block but never past it (no two lots overlap; at street level a tower still stands on its own block, which `map.test.ts` now checks). The tower picture has `SPRITE_PAD` (48 px) of sky above its lot, its body reaching about 40 px over the top of its block (12 px lower on every other tower, by name, so the skyline stays uneven) and its tiers broad (`base = W - 16`, gentler set-backs), so the block shows little bare paving. The bake draws towers back to front; the scene draws the part of each tower above its block again after the cabs and people (`drawTowerTops`), with its lit windows and beacon, so whoever is on the street behind passes behind it. Footbridges now cross only avenues (1 in 5 block sides, was 1 in 11 of every road), since one over a street would land on the back of the tower in front. Plaques (decision 173): the snapshot and `connector.updated` now carry a connector's `roles`. A tool hangs on a tower when that department's team (manager or worker) may use it and it is for that department: `every_department` (Jira) on every tower, the departments Haiku picked on theirs, and a tool whose departments were never worked out on every tower. HQ shows the tools VAIC or the Orchestrator may use, in a row on its plaza (`data-hq-signs`). A tool that stops being ready (switched off, signed out, removed) takes its plaque down, as before. Checked: vitest (sizes, lots never overlapping at street level, the plaque rules with Jira on every tower, a VAIC-only tool on HQ only, a role change arriving live), `test_connector_signs.py`, the Playwright suite, and screenshots of the 12-tower town zoomed out and close up (towers over the street behind with cabs passing behind them, Finance's Jira and Stripe plaques, HQ's three, an avenue footbridge with a cab under it). Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

272. **More elegant roundabouts (2026-11-12).** The owner: "please make the roundabouts more elegant with some details like some edges on the street walk, like on the sidewalk around the roundabouts, and maybe something in the middle like a statue or something … it's just so bad with the UI and the UX". Now `roundabout()` in `scene.js` draws, around the same ring road (cabs still circle at `RING_R` 40, drawn out to 52): a pavement ring with an ink edge, paving joints and a lit kerb, joining each street's pavement, with the kerb rounding each corner between two streets; a dashed lane line round the ring; give-way teeth at each entry; and a raised island (a stone kerb, grass, a ring of flowers, a shadow on the road) with a monument picked by the junction's id: a green-bronze statue on a plinth, a fountain with a spray, an obelisk with a gilded tip, or a clock on a column, each with a soft shadow. Trees keep off the ring and its pavement. Checked: close-ups of both roundabouts in the 12-tower town (a fountain and a statue); the traffic tests and the Playwright traffic and commute specs unchanged. The City canvas has no light or dark variant (the HUD's theme doesn't change it), so one look covers both. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

273. **The owner's Manhattan references are the City's base point; the redesign, in steps (2026-11-12).** The owner sent six photos (`docs/city-references/`, small copies, with what to take from each) and asked: "very tight buildings into each other, very futuristic … a base point for my AI city … every single one of my buildings have a street separating each other. Maybe don't have that … more like New York City … Manhattan … most compact in the middle of the city, and then slowly getting less and less compact … placeholder houses, outside the town … they have to take the cab to the town". The plan, each step deployed and checked on screen against the references: (1) these references and the elegant roundabouts (decision 272); (2) the towers rebuilt in three original pixel styles echoing the photos (a glass curtain wall, brick with fire escapes and a water tower, limestone with setbacks and an arched base) and a few lit signs in the centre, every department's tower keeping its door, manager spot, roof counter, sign and plaques; (3) blocks of two to four lots side by side with narrow alleys instead of a street round every tower, neighbour buildings filling the town tallest and tightest around HQ and lower outward, block widths seeded so the streets are irregular: a one-time move, then nothing moves again and a demolished tower leaves trees; (4) a suburb of small houses along the country roads, where some residents live and ride a cab in (the commute and taxi code), counted with the people at home (decision 213).

274. **Towers in three Manhattan styles (2026-11-12).** Step 2 of decision 273. `FACADES` now carries a `style`, picked by the department's name as before. **Stone:** steps back like a wedding cake, with pilasters, set-back ledges, an art-deco crown, a spire and a stone arch over the entrance. **Brick:** rises straight to a cornice, with brick texture, sash windows with pale lintels, black fire escapes on the right bay (a landing and railing every other floor, ladders zig-zagging between), and a roof house with a wooden water tower on legs under a conical lid. **Glass:** a sheer curtain wall in a grid of mullions and spandrels, two diagonal bands of sky light across it, a louvred crown and a 22 px mast. A glass tower's lit windows are whole panels; a brick one's skip the fire-escape bay. Towers within a block of HQ carry a lit screen of coloured pixels down their left side: no words, no brands. The beacon sits on the spire or mast; brick has none. Since a tower's upper floors reach over the street behind (decision 271), the part drawn again after the people now stops at that street's far kerb, so the next tower's door, its manager and whoever stands there are never covered. Checked: close-ups of every style in the 12-tower town (towers over the street behind, people at the doors visible), zoomed out, vitest and the Playwright City specs. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

275. **Manhattan blocks: buildings shoulder to shoulder, densest in the middle (2026-11-12).** Step 3 of decision 273. The owner: "every single one of my buildings have a street separating each other. Maybe don't have that … still having the buildings maybe tighter to each other … most compact in the middle of the city, and then slowly getting less and less compact". **Blocks.** A block column is now `lotsAcross(c)` lots of `LOT_W` (12) tiles: 2 or 3, seeded by the column. The HQ column always has 2 (HQ and the homes in the west lot, the avenue past HQ at `AVENUE_X` = 24). `colX`/`colW` place the columns, so the avenues run straight at uneven spacing. Rows are as before. **Lots.** Each block still holds one department lot (`deptLot(c, r)`, seeded), so lot n is still block `blockOf(n)` in the same oval order (by `blockScore`, now in real tiles). The other lots hold `neighbours`: buildings that are nobody's department. They are drawn with the same three styles, with no name, some windows lit, and now and then a lit screen in the middle. They are tall (18–19 tiles, over the street behind like the towers) within 2.2 block heights of the middle, mid-rise (14–16) to 3.4, and low-rise (9–11) beyond, where 2 lots in 5 past 4.2 are left to the trees. Every built lot is paved; an empty department lot is forest as before. **Stability.** The change is a one-time move of every tower and road. A neighbour is fixed by its place, so once there it stays as the town grows, and a demolished department's lot goes back to trees among its neighbours. Junction ids keep their column numbers (`avenueCol`), so the lights and roundabouts stay where their columns are. **Also.** The zoom-out floor on a phone is measured against the area the panels leave free. Checked: vitest, including a new test with 60 departments one at a time (no building overlaps another or a road at street level; no neighbour ever moves; tall in the middle, low at the edge); the whole Playwright suite; and screenshots of the 12-tower town zoomed out on a desktop and a phone, compared with the references (towers packed in rows with alleys, glass beside brick and stone, tallest round HQ). Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

276. **A suburb of houses outside the town (2026-11-12).** Step 4 of decision 273. The owner: "you could add some placeholder houses, outside the town … all people, they don't have to live inside the apartment complex. They could also live in the houses outside the town, but they have to take the cab to the town." `layout` adds `homes.houses`: `HOUSES` (6) houses of `HOUSE_W` × `HOUSE_H` tiles, on the north side of the street between HQ and the homes, alternating west and east of the town, `HOUSE_PITCH` apart, starting 4 tiles past its edge. `homeOf` sends every fourth resident (slot % 4 = 3) to a house and the rest to the apartment buildings as before. Since that street runs on off the map (decision 267), a resident walks out of their door to the kerb and rides a cab into town (`rideLegs`). `taxiCall` now lets a called taxi start out of town on that street. The scene treats the houses as homes like the flats (`homesOf`): a lit window for whoever is home, the number on the roof, the people-at-home count (decision 213). It draws each house as an original pixel house: clapboard in one of four colours, a pitched roof, a chimney, two windows, a front door with a porch light, a lawn and a picket fence. **The one exception to "nothing moves":** the houses keep just outside the town, so they move out with its edge when the town grows a column wider. Fixed places would either make the map so big that the town looks small, or put the town on top of them later. Checked: vitest (residents split 12 to each of the three apartment buildings and 2 to each house; the taxi call), the Playwright suite, and screenshots of the houses on both sides of the 12-tower town. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

277. **Department badges are counted; Download on every document and every file (2026-11-12).** The owner: "if I create a knowledge base all departments are going to use, I don't want you to list all of them … just say five departments … the number … in the form of an integer and not in text", and "if someone clicks show entries, or show more … the user can see which departments use this … every single file should be downloadable, even in Files. Also in Knowledge." Built: the department badge on each of the owner's Knowledge documents is the department's name for one, "N departments" in digits for more, and every department counted the same way ("13 departments", the number of departments the chips list); the names are its tooltip and accessible label. The "For …" line counts too, with "Show which" listing the names; Change still opens the chips. The Shared card's badge counts every department; opening a department card ("Show entries") says "Used by Marketing.", the Shared card names every department. Download: uploads keep it from the moment the file has arrived (while being read too); links gain "Open link" and, once read, Download of the saved page text (`normalized_keys` joined, the site profile left out, written to `downloads/items/<id>/` and presigned for 5 minutes; the api role gets `s3:GetObject` on `normalized/*`, which also covers the guide's PDF); every file in the Files grid and list gets Download, fetching a fresh presigned address on click. Tests: `backend/tests/test_knowledge_download.py` (a link's page parts joined without the profile, 400 before it is read; an upload downloads while still being read), `web/test/dept-pick.test.ts` (1, 2, 3 and every department; the names), `web/e2e/download-everything.spec.ts` (badges for 1, 3 and every department, Show which, opened department and Shared cards; an upload's Download; a link's Open link and page download; every file's Download in grid and list saving the same bytes), and the Files and Shared specs updated. Not tested before merge: the live pages after deploy.

278. **The app is shown live while the builders work, with pointers where they edit (2026-11-12).** The owner: "whenever I am seeing new features, like the front end, and when the app was developing, I want to see it in real time to the right, where it says your app appears here as soon as its first version is ready … how the AI agents are working on it, with pointers, with indicators on where they're editing." A builder's file changes go out as a private working copy of the app, not a version: the worker runtime scans its `app/` folder about once a second after a file tool call the gate allowed (`on_tool_allowed`, new on `EngineRequest`) and at the end of its turn, puts the changed files in its own `hires/<hire>/app/` prefix (the one `submit_app` already writes, so workers get no new permission) and puts `app.preview.files`; the apps Lambda (its EventBridge rule now also takes that type) copies just those files to `drafts/prv-<32 hex>/` on the apps origin, one copy per job (`PREVIEW#<task_id>` in `APPDRAFTS`, within its existing permissions), adds the bridge and `vaic-live.js`, and puts `app.preview`. Why this shape: the apps origin's sandbox, CSP and the bridge's "preview has no data" already make a draft safe to frame, and `drafts/` already expires; keeping the copy there needs no new IAM, bucket or signed-URL key group. Owner-only means what draft previews already mean: an unguessable address handed only to the owner's HUD over the owner's event stream (a CloudFront signed URL would need a key pair and a key group for little gain on a page with no data). Where they edit: a plain selector read from what the edit itself wrote (no diffing), drawn inside the frame by `vaic-live.js` (pointer and pulse; scroll kept in `window.name`, so the HUD adds no message listener), and a corner badge naming the file otherwise; above the frame, who is editing which file, linked to the build log. Throttled to one scan and at most one event a second per builder, changed files only. When the job ends the studio shows the version as before; `app.published`/`app.updated` put the copy away. Checked: `test_app_preview.py` (a write produces `app.preview.files` with the changed path only once `index.html` exists, a burst shares one event, files outside `app/` and other tools send nothing, a failed upload never fails the turn; the Lambda stages only changed, allowed files under a 128-bit address, never a draft, version, approval or `live/` file, refuses non-workers and the city's own file names), the gate hook in `test_gateway.py`, `web/test/livePreview.test.ts`, and Playwright (the frame appears on the first update, reloads on the next keeping its scroll, the builder's pointer inside the frame and "now editing app/…" above it, "In the log", then the published version). Not checked: a real build on AWS (CloudFront's one-second minimum cache on `no-cache` objects may delay a reload by a second).

279. **Only departments' towers; windows lit only for work; a much bigger world; smooth road lines (2026-11-12).** The owner, seeing decisions 274 to 276 live: "smooth out the edges of the road where there are the lines, the yellow lines", "make the entire map larger so I can scroll and drag much further", "remove all buildings that are not a department, the larger buildings only", and "some windows are lit up. That should only be the case if there are people working inside … they're working on a project inside that department". **Buildings:** `layout` no longer makes neighbours (decision 275's filler buildings); a block's other lots are trees. Blocks keep their two or three lots, so no tower moved. **Windows:** `workingIn(state)` lists, per department, the agents working in its building right now: workers in `working` or `reporting` (as "Agents working now" counts them) and the manager while a ticket of theirs is `in_progress`, each with its current ticket's title. A tower lights exactly that many windows (before, it lit one for everyone inside, idle managers included). Hovering a lit tower gives a tooltip such as "Marketing: 2 working on …". The apartment buildings lost their few always-lit windows, so a home lights only for whoever is in. HQ keeps its lit windows: it's VAIC's. **World:** `WORLD_MARGIN` = 200 tiles past the town on every side (`MAP_MARGIN`, 48, stays the baked picture's margin). One picture that big would exceed a phone's canvas limit, so the far countryside is drawn in 512 px tiles as they come into view (a few a frame, 90 kept), or zoomed out below 0.6 from one quarter-scale picture of the whole world with simpler trees. It holds the same fixed tree grid, and the street past the homes and the avenue past HQ run on to the edge, which now fades live (`worldEdge`). The camera pans out to that edge and zooms out to see most of it; Fit still fits the town. Cabs come in from the new edge, drive at `FAR_PX_S` (220 px/s) beyond `MAP_MARGIN` so they reach town soon, and their number still comes from the roads out to `MAP_MARGIN`. **Lines:** each dash has soft end pixels. A line that runs off the map keeps a fixed 20 px beat, so the town's line and the far road's carry on without a seam. Where two roads meet, the pavement's corner between them curves with a radius of 8 px; at a corner where both roads end, the outside of the turn is rounded to follow the centre line's bend. Checked: vitest (no lit windows with nobody working; one per working agent in their department only; dark again when the work ends; no neighbours), a new `web/e2e/city-world.spec.ts` (only the four departments' towers, `data-lit` empty, the camera dragged past the old edge), the rest of the Playwright suite, and screenshots of corners, crossings, the far forest and the avenue running on out of town. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

280. **Knowledge upload as a visual, numbered flow: files inside the drop zone, departments right under it (2026-11-12).** The owner, with a screenshot of the live page after dropping a long-named Markdown file (the zone stayed empty and an "Adding …" card appeared below the paste box): "I want the file added as part of the drop box, and I can still upload more files … more visual, better UX … idiot-proof … almost like kids are using it, so it's easy to follow where everything goes." Built: ① the files sit inside the drop zone as tiles (a big picture by kind, the name shortened in the middle keeping its extension with the full name on hover, the size, a 44 px ✕), a "+ Add more" tile (its own chooser), and the zone still takes drops ("3 files ready · drop more here"); ② right under it and joined to it, "Pick who can use them" with big 48 px chips, a picture per department (`DEPT_ICON`), recommended ones outlined in amber, chosen ones filled with ✓, "Use recommended", and "None picked: every department can use them." (nothing picked is still every department); ③ one big button that says exactly what happens (`addLabel`: "Add 3 files for Marketing and Media", "Add 1 file for every department", "Add the link for Legal") and "Remove all". After Add, the tray empties and the new rows slide into the list with an amber glow for about four seconds, the first scrolled into view (reduced motion: no movement, a steady outline). One choice covers all the files: a per-file choice was optional and left out to keep it simple. Pasted links and text keep ② and ③ under the paste box. Phone: two tiles per row, full-width buttons, no sideways scroll. Tests: `web/test/dept-pick.test.ts` (the button's words for 1 and several files, a link and text, every department; long names shortened), `web/e2e/knowledge-tray.spec.ts` (drop 2 → 2 tiles; + Add more → 3; ✕ → 2; step ② joined under the zone and above the paste box; recommended Marketing; pick Marketing and Media → "Add 2 files for Marketing and Media"; uploads carry both; the new rows glow, then calm; axe; phone width and 44 px targets) and the knowledge specs updated for the new words. Not tested before merge: the live page after deploy.

281. **A project's chat is one timeline, oldest first, and follows the newest line (2026-11-12).** The owner's project chat showed his latest message and VAIC's reply at the top, with the builders' steps below them: the steps were drawn after every message (`BuildLog` after `chat.map`). The owner: "Both those messages should be under the 'Searched your knowledge' etc.", "As soon as I write something I should be navigated down", and "while the response is generated word by word, I should always stay on the bottom line". Now `timeline()` (`web/src/lib/buildSteps.ts`) merges the chat lines and the build steps by their time on this device, oldest first, a message before a step on the same millisecond, steps in a row kept as one run that still folds by kind; the turning ring and VAIC's thinking dots stay last; "In the log" goes to the last run. Scrolling is one hook, `useStickToBottom` (`web/src/lib/stickToBottom.tsx`), lifted from the Chat screen, which had the same logic at 120 px: the view sticks to the bottom while anything grows (a MutationObserver and a ResizeObserver tick it down for every new row) only while the owner is within 80 px of the bottom. Scrolled up, nothing moves him and "↓ New messages" shows (sticky at the bottom of the chat). A jump key brings the view down smoothly, and a smooth scroll still under way doesn't count as him reading. In a project the jump is his own message only, so a reply that starts while he reads doesn't pull him down. The Chat screen keeps its rule from 2026-11-11: his message and a new VAIC reply both bring it down. It now also shows the button. Checked: `web/test/timeline.test.ts`, and Playwright (one timeline in order: hello, reply, steps, his question, the reply below the steps; sending from above scrolls down; a 24-line streamed reply keeps the bottom visible after every chunk; scrolled up, a new step and reply leave him where he is and the button takes him down), the Chat screen journeys, the whole suite. Not checked: a real iPhone browser.

282. **The departments packed round HQ in rings; green round every tower (2026-11-12).** The owner, after decision 279: "I don't like how the buildings are distributed … more compressed, an X times X layout. The middle should be the most compact, and going out along the radius it gets less compact, but the buildings should still be really tight together", and "around the buildings there's some grey stuff. Remove that. It should only be grey in front of the building … It should always be green around." **Layout:** every lot of a block is now a department's (before, one per block). `lotOf(slot)` gives lot n as [block column, block row, lot]. Blocks are taken in order of `ringScore`, the 4-norm distance in tiles from the middle of town (between HQ and the homes, on the street between them) to the block's nearest edge, so the 2-lot and 3-lot blocks either side of HQ count the same. Each block is filled lot by lot, nearest first, before the next starts, with no seeded jitter. HQ and the homes keep their own lots; the free lot beside each is a department's. With 12 departments that is two rows of three full blocks round HQ. The outline is a ring of whole blocks at a time, so it swings either side of square (wide-to-tall between 0.8 and 2.4) and stays compact. `blockOf` remains for the drawing. A one-time move: from now on a new department takes the next free lot in this order, a removed one leaves its lot to trees until the next newcomer, and nothing else moves. **Ground:** the paved pad under each lot is gone (decision 259's `CITY_GROUND`); grass runs up to the walls, and the only pavement is the strip in front of the door with its lamps (`towerPlot`). Checked: vitest (ring order, each block filled before the next, no nearer block left out, about square, plus the stability tests); Playwright, including a pixel check that the ground beside every tower is grass and in front of its door is pavement (`_groundAt`); and screenshots of the 12-department town zoomed out and close up. Two traffic tests now expect at least three roundabouts in a big town, since a compact one has fewer crossings. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

283. **A goal's team is sized from its parts, in code (2026-11-12).** The owner: "As soon as I say build or work on a project, the Orchestrator should know how many agents it needs to do this as fast as possible, but still with quality… not just 1,000 agents by default… I can be specific about how many agents I want." A change to the animal memory game ("4 pairs") had a crowd of workers and no parts done after 6 minutes. Until now the count was the model's guess, bounded only by the caps of decision 88 (3 per goal, 10 per department, 10 at work in the city): the prompts said "default one worker, never more than three", nothing computed it, and nothing was recorded. Decided: (1) a manager calls `plan_team` with the goal's parts, each `tiny` or `normal` with the parts it waits for; `team_size.size_team` lays them out in waves and the team is the widest wave of normal parts (a wave of only tiny parts is one worker; tiny parts beside a real part ride with its worker), capped at `max_workers_per_goal` and `max_concurrent_workers_per_department`. A chain of dependent parts is one worker. (2) `hire_worker` hires up to the planned number; with no plan the first hire records "1 worker by default" and the second is refused until `plan_team` sizes it. (3) The owner's number (VAIC's `workers`, carried by the Orchestrator onto the goal) is used exactly with no plan, capped at the department ceiling (before, it bypassed that ceiling; it still may go past the city-wide count of workers at work); the reason says so, and the ceiling when it bit. (4) Calling `plan_team` again grows the team only by new independent parts (no `after`), never shrinks it, and logs the growth. (5) Every decision is a history line on the goal and on the job ("Team for Application: 3 workers: UI, game logic and tests run in parallel"), kept as `team_size` / `team_reason` / `team_parts` on the goal and `team_lines` on the job; `get_status` returns the running jobs' lines as `teams`, and VAIC tells the owner in one line. The Orchestrator no longer guesses sizes. Tests: `test_team_size.py` (tiny edit 1, three independent parts 3, a chain 1, tiny parts folded, owner override, caps, bad plans; the planner records and logs the reason, the hire cap follows it, mid-build growth, no-plan default), the updated end-to-end hierarchy and owner-count tests, and every backend, web and root check. Not checked live: the deployed managers on Fable actually calling `plan_team` and splitting parts well.

284. **A hire in about 12 seconds; "Agents working now" is who is inside a tower; no count over the homes (2026-11-12).** Three of the owner's requests. (1) "As soon as I say to build a project and it hires people into the taxi, that whole visual, from the building, into the taxi, into the workplace … should only take about 10 seconds, 15 at most." A hire now carries a deadline, `HIRE_MS` (12 s), from the moment it is placed. Hires at the same door leave `HIRE_STAGGER_MS` (0.15 s) apart instead of 0.35 s. The called taxi drives at the hire's pace and, after `CALL_PATIENCE_MS` (3.5 s) of queueing, pulls round the traffic to the kerb. During the ride the cab's pace is raised to whatever the ride left needs to reach the door `HIRE_WALK_IN_MS` (1.2 s) before the deadline. In the last `HIRE_RUSH_MS` (2.5 s) it no longer waits at a line or gives way, so a red light can't make it late. (2) "When an agent enters a building, that's when it starts working. So 'Agents working now' should be exactly as many as are inside a building." The scene counts the agents inside the departments' towers each frame and publishes the total (`onWorking`, `data-at-work`). The City screen's top bar shows that number rather than the backend's count of workers on a job: it rises as each one walks in, falls as each walks out, and never counts one waiting at home or riding. A tower's lit windows are one per agent inside, so the count, the lit windows and the tower's own count always agree. Decision 279's `workingIn` remains for the tooltip's "on what". A manager stands beside the door, outside, so a tower with nobody hired is still dark. The iPhone app's native City screen (`ios/AICity/City/CityState.swift`) still shows the backend's count of workers on a job, so it can lead the picture by a ride; the City picture inside the app follows the new rule. (3) The number over the homes is gone (the owner: the agents at home are effectively unlimited, so the count is pointless); their lit windows stay. A reload still places everyone at once. Checked: a new `web/e2e/hire-time.spec.ts`: ten hires into the furthest of twelve towers were all inside after 12.7 s, the published count equalled the people inside at every 100 ms sample and only rose, the top bar said 10, and when the job ended it fell to 0. Also vitest, the rest of the Playwright suite, and `city-world.spec.ts`, which now checks the count is 0 with nobody inside. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

285. **VAIC's automatic chat updates are short, plain and id-free; a review needs a real reason (2026-11-12).** The owner got the Chief of Staff's digest as one paragraph of semicolons and arrows with raw worker ids, text cut mid-word ("is r", "calls loa"), "the only gap is ," and reasons that only said FAIL, and asked for idiot-proof updates that never show a worker id. `digest_text` now writes a headline, at most five bullets grouped by job (review failures folded into one line per job, counted per worker by its newest decision; other calls deduplicated by their answer), and a link to Work → Decided for you; the chat's Markdown now opens `#/…` HUD links in place (http(s) only otherwise). `chat_text.clip` cuts at a sentence end in the second half, else at a word, dropping a dangling small word or an unclosed bracket or quote. One sanitiser, `chat_text.no_ids`, sits where chat text leaves the backend: `Ledger.add_conversation_line` for VAIC's lines, the `vaic.reply` event model (every bus and Lambda validates it) and `LiveReply` (the WebSocket shortcut). Worker ids become "a/an <Department> worker", manager ids "the <Department> manager", any other `<prefix>-<12 hex>` id is dropped; text inside Markdown links, URLs and code is untouched so an app link still works. Why the reasons said FAIL: they were not empty. The manager's `review` tool takes `reasons` as a free string of at least 3 characters, and managers passed the verdict word itself ("FAIL"), so the worker was told "Fix exactly this: FAIL" with nothing to fix and failed again until the review limit; that loop is a likely part of why the memory-game build ran 18 minutes and more. `review` now refuses a FAIL whose reason is only FAIL/failed/none ("say exactly what is wrong"), and the owner's question names the worker by specialty with the real reason, or "It didn't pass review." Checked: `backend/tests/test_chat_text.py` (the screenshot rewritten as a snapshot, no ids, no FAIL, no empty fields, no mid-word cuts, the five-bullet cap, grouping, the sanitiser on all three output paths, the refused bare FAIL) and `web/test/markdown.test.tsx`. Not checked: a real digest in prod (it runs daily at 17:00 UTC), VAIC's own model-written replies beyond the id sanitiser.

286. **The town grows like a real one round HQ, never a rectangle (2026-11-12).** The owner, after decision 282: "The city is too rectangular. I said random, but compact in the middle. They shouldn't be in a rectangle, but not spread apart either. Just place them randomly while still compact around the middle." Decision 282's ring order gave a neat strip of full blocks, and an empty block with a loop road where a freed lot had been numbered. **Growth:** `blockOrder()` now grows the town a block at a time from HQ's block and the homes'. The next block is drawn by a seeded random number from every block touching the town so far, each weighted 2^(−`ringScore`/6), so a block one 6-tile step further from the middle is half as likely. The town stays densest round HQ while its edge pushes out on any side. Each block is filled lot by lot, nearest the middle first, before the next starts. The lot beside HQ and the one beside the homes come first. The seed is `vaic-city-1:grow4`, picked from a handful tried because the town starts as a 2 × 2 round HQ rather than a line (the first try grew straight up and down HQ's column). **Roads:** a block now gets roads only while a tower stands on it, so a block whose lots are all free (freed, or numbered but empty) is plain forest. The map's extent follows the same blocks. **Stability:** a one-time move. The order is fixed by the seed, so a new department takes the next lot in it, a removed one leaves its lot to the next newcomer, and nothing else moves. Checked: vitest (the order is the same every time; a block is filled before the next; at 13, 30 and 60 departments every block touches another, the bounding box has blocks left out, and the mean distance from the middle stays within 1.6 times the best possible; plus the stability tests), the Playwright suite, and a screenshot of the 13-department town: blocks stick out above, left, right and below HQ. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

287. **Every build runs the fastest way by default, fixed in code (2026-11-12).** The owner: "If I'm building an app, it should go the fastest way possible, by any means necessary: add more agents, change settings so agents work faster, whatever it takes. That should be the default and set in stone. Simple app or complex app, it doesn't matter." The memory game's "change to 4 pairs" ran more than 18 minutes with about 20 workers, several failing review 3 or 4 times with the reason just "FAIL". No live traces were readable from the session that made this change (no AWS credentials), so the time breakdown is from the code: (a) team size was a model guess (fixed by decision 283); (b) a bare "FAIL" gave the worker nothing to fix (refused since decision 285's change), and each round costs a worker turn plus a manager review turn on Fable; (c) after 3 FAILs the part waited on a question that the Chief of Staff answers with "Try once more", the 4th round; (d) nothing noticed a stuck part except the 15-minute idle release. Decided: (1) `vaic.shared.speed` holds the pace: `FASTEST` is `DEFAULT_PACE`, and only the owner's own words ("take your time", "no rush", "take as long as") make a task `unhurried`; "use 5 agents" still wins for the team size (decision 283). (2) The pace goes VAIC → task → every goal (with a pace line in the manager's brief) → every part. (3) At the fastest pace a part's second FAIL cancels it for that worker (who goes home and frees the place) and returns every reason to the manager, who re-assigns it to a fresh worker or splits it; no owner question. Every FAIL's fix message carries the earlier reasons too. Unhurried keeps the 3-round limit and the question. (4) The 5-minute sweep reports a worker's part that has run past its budget (10 minutes fastest, 30 unhurried) to its manager once, with what to do; the sweep stops nothing itself. (5) The Orchestrator and manager prompts say speed is the default: every goal and part at once, review the moment a part reports. Unchanged: review on every part, the policy gate, the hard caps, the ad spend limit, and the pinned models (a stronger model for a failing part would break the model rules, so the part goes to the Fable manager to re-plan instead). Tests: `test_speed.py` (fastest is the default and only the owner's words change it; the reason reaches the worker; escalation after 2 FAILs with no owner question; unhurried keeps the longer loop; the Orchestrator passes the pace; the time budget reports once), the updated review and sweep tests, and every backend, web and root check. Not checked live: real build timings after this change.

288. **No limit on agents (2026-11-12).** The owner: "No limit on agents, absolutely no limit. If it needs 100 agents to build and deploy faster, go ahead. If it needs 1,000, be my guest. As long as it builds the fastest way possible, I'm happy." This reverses the agent-count ceilings of decisions 88 and 98 and the ceilings in decision 283. Decided: (1) `max_workers_per_goal`, `max_concurrent_workers_per_department`, `max_concurrent_workers_global` and `max_live_workers_city` are removed from `config/caps.json`, the settings, the Settings screen and VAIC's settings list (a value still stored in prod is ignored: nothing reads those keys). (2) `team_size.size_team` has no ceiling: the team is the widest wave of parallel parts, up to 1,000 parts per plan, so a tiny change still gets one worker and 500 parallel parts get 500; the owner's number is used exactly. `hire_worker` hires up to the goal's planned team, the only remaining check (more workers than parallel parts would be slower, not faster). (3) The dispatcher still counts workers for "workers busy" but holds none back. AWS is the limit now: when starting a turn is throttled (`ThrottlingException`, `ServiceQuotaExceededException`, `TooManyRequestsException`, "Rate exceeded"), the message is re-queued with full-jitter exponential back-off (2 s doubling to at most 2 minutes) instead of failing; other errors go back to SQS as before. Model throttling inside a turn keeps its jittered back-off (5 tries). (4) Unchanged, on purpose: the policy gate on every tool call, the security blocks, secrets handling, the owner's ad spend limit, optional spend caps, `max_open_jobs`, `max_departments` and `max_new_sessions_per_second` (20, under AgentCore's 25 a second). The quotas that now cap parallelism, from the repository (not read from the account): AgentCore active session workloads 2,500 per account in Stockholm (5,000 in us-east-1/us-west-2); AgentCore new sessions 25 a second; AgentCore 2 vCPU / 8 GB per session and 8 h per async job; Bedrock tokens and requests per minute per model and profile (Opus 5.5 for workers, Fable 5.1 for managers; the account's values were not readable here); Lambda 1,000 concurrent executions per region, with the dispatcher reserved at 200 and the worker queue's SQS maximum concurrency at 50 (batches of 10). Tests: `test_team_size.py` (500 parallel parts give 500 workers, through the planner too), `test_hire_caps.py` rewritten (the planned team is the only limit; 150 on one goal all hired), `test_dispatch_throttle.py`, the 500-worker dispatch test now with an AWS quota of 150 that throttles and every worker still starting, and every backend, web and root check. Not checked live: real throttling from AgentCore or Bedrock, and how many workers actually run at once on the account.
289. **The project board and a new model map, stage 1 of 4 (2026-11-12).** The owner approved a re-architecture of how AI City builds, exactly as his final diagram, and said to start migrating now: "make sure the UI is not being bugged out by this migration… from now on only think in performance optimization." Target: VAIC (Haiku 5.5) sizes the job; a shared **project board** (no model) holds the owner's words, the spec, the parts and live status and replaces the VAIC → Orchestrator → Manager → Worker message relay; four sizes chosen by VAIC, the owner's words always winning (small: one Sonnet 5.5 worker, no planner; medium: a Sonnet 5.5 planner; large: an Opus 5.5 planner; extreme, a whole app from business requirements with AWS infrastructure: a Fable 5.1 architect handing parallel specialist leads to departments); workers in parallel with no cap, each owning its own files, on Sonnet 5.5 (Haiku 5.5 for trivial parts, moved up to Sonnet after one failure), planners dispatching each part as it is written; tests (no model) first, then review by Opus 5.5 (Fable 5.1 on extreme; on small jobs only when risky; every part on extreme, with security and cost checks on the AWS setup); a part still failing after 2 tries is taken over by Opus 5.5 (Fable 5.1 on extreme), every FAIL keeping its real reasons (decision 287); a whole-app check on medium and larger jobs; departments stay (every part is tagged with one, its workers are hired into that building and read only its knowledge plus shared, and the old managers become the departments' reviewers). **This changes the pinned model rules** of `CLAUDE.md`, decisions 129, 287 and 288 (workers pinned to Opus 5.5, managers to Fable 5.1): the owner approved the new map, which lives in `config/models.json` → `build` and is read only by `vaic.shared.build_models`. Inference: the global cross-region profiles (`profilePrefix`); Bedrock's latency-optimised inference is not used, because as far as this repository knows it is not offered for these models in eu-north-1 (not verified on the account; no AWS access from the session). Migration in four stages, each its own commit and deploy, the old chain the fallback behind one switch until the new path has passed for every size. **Stage 1 (this change):** (1) `vaic.shared.board`: one board per job in the ledger table (`BOARD#<task>`: `META`, a `PART#<ticket>` per goal and part, and one `MARK#…` item per timing mark, written once, so parallel events never race on one item). It is a projection of the city's events, folded in by the ledger writer Lambda that already receives every event, so nothing in the chain waits on it; out-of-order and repeated events change nothing. Child tickets now carry `task_id`, so the board needs no walk up the tree. (2) Timings for every stage of a build, worked out on read: VAIC's hand-over (its turn start to the job), sizing, planning (the job to the first part handed to a worker), workers starting (a part handed over to its worker's turn picking it up), work, review, publishing (submit to staged) and the total, as wall-clock spans plus time summed over parts; tests have no separate stage in the old chain (workers run them inside their turn). `GET /jobs/{id}/board` returns the board. (3) VAIC's sizing in code (`vaic.shared.sizing`, microseconds): the owner's words win ("go big", "use 10 agents", "just one worker", "make it extreme"); otherwise an existing app's change is small, a new app medium, a new app with many features large, a whole app with its AWS infrastructure or from business requirements extreme, and anything unclear starts small. The size, its reason and whether the owner set it go on the job and the board; stage 1 still runs every size through the chain. (4) Instant status: a plain status question ("status?", "how's it going?", "is the memory game done?") is answered in the API Lambda from the board with no model call and no runtime, as the same `vaic.reply` event and chat lines VAIC's turn would write, including where the time went for a finished job ("It took 2 min 41 s: planning 40 s, …"). Anything else, a job it can't find, or a chat with no job at all goes to VAIC's model as before. (5) Terraform: the ledger writer may read (`GetItem`, `Query`) for the board; workers and managers may invoke every model of the build map (IAM only; nothing calls them yet); the new route. Unchanged: the UI and every API shape (the ticket event gains optional fields only), the chain, the policy gate, the security blocks and the ad spend limit. Tests: `test_board.py` (the whole chain lands on the board with stage timings; events shuffled and repeated; spans and busy time; which questions skip the model; the API answers without queueing a turn and never twice), `test_sizing.py` (signals, the owner's override words, VAIC's count, the model map, Haiku-to-Sonnet and takeover, which parts are reviewed), and every backend, web, root and Playwright check. Not checked live: the board filling in prod, real timings, and whether Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5 are invocable on the account's global profiles today (VAIC ran on Sonnet 5.5 early on, decision 10).

290. **Roundabouts never run under a tower; buildings stand in front of a footbridge's ends (2026-11-12).** The owner, with a screenshot of the roundabout by Customer Support: "fix this bad UI bug, it's the building's edge going into the roundabout". Customer Support's corner, entrance and manager stood inside the ring's pavement, and the brick tower below started inside the ring. The cause: decision 252 doubled the roundabout to a 40 px ring, and decision 272 drew its pavement out to 63 px, round a crossing whose roads are 48 px wide. So every roundabout reached about 40 px into the corners of the four blocks round it, where towers stand since the blocks were packed (decisions 275, 282). Now: `RING_R` is 24 px (where the cabs circle). The ring road runs from the island (14) to 32 px, its kerbed pavement to 38, and the monument is drawn at two thirds. `RING_FOOT` (40 px) is kept clear of buildings. With the smaller ring, a tower centred on its lot already ends at least 40 px from it, unless it is as wide as its lot (11 tiles). The owner pointed out that only the blue Customer Support tower was wrong and the brick one below was fine, so `lotShift` moves just such a tower one tile to the far side of its lot, and only on a corner by a place that `junctionKind` makes a roundabout. Every other tower stays where it was. Because that depends only on the place, no tower moves when the crossing later becomes a roundabout, and a roundabout never appears where a tower already stands. **Footbridges:** the owner, "The bridge should not be in front of the building. The building should be in front of the bridge, so the building covers part of the bridge." A bridge is drawn after the cabs, so they pass under its deck, which also put its landings over the buildings beside it (in the owner's town, a brick apartment building by the homes). Now, after each bridge, the part of any tower within its reach is drawn again from the tower's picture with its live windows, doors and plaques, and the walls of an apartment building or house are copied back from the town's picture, so the building covers the landing (`coverBridgeEnds`). Lamps, signs and plaques are part of a tower's own drawing, trees are drawn before the buildings, and the traffic lights stand in the road, so nothing else is drawn over a building by mistake. The traffic is unchanged apart from the ring's size. Gridlock runs at 12, 30 and 60 towers are clean, and a ring still holds three cabs at a time. Checked: vitest (from 1 to 40 departments, with gaps demolished and refilled, no tower, HQ or home within 40 px of a roundabout or of any place that may become one), a new `web/e2e/city-rings.spec.ts` (48 pixels round the ring by Customer Support in the owner's 13-department town are all road, kerb or pavement; the bridges' ends are covered by the buildings beside them), the Playwright suite, and screenshots zoomed out and close up. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.

291. **Every lamppost stands in front of the buildings (2026-11-12).** The owner, the opposite of the footbridge rule (decision 290): "Same goes for all the lamps. The lamppost's actual lamp is behind the building. They should all be in front of the building, no matter where they are." The lamp beside the blue Data & AI tower's entrance was hidden. Lamps were drawn with each tower's pavement before its body, so a tower drawn later, such as the one in front whose crown reaches back over the street (decision 271), could cover them. Now `lamp()` waits in a queue while the town's picture is drawn, and every lamppost goes up last: by each tower's door, round HQ's plaza, and anywhere else. Pole, head and glow are all over every building, while footbridges stay behind them. Checked: a new test in `web/e2e/city-rings.spec.ts` that every lamp head in the owner's 13-department town is lit on both sides (`_lamps`, `_groundAt`). In that test town no lamp happened to be covered even in the old order, so the test guards the rule rather than reproducing the owner's case. The close-up of Data & AI's entrance shows both lamps in front. Also the vitest and Playwright suites. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.
292. **Small jobs run on the project board, stage 2 of decision 289 (2026-11-12).** Live by default for small jobs; every other size still runs through the Orchestrator chain. (1) VAIC's `delegate_to_orchestrator`, once the job is written and sized small, puts it on the board when the department is clear (an app change goes to the app's home department, Application; another job to the department the Haiku triage named) and nothing needs the chain (file references, a new-department choice): `board_path.start_small` writes the department's goal and one part (`path: board` on the job, goal and part; `task_id` on both), hires one worker on the size's model (Sonnet 5.5) straight into the department through the in-process Factory (`agent.hired`, so the City's taxi takes it to the tower as before), gives it the part and starts its turn. No Orchestrator turn and no manager planning turn. The part's message names its model; the runtime calls that model through its system inference profile (`models.resolve_build_model`), so the City and the turn records show Sonnet. (2) Tests first, in code, in the worker's own runtime when it calls `report_to_manager` (`vaic.shared.part_checks`, no model): an app has `index.html` and every local file its pages load exists, JSON loads, JavaScript parses where Node is installed (the runtime image has none, and adding it would make every new session's image bigger, so the check is skipped there), `npm test` runs when there is a test script; an app part must also have a staged draft (the apps Lambda's checks count as tests; it waits up to 30 s for staging). A failure goes straight back to the worker in the same turn with the output. (3) Then review only when risky (decision 289's small rule): the goal or the owner's words touch money, logins, secrets, deletion, infrastructure, deploys, data, email, legal or prices, or nothing automated could check the part. The department's manager reviews it on Opus 5.5 for that turn (the message names the model), with a review-only brief. (4) Otherwise the part is finished in code: the worker sends a `board: finish` message to its manager's queue (the only queue a worker may use, so its IAM is unchanged) and the dispatcher Lambda runs it as a code step, never a model turn, once (`board:<id>` claim). It writes what the chain's sign-off wrote: the part, the goal and the job close (the worker goes home), `review.result` PASS, `signoff.result`, the app's publish approval, `result`, and a report to VAIC, which tells the owner in its own words as before. (5) Two failures, tests or review together, every reason kept, hand the part to a fresh worker on Opus 5.5 (`take_over`; decision 287's rule that every FAIL carries its real reasons holds). (6) The single switch: setting `board_sizes`, default `["small"]`; `[]` sends every job through the chain. (7) Timing marks for the tests and the report (`build.stage` events) join the board's stages. The UI is unchanged: the job, goal and part are the same ticket records and the same events, so City, Work, Projects, the live preview, the project chat and Needs you read them as before. Tests: `test_board_small.py` (a small job on the board with one Sonnet worker and no planning turn, the same records and events, timings with tests; a risky one gets the Opus review; tests failing twice and review failing twice each hand the part to an Opus worker with every reason; the switch; medium still on the chain; a board step runs once, in code), `test_board_fixtures.py` records the real event streams of a small change on the board, the same change on the chain and a medium build into `web/e2e/fixtures/`, and `web/e2e/board-path.spec.ts` replays them: the hire rides into the Application tower, "Agents working now" equals who is inside and falls to 0, the live preview names who edits what, the project chat is one timeline ending with VAIC's reply, and no id reaches the chat. Before/after screenshots of City and Projects (chain vs board) look the same apart from the team size. Every backend, web, root and Playwright check. Not checked live: a real small job in prod and its timings (the owner's "change the memory game to 4 pairs" is the first measurement), Sonnet 5.5 on the account's global profile, and VAIC's model wording after the new "Started: one worker…" tool answer. Seen and left as it was: in both paths the app's publish approval is withdrawn as the job closes (`move` withdraws a finished ticket's asks); not changed here.
293. **The iPhone app follows decision 288 (2026-11-12).** The iPhone Settings screen still showed and sent `max_concurrent_workers_per_department` and `max_concurrent_workers_global`, which the backend no longer reads. Decided: both fields are removed from `SettingsForm` (read and PUT body), the demo data and the test; the section is renamed "Work at once" and keeps only "New workers per second (max 25)" (`max_new_sessions_per_second`), with the web's note as its footer: "No limit on agents: each build gets as many workers as its parts can run at the same time." The iPhone app still has no fields for `max_open_jobs` or `max_departments` (it never had; left as is). Tests: `HudStoreTests.testSettingsFormMatchesTheAPI` now checks the PUT body sends new workers per second and neither removed key; all 164 iOS tests pass on the iPhone 18 Pro simulator. Not checked: the screen on a real device.
294. **Every job size runs on the project board, stage 3 of decision 289 (2026-11-12).** The owner's go, through the main session: start stage 3 now and go straight on to stage 4. (1) **Planners.** VAIC's `delegate_to_orchestrator` puts a medium, large or extreme job on the board (`board_path.start_planned`): one planner turn on the Orchestrator's runtime with the size's model from the build map (Sonnet 5.5, Opus 5.5, Fable 5.1; the message names it, `resolve_build_model` now covers the Orchestrator's role, and Terraform lets that runtime call the build models). The brief (in the message until stage 4 moves it into `agents/`) says to split the job into parts that run at the same time and to call `add_part` for each part the moment it is written; the extreme architect is told to investigate first and hand parallel specialist leads to Application, IT Infrastructure (AWS in Terraform), Data & AI and a security part, marking AWS parts `aws`. (2) **Parts start as they are written.** `add_part` stores the part under its job (`TICKET#<task>` / `PLAN#…`: name, goal, acceptance, department, the parts it waits for, the files it alone owns, trivial, risky, aws) and `advance` starts every part whose parts-before are done, each once (a conditional claim): one goal per department on the board, one worker per part hired straight into that department's tower, the part's ticket and its turn. No team ceiling (decision 288). When a part passes, its department's goal closes once it has nothing open, and the next parts start. `add_part` refuses an unknown department, an unknown earlier part, a name used twice and a file that already has an owner. (3) **The whole-app check.** `finish_plan` adds one last part after every other: a worker on the size's reviewer model (Opus 5.5, Fable 5.1 on extreme) that reads every part's report, makes the parts fit together and delivers the result; its report is the job's result. It is not reviewed again; it is the check. (4) **Workers per part:** Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5 for a part the planner marked trivial. A Haiku part that fails once (tests or review) is handed to a fresh Sonnet worker; any other part after two failures to Opus 5.5 (Fable 5.1 on extreme), every reason travelling (`fails_allowed`, `take_over`). (5) **Reviewers:** after the tests, the department's manager reviews each non-trivial part on Opus 5.5 (medium and large), every part on Fable 5.1 on extreme; a part marked `aws` gets security (least privilege, no public data, encryption, no secrets) and cost (nothing left running or oversized, tags) checks in its review brief. (6) **One worker grows into a team:** a small job's worker may call `needs_a_team` with what it found; the dispatcher (a code step, like `finish`) closes its part, marks the job medium and starts a planner with the worker's notes.
