(7) **One app, several workers:** parts of one app share one copy in the workspace bucket, `hires/wkr-shared-<task>/app/` (the `wkr-` prefix keeps the workspace role's session-tag rule unchanged). `load_app` gives a part the app's current version plus every file the other parts saved so far and remembers what it loaded; `submit_app` saves only that part's changed files there (no draft per part); the whole-app check loads the shared copy, fixes and submits the one draft that the owner approves. (8) The switch: `board_sizes` now defaults to every size; `[]` sends every job back through the chain, `["small"]` keeps stage 2. Unchanged: the same ticket records and events (City, Work, Projects, the live preview, the chat and Needs you read them as before), the policy gate, the security blocks, the ad spend limit. Tests: `test_board_planned.py` (a medium job planned on the board with Sonnet, parts starting while the planner still writes, Haiku for the trivial part, Score waiting for Cards, Opus reviews of the non-trivial parts only, the check on Opus after every part, goals and job closed, one report to VAIC, board timings; large on Opus and extreme on Fable with every part reviewed and security and cost checks for the AWS part; Haiku moving up to Sonnet after one failure; a single worker handing a bigger job to a planner; the plan's refusals; the switch; two workers sharing one app copy and saving only their own files), the fixtures re-recorded with a medium build on the board and the same build on the chain, `board-path.spec.ts` replaying both (City: the hires ride into the Application tower and the count is who is inside, the whole-app check's worker arriving after the others leave; Projects: steps, preview and the final reply), before/after screenshots that differ only in the turns and the builder's name, and every backend, web, root and Playwright check. Not checked live: planners on the deployed models splitting real jobs well, the shared app copy on S3 in prod, and real timings.

295. **Managers are Reviewers in the City, and work inside while they review (2026-11-12).** The owner agreed: with the project board's build design (decisions 289, 292) each department's manager becomes its reviewer, checking every part its department finishes, so the City shows it that way. **Name:** `roleOf(agent)` gives "<Department> Reviewer" for a manager, used on its card, in the department's facts, in the visit view's tag and label, and in the Activity and needs lines ("the Research reviewer"). The suit look stays. **Inside while reviewing:** `isReviewing(state, id)` is true while the manager's latest activity is "reviewing" (today's chain: its review tool) or a review of its department is open (a `review.started` with no result yet). The board path (decision 294) marks a part's review starting with a `build.stage` event (stage "review", edge "start", the part as key), which opens a review of that part's department. `review.result`, the board's `review.finished` or a `build.stage` review end close a review, and so does the part being finished, failed, parked, reopened or sent back from review to work. A result also clears the reviewer's "reviewing" activity. A review left open is let go after `REVIEW_STALE_MS` (15 minutes). While it is true, `placement` puts the manager inside its tower (`desk:<dept>:reviewer`), so it dashes in through the doors (a short dash, no taxi), lights one window and is counted in "Agents working now" (decision 284). Several reviews at once are still one reviewer. When they end it dashes back out to its spot by the door, and leaving now takes it off the count at once. The scene re-places people on review events and when a manager's activity starts or stops being "reviewing". An activity event without a time is stamped when it arrives. **Reload:** the scene places the reviewer directly from the snapshot, with no walk, and the snapshot handed to the scene now carries agents' activities. But the backend's City snapshot doesn't include agents' activities or open reviews yet, so after a real reload a reviewer mid-review stands at its door until its next activity event. That needs a backend change (adding `activities` to the snapshot), left to the board migration, which owns those events. The iPhone app's native screens are unchanged; the City picture inside it follows these rules. The board's own `review.started` / `review.finished` events (decision 297, with the department and reviewer named) are read as they come. The Activity line for a review names the part's worker in words ("The Research reviewer is checking the work of the analyst in Research"), never an id; the round is shown only from the second one. Checked: `web/test/reviewer.test.ts` (the name; inside while reviewing; two reviews at once still inside; out when both end; a stale review let go), `web/e2e/reviewer.spec.ts` (walks in and the count goes to 1, two parallel reviews still 1, out to its door and 0 after; a reload while reviewing places it inside at once with no walk), the Playwright suite, and vitest. Not checked: a physical iPhone. The iPhone City bundle was rebuilt.
296. **The agents' prompts know the project board; the old relay stays as the fallback for now, stage 4a of decision 289 (2026-11-12).** `agents/orchestrator.md` gains "Board planning" (plan parts with `add_part` as they are written, `files` with one owner each, `trivial` for Haiku, the extreme architect's investigation and specialist leads with `aws` parts, then `finish_plan`; no departments, assignments, sign-off or reports from the planner) and its chain steps are marked as the chain's; `agents/manager.md` gains "Board review" (one `review`, PASS or FAIL with the exact change, security and cost checks for AWS parts, nothing else in that turn); `agents/worker.md` gains "On the project board" (tests on report, edit in place, own files only, the shared app copy through `load_app` and `submit_app`, `needs_a_team`) and lists `needs_a_team`; `agents/vaic.md` says the city sizes every job, what the two hand-off answers mean, and that plain status questions are answered from the board before VAIC is called. Stage 4 of the owner's plan also removes the Orchestrator → Manager relay code, but the same plan keeps the old chain as the fallback behind the one switch until the new path has passed live for every size; no size has run live yet (the memory-game rerun is the first), so the removal (stage 4b: `assign_to_department`, `sign_off`, `report_to_vaic`, `plan_team`, `hire_worker`, `assign` and the chain prompts) waits for those passes. Tests: the prompt tool lists, and every backend check.
